> COPY-incorporated library text it is the copybook's file name (no directory) followed by the line in parentheses
> (`PB82LOC; ; pb82loc_proc.cpy(2)`), the same `file(line)` shape the compiler's own diagnostics print. The
> clause's NOTE disclaims stability across compilations; COBOL.NET's value is stable for one compiler version and
> one set of source files. Pinned by `2023/pb63_exception_location_procedure_field` (no COPY) and
> `2023/pb82_exception_location_source_line` (a COPY, a REPLACE and a continuation before each RAISE; a RAISE
> inside a procedure copybook).

> ⚖ **DETERMINATION — a level-66 RENAMES THRU alias whose data-name-2 / data-name-3 is (or lies under) a
> REDEFINES entry (§13.18.45.4 GR2)** (2026-08-18; kb/Work PB96). GR2 says the alias "defines an alphanumeric group
> item that includes all elementary items starting with data-name-2 … and concluding with data-name-3"; a
> redefinition adds no storage (§13.18.44), so the elementary items named can overlap. COBOL.NET's alias is the
> record's STORAGE WINDOW from the leftmost character position of data-name-2 to the rightmost character position
> of data-name-3 — a REDEFINES view inside the range contributes nothing beyond the storage it overlays, a
> data-name-2 / data-name-3 that is itself a redefinition maps to the area it overlays, and when data-name-3 is a
> SHORTER redefinition of an earlier item the alias ends at data-name-3's last character (a partial view of the
> underlying item), which is the offset arithmetic GnuCOBOL and IBM apply. Pinned by
> `2023/pb96_renames_span_over_redefines`.

## 5. Annex A.4 optional-element disposition (§4.2.7)

One row per A.4 optional module. **Claimed** = support is claimed (§4.2.7/A.4.1); **Partial** = the supported /
not-supported split is itemized (the §4.2.7 second-sentence requirement); **Not claimed** = the module's syntax
is not accepted (per A.4.1, optional-element syntax is accepted only when support is claimed — so a parse error
or a named error is the conforming posture). This section is the user-documentation face; §1's conformance
summary claims only what is Claimed/Partial here.

| A.4 § | Module | Disposition | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| A.4.2 | ACCEPT/DISPLAY screen handling | Not claimed | The SCREEN facility (§4 item 4). **REFUSED BY NAME, uniformly, since 2026-09-02 (kb/Work PB260)** — the row used to record a measured NON-uniform accept-and-warn posture, and the measurement that produced it is what refuted it. Annex A.4.1 admits an optional element's syntax "only when support for that language element is claimed by the implementor", so an unclaimed module is not accepted at all: all SEVEN source shapes now draw a named **Error** through the single funnel `src/Cobol.Net.Compiler/Binding/ScreenFacility.cs` — **COBOLNET1560** for the SCREEN SECTION header (§13.9), every screen description entry clause (§13.17 / §13.18.x, one message per clause KIND naming the clause and its ISO §), the SPECIAL-NAMES `CURSOR` / `CRT STATUS` clauses (§12.3.7) and the OPTIONS `INITIALIZE … SCREEN` target (§11.9.10.4 GR3 — the seventh shape, which parsed into a flag nothing read; GR1's `ALL` stays legal), and **COBOLNET1707** for ACCEPT format 3 (§14.9.1), DISPLAY format 2 (§14.9.11) and SET format 6 ATTRIBUTE (§14.9.39 — whose printed format has no `TO`) plus every EC-SCREEN exception-name in the six item-10 positions. Two codes, split at the DIVISION boundary, because a statement witness must declare a SCREEN SECTION to have a screen-name and would otherwise pass on the section's diagnostic. Declining the module costs the user none of its WORDS: the fifteen §8.10 screen words are reserved at no edition and the seven §8.9 ones only where §8.9 says (all but `COLUMN` from 2002), so all 22 are legal user words exactly where the standard leaves them so — and the population is DERIVED from `CobolScreen.g4` plus the two SPECIAL-NAMES screen clauses, so the next screen token added without a `cobol-words.json` `nameSlot` row fails a gate (kb/Work PB301). Witnesses: 41 negative-corpus programs `negative/a42-*` (each `.err` carrying the code AND the named construct) plus `ScreenFacilityConstructDriftTests`, which reads `CobolScreen.g4` and fails when a `screenClause` alternative has no row. Every rule the facility conditions carries this row's determination through `inventory-schema.json` `derived-verdicts.screen-handling-only` (178 rows — ten of them the Annex A.1 items 3, 4, 11, 27, 41, 45, 83, 91, 172 and 199, withdrawn by owner decision R42 through the selector's Annex A.1 arms) |
| A.4.3 | Commit and rollback | Not claimed | §4 item 2. **The two halves differ, and both are now named.** The COMMIT and ROLLBACK *statements* are accepted-inert and named — COBOLNET1579 at every site (`StatementBinder.BindCommitRollback`) — which is the COMPLETE behaviour §14.9.7.4 GR1 / §14.9.36.4 GR1 give them when no APPLY COMMIT clause is active. The I-O-CONTROL **APPLY COMMIT clause** (§12.4.6.3) is refused by name, **COBOLNET1709**, an Error at every edition it exists in (kb/Work PB261, landed; it was a generic syntax error before). Because no clause is ever accepted, no APPLY COMMIT clause is ever active — the state that makes the statement half's inertness exact. ⚖ **Seven syntax rules of CLAIMED facilities are thereby VACUOUSLY SATISFIED** (LOCK MODE, SAME ×2, OPEN, READ, REWRITE, UNLOCK): their antecedent is "a file subject to an APPLY COMMIT clause", which no compilable program can create. Owner decision 2026-09-02 (kb/Work PB371): they record **CONFORMS**, each on a negative witness that writes the rule's own forbidden construct beside the refused clause, so the verdict is forced back to a real adjudication the day this module is claimed. They are NOT documented non-support — §4.2.7 discharges non-support through documentation that declines commit and rollback, never OPEN or UNLOCK. Rules: `derived-verdicts.commit-and-rollback-only` (26 rows, one of them Annex A.1 item 28, withdrawn by owner decision R42; the seven above are deliberately EXCLUDED from it) |
| A.4.4 | Dynamic capacity tables | **Claimed** | All three items. Item 2, OCCURS format 4 (P12 §8.5.1.9); item 3, SET format 14 (§14.9.39.4 GR29–GR31); item 1, the four exception conditions, each with a LIVE raise site: EC-BOUND-OVERFLOW (implicit growth past the expected capacity, first crossing only — §8.5.1.9.6 GR1, `CobolDynTable.RefReceiving`), EC-BOUND-TABLE-LIMIT (a new capacity above the implementor maximum, capacity unchanged — GR30 first arm, `GrowTo`), EC-FLOW-SEARCH (a capacity SET during a SEARCH of the same table — GR31, `SetCapacity`) and **EC-BOUND-SET** (a SET whose NEW capacity exceeds the expected maximum capacity — GR30 second arm, `SetCapacity`; kb/Work PB460 — before it the condition had a catalog row and no raise site while this row read Claimed). ⚖ Two determinations ride EC-BOUND-SET: (1) the explicit SET raises EC-BOUND-SET, the name its own statement rule gives, and NOT also EC-BOUND-OVERFLOW, although §8.5.1.9.6 GR1's first sentence is worded broadly enough to reach an explicit change — GR1's exemption is written for implicit changes only, and GR30 names the explicit case; (2) EC-BOUND-SET is raised on EVERY such SET (GR30 has no first-crossing exemption), and it is raised BEFORE the capacity changes — the shape of its implicit twin — so a USE declarative sees the capacity the table had, a declarative that completes normally lets the change proceed, and a RESUME that leaves the statement abandons it. Witnesses: `conformance:2014/pb460_ec_bound_set`, `conformance:2014/ec_bound_overflow`, `conformance:2014/dyn_capacity_set`. |
| A.4.5 | DYNAMIC LENGTH elementary items | Partial | Alphanumeric live (§13.18.19, the 1561–1563 SR band); the NATIONAL dynamic-length FUNCTION LENGTH / BYTE-LENGTH runtime paths are staged loud (the P12 residue ledger) |
| A.4.6 | Extended letters | **Claimed** | National (UTF-16) repertoire (§2 row 38) |
| A.4.7 | File sharing and record locking | **Claimed** | SHARING/LOCK MODE/RETRY on every organization (P10 FILE-LOCK) |
| A.4.8 | FORMAT and SELECT WHEN file handling | Not claimed | **The decline is now NAMED, not accidental (2026-09-02).** Both clauses have a parse surface — `formatClause : FORMAT (BIT | CHARACTER | NUMERIC)+ DATA?` in `fileDescriptionClause` and `selectWhenClause : SELECT WHEN (OTHER | condition-name)` in the data description — precisely so the construct can be RECOGNISED and refused by name rather than tripped over: **`COBOLNET1705`**, raised at bind through `EditionContext.Declined`, quoting §4.2.7, Annex A.4.1 and both clause numbers. It is an **Error, not the COBOLNET1560 accept-inert band**, and unmoved by `--permissive` at every edition, because an inert FORMAT changes which bytes reach the medium (§13.18.24.4 GR1) and an inert SELECT WHEN selects the wrong record description entry (§13.18.51.4 GR1, with a §9.1.13.7 rule 5 I-O status 45 path) — a wrong answer, not a missing facility. `FORMAT` gained a lexer token and a `cobol-words.json` nameSlot row, so `01 FORMAT PIC X(4)` stays legal at `--std 85` (§8.9 reserves the word only from 2002) and draws `COBOLNET0901` by name at 2002+. No `constructs.json` row: a permanently declined element never compiles clean at an introducing edition. Witnesses `negative/a48-format-clause-declined`, `-choice-combination`, `-no-optional-word`, `negative/a48-select-when-condition-name`, `-other`. ⚠ §4 carries no item for this module, so THIS ROW is the whole of the §4.2.7 user documentation. Rules: `derived-verdicts.format-select-when-only` (37 rows, incl. the Annex A.1 obligations A.1-84/-85/-173, which A.1's own preamble makes not-required while the clauses are declined — they close on the WITNESS, never on a §7 determination) |
| A.4.9 | Locale support and related functions | **Claimed** | **All thirteen items** (kb/Work PB64 increments T1/T4/T5/T6 + PB101 T3/T7): the EC-LOCALE / EC-ORDER-NOT-SUPPORTED names; `LOCALE-COMPARE` / `-DATE` / `-TIME` / `-TIME-FROM-SECONDS`; the `LOCALE` phrase of `LOWER-CASE` / `UPPER-CASE` and the `LOCALE` keyword of `NUMVAL-C` / `TEST-NUMVAL-C` (item 12; `NUMVAL-C`'s rides the §4 editorial-omission determination); the OBJECT-COMPUTER `CHARACTER CLASSIFICATION` clause; PICTURE format 2 (item 8 — locale editing over the ONE LC_MONETARY model, EC-LOCALE-SIZE live); `SET LOCALE` formats 11/12; the SPECIAL-NAMES `LOCALE` clause and both `ALPHABET … IS LOCALE` forms; `STANDARD-COMPARE` + `ORDER TABLE` — "Implements collation behavior consistent with ISO/IEC 14651 through derived tables and CLDR/UCA data", §2 row 25 |
| A.4.10 | Object orientation optional items | Partial | The three OPTIONAL items only; the OO CORE is mandatory surface, claimed separately. **Item 2 — INTERFACE-ID multiple inheritance, the repetition of interface-name-2 (§11.6) — IS SUPPORTED**: `CobolOO.g4` parses `INHERITS FROM interfaceName+`, `OoClassTable` carries and resolves the full base list, `OoInterfaceSymbol.AllPrototypes()` walks the whole multi-base closure, and §11.6.3 SR2 (unknown base), SR3 (cycle) and SR6 (a base named twice) are enforced by COBOLNET0840. **Item 1 — CLASS-ID multiple inheritance (§11.3) — is NOT claimed**: a multi-base class INHERITS is refused COBOLNET0849 at pass-1, the deliberate asymmetry (a C# interface list is native; multiple class inheritance is not). **Item 3 — parametric polymorphism (§9.3.5.3) — is NOT claimed**: method overloading is refused COBOLNET0822, and the implemented method-resolution signature is the degenerate one, the method NAME alone. ⛔ This row read "Not claimed" of all three until 2026-09-02 while the compiler shipped item 2 — a conformance claim must describe the compiler (kb/Work PB285). §2 row 45 (parametric-polymorphism method resolution) claimed the A.3 item on the strength of that degenerate signature and contradicted item 3; it was corrected to **N/A** in the same change set, the disposition §2's own header defines for a property of an unsupported facility. ⚠ §4 carries no item for this module. Rules: `derived-verdicts.oo-optional-items-only` |
| A.4.11 | Report Writer | Partial | Implemented: the RW nucleus incl. PRESENT WHEN + VARYING (P10 RW-2002) and the SUPPRESS statement (§14.9.45 — inhibits the current instance's printing/page-advance/NEXT GROUP/LINE-COUNTER but not sum accumulation or the end-of-group reset; SR1/GR1 resolve the enclosing USE BEFORE REPORTING group at bind, COBOLNET1581 rejects a misplaced SUPPRESS). A report group description entry REPEATS ON EITHER AXIS (kb/Work PB565): the multiple LINE clause is bound as §13.18.35.4 GR9's own reduction to a LINE clause with one operand plus a simple OCCURS clause, so it and §13.18.38 format 3 are ONE mechanism, and the entry's axis decides whether integer-3 displaces vertically (GR10c/GR10d, GR12c/GR12d) or horizontally (GR10a/GR10b, GR12a/GR12b). Both COBOLNET0899 report-repetition stages are retired; the syntax rules now report through the family code that owns them (COBOLNET2021 for the OCCURS clause, COBOLNET2199 for the LINE clause). The NEXT GROUP clause is LIVE in all three forms, WITH RESET included (§13.18.37; kb/Work PB957 — the engine applies §13.18.37.4 GR1–GR6 after the group's last line, and COBOLNET2284 screens §13.18.37.3 SR1/SR3–SR7 and §13.15.3 SR6), and every report-writer relative operand — LINE, COLUMN and NEXT GROUP — admits both PLUS and + (§13.18.35.3 SR1 / §13.18.14.3 SR2 / §13.18.37.3 SR2; kb/Work PB951). The LINE clause's NEXT PAGE phrase is LIVE in both operand forms (§13.18.35; kb/Work PB1001 — §13.18.35.4 GR4a/GR5a, and COBOLNET2199 screens §13.18.35.3 SR3/SR5/SR7/SR8 — kb/Work PB1002). Staged LOUD (COBOLNET0899 band): the GLOBAL clause, cross-program CODE, a VARYING counter inside a FROM/BY expression, a FUNCTION reference inside PRESENT WHEN, GROUP INDICATE on a relative COLUMN operand, and several SOURCE/SUM legs. A report group item may be DISPLAY **or NATIONAL** (§13.18.60.3 SR7, COBOLNET2198 screens the clause and SR2's group/subordinate agreement; §13.18.60.4 GR1 inherits a group entry's usage — kb/Work PB541), and PAGE-COUNTER is a receiving operand wherever an integer data item may appear (§8.4.3.15.3 SR1; LINE-COUNTER is refused by SR3 as COBOLNET2197 — kb/Work PB429). NO grammar surface yet (tracked, the P13 grammar batch + ledger): COLUMN LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT, PAGE COLS, LAST CONTROL HEADING. The full itemization: `docs/COBOLNET_REPORT_WRITER_DESIGN.md` §5 |
| A.4.12 | RESUME statement | **Claimed** | §14.9.33 (the EC declarative RESUME) |
| A.4.13 | REWRITE FILE and WRITE FILE | Not claimed | ⛔ **This row previously read "No surface — a parse error today"; that was FALSE IN BOTH ARMS and the compiler was quietly SHIPPING the module** (measured 2026-09-02, kb/Work PB282): `WRITE FILE F FROM X` parsed, bound and RAN at every edition through a live `SequentialIoBinder` arm that wrote the whole record area — undeclared support, and not even the §14.9.51.4 GR8 implicit-record model — while `REWRITE FILE F FROM X` compiled clean and threw at RUN TIME with an empty name in its message. **Corrected in the same change set**: the live arm is deleted and both statements' FILE phrase is refused at bind by **`COBOLNET1706`** through the one `DeclinedFilePhrase` site, placed BEFORE the sequential/keyed reroute so neither verb can slip past it. Error, not accept-inert, and unmoved by `--permissive` at every edition: an inert WRITE FILE writes nothing where the program believes a file was written. `FILE` is reserved at every edition, so the alternative is reachable and the refusal is reached. Witnesses `negative/a413-write-file-format-1`, `-format-2`, `negative/a413-rewrite-file-declined`, plus the positive control `85/a413_declined_phrase_positive_control` proving the ordinary record forms still compile and run. ⚠ §4 carries no item for this module, so THIS ROW is the whole of the §4.2.7 user documentation. Rules: `derived-verdicts.rewrite-write-file-only` (12 rows — the FILE PHRASE only; the two statements are mandatory context per A.4.1 NOTE 1) |
| A.4.14 | VALIDATE | Not claimed | §4 item 3. **The posture splits and the row says so.** The VALIDATE *statement* is recognized and named — COBOLNET1580, a warning, at 2002/2014/2023 — and VALIDATE stays a user-defined word at COBOL-85. Every other item is REFUSED BY NAME: the nine data-division elements (`CLASS`, `DEFAULT`, `DESTINATION`, `INVALID`, `VAL-STATUS`/`VALIDATE-STATUS`, the format-4/format-5 `VALUE` entry, and `PRESENT WHEN` / `VARYING` outside a report group) draw **COBOLNET1708** through the one `DeclinedFacilityPass` seam over `Grammar/Core/CobolDeclined.g4`, and the EC-VALIDATE family draws **COBOLNET1710**. ⛔ Before that surface existed (kb/Work PB283, landed) each of these was a **generic parse error** — or, for DESTINATION / VAL-STATUS / VALIDATE-STATUS, the *misleading* COBOLNET0901 "is a reserved word" — and `>>TURN EC-VALIDATE CHECKING ON` compiled clean against a facility with zero setting sites. ⚠ The §8.9 reservation of VAL-STATUS / VALIDATE-STATUS and the context-sensitivity of RELATION / NONE survive the decline and are an EDITION-level obligation no inventory row covers; `negative/declined-validate-entry-name-still-0901` is what would catch a regression. Rules: `derived-verdicts.validate-only` (**81 rows** — §13.18.11 CLASS joined 2026-09-03 by owner decision, kb/Work PB375) |

## 6. Maintenance

Update this document in the same change set as any change to the supported surface (a new usage, a facility
newly implemented or newly documented as non-support, an I-O status determination). The declined-facility
sites — every one of them routed through `EditionContext.Declined`, whose descriptor severity chooses
warn-inert from refuse — are the code-side counterpart; keep the two in sync. This file is referenced by
`docs/VERSION_CHANGE_REFERENCE.md` (the edition-change checklist) and by `docs/DOC_INDEX.md`.

## 7. Annex A.1 — implementor-defined language element register (§4.2.5 / §4.2.16)

> **⛔ STATUS: INCOMPLETE — this is a known, registered v1.0 conformance gap, not an oversight.**
> Annex A.1 lists **222** implementor-defined language elements: **164 required · 30 optional · 27
> conditionally required · 1 whose class the standard states in prose rather than the usual sentence
> (item 176, the SET NaN payload)**, of which **199 carry the obligation "This item shall be documented in the
> implementor's user documentation"** and 23 explicitly do not. §4.2.5 requires the implementor to *specify*
> every element identified as required and to *document* every element identified as requiring documentation;
> D13 makes those 199 part of the definition of done.
> **MEASURED 2026-09-24 — this register discharges 125 of the 184 in scope, and 59 obligations remain**;
> 143 items carry a determination, eighteen of them (**35**, **61**, **69**, **90**, **92**, **104**, **118**,
> **119**, **123**, **125**, **138**, **144**, **169**, **170**, **186**, **194**, **201**, **220**) documented
> voluntarily where A.1 does not require it. ⚙ **The numbers and the item list are `python scripts/spec/audit_annex_a1.py`'s
> output, not a hand count — run it rather than trusting this sentence.** An enumeration of the discharged items
> used to live in this paragraph and it rotted twice: stale by three when item 50 landed, and by three again by
> 2026-09-02. The script prints the list (`--json`'s `filed`), the C# gate `AnnexA1RegisterDriftTests` runs it
> every build, and a list here could only ever be a second answer to a question one artifact already owns.
> The rows below are the register itself, and they are what the script reads. Notable among them are the
> **storage-representation items 205–209, 211 and 215** that V59 pinned (BINARY · the BINARY-CHAR family ·
> COMPUTATIONAL · INDEX · PACKED-DECIMAL: the byte width, the radix, the byte order and a worked example for
> each, since a COBOL developer reading this needs to know what lands on disk).
> ⛔ **FIFTEEN OF THE 199 CANNOT ARISE, so the denominator is 184** — items **84**,
> **85** (FORMAT clause), **173** (SELECT WHEN clause) and **86** (Format validation, which cites only PICTURE GR15
> and USAGE GR3 *during a VALIDATE statement*), and — by owner decision R42 (2026-09-24) — the eleven items of the
> declined screen-handling and commit/rollback modules: **3**, **4** (ACCEPT screen format), **11**
> (BACKGROUND-COLOR), **27** (color number), **41** (CRT status), **45** (cursor), **83** (FOREGROUND-COLOR), **91**
> (function keys), **172** (SECURE), **199** (terminal screen) and **28** (commit and rollback). Annex A.1's own preamble is the licence, not an inference from §4.2.7: *"Required: The
> element shall be provided by the implementor. **When the element is part of a feature that is optional or
> processor-dependent, the item is not required if the optional or processor-dependent feature is not
> implemented.**"* A.4.2, A.4.3, A.4.8 and A.4.14 are Not claimed (§5), so these obligations cannot be incurred;
> §7 carries no row for them, and they carry DOCUMENTED-NON-SUPPORT in the traceability inventory, each on the
> negative golden that diagnoses its declined construct. ⚠ The exclusion is **derived, not listed**:
> `audit_annex_a1.py` reads the same `derived-verdicts` selectors the inventory batch and the C# drift test read,
> so the next declined module is handled with no edit here — and, more to the point, this counter and the
> inventory can never give two answers about one item, which is what they did before 2026-09-02.
> ⚠ The MODULE-NAME row was filed under item **213** (which is USAGE NATIONAL) from 2026-08-05 until
> 2026-08-08 — the audit's number/element cross-check caught it; its true item is **135** (§15.65.4 r4).
> ⚠ **The two item-92 rows are VOLUNTARY**: A.1-92 is one of the 23 elements the standard says need *not* be
> documented. They are kept because the determination is load-bearing for users, but they discharge no
> obligation. (Their DISPLAY-conversion half was subsumed when A.1-56 was completed — kb/Work PB148.)
> Completing this register is **PHASE-14 Step 0** work — the four-edition traceability inventory enumerates
> every A.1 row and drives it to zero-GAP; do not attempt it piecemeal here. Items are added below as, and only
> as, the compiler's behaviour for them is actually settled — an undocumented determination and a
> wrongly-documented one are both non-conformance, and the second is worse.
> ⛔ **That last sentence earned itself on 2026-08-03**: the §15.3.3.2 fractional-seconds determination had been
> filed under **item 87**, which is *FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE (accuracy of returned time)* — a different, still
> undocumented obligation. It belongs to **item 202**. The number was inherited and never re-derived, exactly
> the failure mode CLAUDE.md rule 1 names. `audit_annex_a1.py --check` now re-derives every number here against
> the catalog parsed straight out of Annex A.1, so it cannot recur silently.
>
> ⚙ **THE ITEM KEY IS THE INVENTORY RULE-ID, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES A ROW ADDRESSABLE.** Each row's first cell is
> `DOC-A.1-<n>` — the `rule-id` of the P14 traceability-inventory row for A.1 item *n* — because that row's
> `code-location` is **computed** as `docs/CONFORMANCE.md#DOC-A.1-<n>` from its own rule-id
> (`tests/version-matrix/inventory-schema.json` → `kinds.DOC.anchor-template`). A determination therefore cannot
> be filed under a neighbouring item and still resolve: the anchor is derived, never typed. The bare number would
> not do — `#7` matched the digit 7 anywhere in this document, and five live inventory rows were closed on it.
> Prose elsewhere still cites items as *A.1 item N* / *A.1-N*, which is deliberate: the `DOC-A.1-<n>` token
> appears **only as a REGISTER ROW KEY** — here, or as the `DRV-`-prefixed key of the §8 derivation row that
> closes the same inventory row — and `audit_annex_a1.py` fails if it appears anywhere else in the file, so a
> passing mention can never masquerade as a filed determination.
>
> ⚙ **`Pinned by` names the SPEC-DERIVED test(s) that pin the determination, machine-readably** — in the
> inventory's own `test-ref` vocabulary (`conformance:<edition>/<case>`, `unit:<Class>.<Method>`), `—` when
> nothing pins it yet. `audit_annex_a1.py --check` asserts each cell AGREES with the inventory row's own
> spec-derived `test-ref`, so the determination and its evidence are one artifact rather than two that drift. A
> NIST golden or a `*_MatchesLegacy` differential is corroboration and never appears here: it cannot close a row
> (CLAUDE.md rule 1). ⚠ A `—` is an honest state, not a defect — the determination is written and correct, and
> the row stays a GAP until a golden pins it.
>
> ⚙ **A determination may cite the document that governs it, but it shall also STATE THE VALUE** — owner
> decision, `kb/Work/PB280` Q3, 2026-09-02. §4.2.16 permits an implementor to meet a documentation requirement
> "by reference to other documents", and A.1's own preamble likewise allows a row to "reference other
> documentation that fulfills this requirement"; this register takes the narrower path deliberately, because a
> by-reference row states no value, so **no test can pin it** and the gate can only check that a row exists —
> the same unfalsifiable shape Q2 refused. Where the answer is the host's (the .NET case-mapping table, the
> Unicode version, an OS limit), name the document *and its version* **and** write down the resulting value; the
> value is what the `Pinned by` golden asserts.
>
> ⚙ **An element the standard marks OPTIONAL that we do not provide is recorded here as `Not provided.` and
> carries DOCUMENTED-NON-SUPPORT in the traceability inventory** — owner decision, `kb/Work/PB280` Q1,
> 2026-09-02. A.1's preamble conditions the duty on provision ("Documentation required: **If the element is
> provided by the implementor**, the implementor's user documentation shall document the element…") and §4.2.7
> obliges us to identify the optional elements we *do* claim, so stating the non-provision IS the determination —
> which is why the row stays here rather than being withdrawn the way the four items above are. The verdict is
> applied by a predicate, not row by row: `inventory-schema.json` → `derived-verdicts.a1-optional-not-provided`
> selects on the requirement class plus this cell's opening words, so writing `Not provided.` into an optional
> item's row turns the drift gate red until the batch is re-run. Items **7**, **64**, **106**, **127**, **143**, **150**, **193** and **196** today. Item **206** LEFT the set on
> 2026-09-09, which is this paragraph's own promise running forwards: owner decision Q30 (kb/Work PB592) replaced its
> cell with a `Provided` determination, the selector stopped selecting it, and the row was re-verdicted CONFORMS by
> a batch.

| A.1 item | Element | Our determination | Pinned by |
|---|---|---|---|
| DOC-A.1-1 | **ACCEPT statement — conversion of data**, §14.9.1 GR1 (GR14 is the screen format — Annex A.4.2, not claimed), required + documented | The device delivers characters of the UTF-16 repertoire (the transfer size is item 5); each receiver shape converts them as follows (`AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitAcceptDevice`). **Alphanumeric, alphabetic, alphanumeric-edited, numeric-edited, national and national-edited** receivers: **no conversion** — the characters store as-is, one per character position, with no editing applied (a NATIONAL receiver's conversion is the identity on the UTF-16 substrate). A **reference-modified** receiver takes the characters into the slice the same way; a **group** receiver takes them positionally across its character positions. A **fixed-point numeric** receiver of ANY usage (DISPLAY, COMP, COMP-3, BINARY-\*, …) reads a window of its digit count plus one position for SIGN SEPARATE and **decodes it as that item's display image**: the digit characters, in order, form the value at the PICTURE's implied scale, every non-digit character contributes no digit, and the sign is read where the description puts it (a separate `+`/`-`; an over-punch per the program's §13.18.52.4 GR5 b) sign set; a leading `-` for the binary family); no exception is set. A **BOOLEAN** receiver takes boolean one for each `'1'` and boolean zero for every other character (`AcceptSource.DeviceBoolean`). A **floating-point** receiver (COMP-1/COMP-2/FLOAT-\*, kb/Work PB887 — this arm aborted the run unit until 2026-09-22) takes **exactly one record** and reads it as the **inverse of the DISPLAY conversion** (item 56's shortest-round-trip image): the §15.69 NUMVAL-F argument format — optional sign, digits with an optional period decimal point, an optional `E`±exponent, surrounding spaces ignored — correctly rounded to binary64 and narrowed to a binary32 receiver; a record that does not conform (an all-space record included) converts to **zero** and sets no exception condition (`AcceptSource.DeviceFloat`). | `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_FloatReceiver_ReadsTheDisplayImage`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_FloatReceiver_RoundTripsItsOwnDisplayImage`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_NumericReceiver_ConvertsAndComputes`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_BooleanReceiver_ConvertsToBooleanCharacters` |
| DOC-A.1-2 | **ACCEPT statement — device used when FROM is unspecified**, §14.9.1 GR5, required + documented | The implementor default ACCEPT device is the process **standard input** stream. The input-capable SPECIAL-NAMES device-names (§12.3.7 Format 4, `device-name-1 IS mnemonic-name-3`) are **CONSOLE** and **SYSIN**, both naming standard input; a mnemonic bound to an output-only device fails §14.9.1.3 SR2 (`COBOLNET0817`). The device-names and their capabilities are ONE table, `ImplementorNames` (item **189**). Implemented in `AcceptDisplayBinder.MnemonicDevice` + `AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitAcceptDevice`. | `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_ShortLine_StoresLeftAlignedSpaceFilled` |
| DOC-A.1-5 | **ACCEPT statement — size of data transfer**, §14.9.1 GR2, required + documented | **One 80-character card-image record** per transfer, for every input device (CONSOLE and SYSIN both name standard input, item 2), `AcceptSource.RecordSize`. A line of standard input shorter than 80 characters is padded with spaces to the record; a longer line is CONSECUTIVE records, each padded — never one oversized transfer. The receiving size GR3/GR4 compare against is the receiver's character positions: the PICTURE length for a character receiver, the slice length for a reference-modified one, the digit count plus a SIGN SEPARATE position for a fixed-point numeric one, the group's character positions for a group, the MAXIMUM size for a dynamic-length one (D-DL2), the ARGUMENT's length for an ANY LENGTH one (§13.18.2.4 GR1 b); kb/Work PB1013), and exactly one record for a floating-point one (item 1) — one reader, `ReceivingStore.CharacterPositions`. A larger receiver requests further records until filled (GR4a, left-aligned); a smaller one keeps the leftmost characters and ignores the rest of the record AND the line's remaining records (GR4b); end-of-input leaves the unfilled remainder as spaces; a zero-length receiver consumes one record and ignores all of it (GR4b's closing sentence). | `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_LongLine_SplitsAtRecordBoundary`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_WideReceiver_ReadsAdditionalRecords`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_AnyLengthReceiver_TransfersTheArgumentsSize`; `unit:AcceptSourceTests.ZeroLengthReceiver_ConsumesItsRecord` |
| DOC-A.1-18 | **CANCEL statement — result of canceling an active program when EC-PROGRAM-CANCEL-ACTIVE is not enabled**, §14.9.5 GR5, required + documented | The program is **not canceled and the run unit terminates** with the fatal `EC-PROGRAM-CANCEL-ACTIVE` raise (kb/Work PB154): `ProgramTable.CancelNode` throws before any state mutation regardless of the checking state — GR5's implementor-defined not-enabled arm is the SAME disposition as the enabled arm, so a program cannot observe a half-canceled active target either way. With checking enabled a USE declarative can take the condition and RESUME (pinned by `2023/pb154_cancel_active`). | `conformance:2023/pb154_cancel_active` |
| DOC-A.1-19 | **CANCEL statement — result of canceling a non-COBOL program**, §14.9.5, required + documented | **No effect** (kb/Work PB154). A name that does not resolve to a registered COBOL.NET unit is the GR7 no-op, and CANCEL never probes sibling modules (the probe is a CALL-side locate step; running it on CANCEL loaded assemblies and cached the miss). A foreign (non-COBOL) DLL on disk is therefore never touched by CANCEL. There is no CALL-CONVENTION directive (§7.3.9) — no non-COBOL program can be declared, so no richer effect is definable yet. ⚠ **This determination does not, and today cannot, answer §14.9.5.4 GR10's own question**, which governs a program the runtime system LOCATED per GR2: `ProgramTable.Cancel` resolves every target with `probe: false` and returns on a null node, so no locate step runs and the named module's language is never consulted — `CANCEL "Cobol.Net.Runtime"` and `CANCEL "L1NOSUCH"` take the byte-identical path. The branch is therefore UNREACHABLE from COBOL source, which is why the inventory row records CONFORMS with an empty `test-ref` and stays a GAP: a golden written against it would be invariant under the determination it claimed to measure (one was drafted in golden-lane round 2 and deleted for exactly that reason). Closing it needs either a `unit:` harness that deploys a companion non-COBOL module and drives `ProgramTable.Cancel` directly, or the unobservable-rule closure path kb/Work PB386 puts to the owner. | — |
| DOC-A.1-56 | **DISPLAY statement — conversion of data**, §14.9.11 GR1, required + documented | Each operand converts to its character image (kb/Work PB148 completed the register): a **PICTURE-bearing numeric** item renders its PICTURE-digit image (digits the description declares, the decimal point and sign per the description; a BinaryCapacity item's beyond-PICTURE value renders the PICTURE-digit image, the value modulo 10^digits (owner decision R13: follow GnuCOBOL; kb/Work PB825 retired against it), pinned by `comp5_display_beyond_picture`); a **signed item of a non-DISPLAY usage** (COMP/COMP-3/COMP-5/BINARY-*) has no zoned overpunch to render, so it prints a **leading `-` when negative and no sign character otherwise** — a VARIABLE-width form (digits, or `-`+digits), pinned by `pb148_display_forms`; a **PICTURE-less float** (COMP-1/COMP-2/FLOAT-*) renders the invariant-culture shortest-round-trip image (`CobolFloat.Display` — the same image a function result uses, item 92); **alphanumeric/edited/national/boolean** items transfer their character content verbatim (the UTF-16 repertoire is the device repertoire; a boolean prints its '0'/'1' characters). Class **object, pointer and index** operands (and the word NULL) are compile-time rejections (`COBOLNET1694` — §14.9.11.3 SR1 / §13.18.60.3 SR10), never converted. The variable-length-group format (A.1 item 57) is kb/Work PB164's open half. | `conformance:2002/pb148_display_forms`; `conformance:2023/comp5_display_beyond_picture` |
| DOC-A.1-58 | **DISPLAY statement — size of data transfer**, §14.9.11 GR2, required + documented | **Unbounded.** The standard display device (a byte stream) imposes no transfer size: every DISPLAY statement's operands are joined into ONE sending item and written in a single stream operation (GR4's device-accommodates arm is always taken; no operand splitting, no line folding). Implemented in `AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay`. | `conformance:2023/l1_display_transfer_size` |
| DOC-A.1-59 | **DISPLAY statement — standard display device**, §14.9.11 GR8, required + documented | When the UPON phrase is omitted the standard display device is the process **standard output** stream. The output-capable SPECIAL-NAMES device-names are **CONSOLE** and **SYSOUT** (→ standard output) and **SYSERR** (→ standard error); a mnemonic bound to an input-only device (e.g. SYSIN) fails §14.9.11.3 SR2 (`COBOLNET0817`). The device-names and their capabilities are ONE table, `ImplementorNames` (item **189**). Implemented in `AcceptDisplayBinder` (`BindDisplayUpon` / `MnemonicDevice`) + `AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay`. | `conformance:2023/l1_display_standard_device`; `conformance:negative/l1-display-upon-input-only-device` |
| DOC-A.1-62 | **Dynamic-length elementary items — maximum length**, §8.5.1.10.1 / §13.18.19.4 GR2, required + documented | **1,073,741,791 characters** (`0x3FFFFFDF`) for every dynamic-length elementary item, alphanumeric or national. §8.5.1.10.1 makes the maximum size "the smallest of" three candidates and this is the third of them, "the maximum permitted by the implementor"; §13.18.19.4 GR2 makes it the whole answer when no LIMIT phrase is written. The value is the CARRIER's own ceiling and nothing else: a dynamic-length item IS a native .NET `System.String`, whose maximum length is 0x3FFFFFDF characters — the same "the carrier's headroom IS the implementor maximum" rule a dynamic-capacity table's constant follows (`CobolDynTable<T>.MaxOccurrences`; item 60's own determination is not yet written — the table's runtime maximum is the open defect kb/Work PB1410). A LIMIT phrase BELOW it is the maximum size; a LIMIT phrase above it cannot raise the maximum and is reported (COBOLNET2027, a warning — §13.18.19.3 states no rule bounding integer-1 unless a dynamic-length-structure-name is written too, SR4, so the entry is legal). The second candidate, "the largest integer that can be stored in an item of the usage specified in the PREFIXED phrase", applies when the item names a dynamic-length-structure-name whose clause has a PREFIXED phrase (kb/Work PB829; determination D-DL3 in §3): SHORT PREFIXED 65535, SIGNED SHORT PREFIXED 32767, and the 32-bit fields, both above this maximum, change nothing. ONE producer — `CobolDynString.MaxSizeOf` — computes it, and `DataItem.DynMaxSize` carries it to every emitter, so no site can see "no maximum". ⚠ A request ABOVE the maximum takes §14.9.39.4 GR38's clamp with EC-STORAGE-NOT-AVAIL, and the maximum is a ceiling on the SIZE, not a promise the host can allocate it — GR38's third leg covers that and is implemented (§4 above). | `unit:CobolDynStringTests.MaxSizeOf_NoLimitPhrase_IsTheImplementorMaximum`; `conformance-test:DynamicLengthTests.ImplementorMaximum_IsTheNumberPublishedInConformanceSection7`; `conformance:2023/pb463_dyn_implementor_maximum` |
| DOC-A.1-64 | **ENTRY-CONVENTION clause (entry-convention-names, their meanings and the default when not specified)**, §11.9.7.4 GR2/GR3/GR4, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** COBOL.NET defines NO entry-convention-name-1: the one entry convention is COBOL, the convention §11.9.7.4 GR4 c makes the default when the clause is not specified and GR2 names when it is. Every runtime element - program, contained program, function, method - is activated through the one uniform entry ABI (`ICobolProgram.Call(CobolArg[] args, ManagedPointer? returning)`, reached through `ProgramTable.CallProgram`), with the §8.3.2.2 program-name mapping (case-insensitive, `ProgramTable.NameEquals`). `ENTRY-CONVENTION IS COBOL` is accepted where §11.9.7.3 SR1 permits the clause; `ENTRY-CONVENTION IS <any other name>` is **COBOLNET2385** (GR3 - a name this implementation gives no meaning is refused by name, never silently activated with the COBOL convention), and so is the clause in a contained program, a method, or a FACTORY / OBJECT paragraph (SR1) - kb/Work PB232. | `conformance:negative/pb232-entry-convention-name`; `conformance:2014/pb232_entry_convention_cobol` |
| DOC-A.1-158 | **Reference format — rightmost character position of the program-text area (margin R)**, §6.3, required + documented | **Margin R is immediately to the right of character position 72**, i.e. the fixed-form program-text area is columns **8–72**. Characters beyond it are not part of the program text and are ignored — they are not an error (§6.3.4; comment-text likewise runs only "up to margin R"). Note §6.3.1 makes this position *implementor-defined*: the standard does **not** mandate 72, and ISO 2023 has no "identification area" (that was a COBOL-85 card-image convention). Columns 1–6, the sequence number area, are **optional** and may hold any character (§6.3.2) — a blank sequence area is ordinary fixed-form source. Free-form reference format (§6.2) is not column-bounded and is unaffected. Our auto-detection between the two formats is an implementor extension beyond the standard, which specifies fixed-form as the default and `>>SOURCE FORMAT` as the selector; the detector's rules live in `ReferenceFormatProcessor.IsFixedForm`. | `conformance:2023/l1_margin_r_program_text_area` |
| DOC-A.1-48 | **Default endianness specifications (for standard floating-point usages)**, §11.9.8 / §11.9.9, required + documented | When no OPTIONS **FLOAT-BINARY** clause is specified, the implied endianness-phrase for the standard binary floating-point usages (FLOAT-BINARY-32/-64) is **HIGH-ORDER-LEFT — big-endian** (§11.9.8.3 SR1 makes this the implementor's choice; it matches the big-endian pin of every other pinned byte representation, items 205/207, so records interchange). A specified `FLOAT-BINARY DEFAULT IS HIGH-ORDER-RIGHT` is **honored**: the implied phrase makes each FLOAT-BINARY-32/-64 item's IEEE interchange image **little-endian** (§13.18.60.4 GR19b), carried per item on its emitted numeric profile — the implementor-defined float usages (COMP-1/COMP-2/FLOAT-SHORT/-LONG/-EXTENDED) are unaffected (GR19c scopes the clause to the standard usages; GR13/GR21 pin ours big-endian). A per-item endianness-phrase on the USAGE clause itself (§13.18.60.2) is **parsed and honored**, and it **wins** over the OPTIONS-implied phrase — not by preference but by §11.9.8.3 SR2/SR3's own wording, which implies the clause only "for … any data item described with a standard binary floating-point usage in which an endianness-phrase is not specified". So the documented default above is the FALLBACK of a two-level derivation: the item's own phrase, else the OPTIONS clause, else HIGH-ORDER-LEFT. The phrase is rejected on a usage the general format does not print it on (COBOLNET1716), an encoding-phrase outside FLOAT-DECIMAL-16/-34 is rejected (COBOLNET1717), and a repeated phrase is rejected (COBOLNET1718, §5.2.6.4). **FLOAT-DECIMAL** (§11.9.9): the standard decimal floating-point usages are rejected as processor-dependent non-support (COBOLNET1564, item 207's family), so no default encoding/endianness determination arises; the clause binds and is inert. | `conformance:2023/pb174_float_item_endianness`; `conformance:2023/pb174_float_item_endianness_default`; `conformance:negative/pb174-float-decimal-encoding-nonsupport` |
| DOC-A.1-57 | **DISPLAY statement — format for display of a variable-length group**, §14.9.11.4 GR7, required + documented | A variable-length group (§8.5.1.12 — a dynamic-length elementary item or dynamic-capacity table is subordinate to it) displays as **its members' images concatenated in declaration order, each dynamic member at its CURRENT extent**: a fixed member contributes exactly the character image it occupies in a record (the item 205–215 representations — zoned digits, radix-2, BCD, IEEE interchange bytes), a DYNAMIC LENGTH item its current content at its current length, a dynamic-capacity table every occurrence at its current capacity, and a nested **scalar** variable-length group the same format recursively. The geometry follows the §15.50.4 r7 `FUNCTION LENGTH` sum: `LENGTH(G)` **equals** the displayed width, with no exception — a NATIONAL member contributes the TWO BYTES it occupies (§13.18.60.4 GR8 leaves the size to the implementor; D-N1 pins two, UTF-16BE, high-order first), exactly as LENGTH and BYTE-LENGTH have always counted it. ⚠ This row used to record a "one sanctioned width divergence" — a national member displaying ONE character per position while LENGTH counted two bytes — and kb/Work PB327 dissolved it: the group image became the group's STORAGE, so a national leaf could ride a file record at all, and the same change made the displayed width and LENGTH agree. §13.18.29.4 GR2's own NOTE states the consequence ("Without the GROUP-USAGE NATIONAL clause, the content of such a group item would be treated as category alphanumeric, possibly leading to corruption or invalid handling of data") and names the remedy: a GROUP-USAGE NATIONAL group is category national and displays its national CHARACTERS, through the generated `AsNat()` face. **Excluded shapes, rejected loudly rather than shown at a wrong extent**: a runtime-length item *inside* a table element (fixed, ODO, or dynamic — the same named stage `IntrinsicBinder.VariableLengthGroupSum` documents; a struct-scoped composer also cannot reach an OCCURS DEPENDING counter declared outside the group), any member carrying or containing an OCCURS DEPENDING clause (its current count lives on the operand's access path, not the struct). The same two excluded shapes are the ONLY residue in the other statements that carry such a group whole, because both consume the same current-extent composer: the §14.8.2.2 / §14.8.3.2 activation-boundary crossing (kb/Work PB204) and — since kb/Work PB393 — the §14.9.25.4 GR9 MOVE, §14.9.20.4 GR8/GR10 INITIALIZE, and the CORRESPONDING verbs, all of which now carry a variable-length group in full. Comparison of such a group remains rejected — GR7 is a DISPLAY-statement determination; since kb/Work PB981 a WRITE / REWRITE / RELEASE of a variable-length RECORD sends this same composition (§3 determination D-FRA). (A USAGE INDEX leaf no longer excludes a group — the R40 pin, item 211.) | `conformance:2023/pb164_vlg_display` |
| DOC-A.1-205 | **USAGE BINARY clause — computer storage allocation, alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR4, required + documented | A BINARY item is a **two's-complement integer of the item's UNSCALED value, MOST SIGNIFICANT BYTE FIRST (big-endian)**, in a width pinned by the PICTURE's digit count: **1 byte** for 1–2 digits · **2** for 3–4 · **4** for 5–9 · **8** for 10–18 · **16** for 19–38. The implied decimal point occupies no storage (§13.18.40.4) and the sign is the two's-complement sign — there is no separate sign byte. **Alignment: none** — COBOL.NET performs no SYNCHRONIZED physical padding, so an item begins at the next byte and a group carries no implicit FILLER. The 16-byte tier exists because GR4's closing sentence requires storage "sufficient … to contain the maximum range of values implied by the associated decimal picture character-string" and a signed 19-digit picture (10^19−1) exceeds 2^63−1. Widths are sign-INDEPENDENT (GR12's precedent for the fixed-width usages). Worked example: `PIC 9(4) COMP VALUE 1234` occupies 2 bytes `04 D2`; `PIC S9(4) COMP VALUE -1234` occupies `FB 2E` (65536−1234). An UNSIGNED item holds the absolute value (§14.9.25.4 GR6d2b). `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` reports exactly this width, and it is the width the item occupies in a group image, a record, a SORT key and a REDEFINES view — one representation at every byte boundary. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_binary_storage` |
| DOC-A.1-206 | **USAGE BINARY-CHAR / -SHORT / -LONG / -DOUBLE — wider range than the minimum**, §13.18.60.4 GR12, optional | **Provided, the native range.** GR12's MINIMUM bands are asymmetric, and the asymmetry is the whole content of the rule: every SIGNED row of its table prints a STRICT `<` on both sides (`-2**(w-1) < n < 2**(w-1)`) while every UNSIGNED row prints `<=` on the left (`0 <= n < 2**w`) — re-rendered from the canonical PDF, printed page 507. The signed exclusion of `-2**(w-1)` is deliberate — it lets a sign-magnitude or ones'-complement machine conform — and §15.58.4's NOTE concedes it in terms: “BINARY-CHAR SIGNED \| -128 (assuming an 8-bit twos-complement representation)”. COBOL.NET provides the full NATIVE TWO'S-COMPLEMENT band of each usage's byte width: **BINARY-CHAR** SIGNED -128..+127, UNSIGNED 0..255; **BINARY-SHORT** SIGNED -32768..+32767, UNSIGNED 0..65535; **BINARY-LONG** SIGNED -2147483648..+2147483647, UNSIGNED 0..4294967295; **BINARY-DOUBLE** SIGNED -9223372036854775808..+9223372036854775807, UNSIGNED 0..18446744073709551615. Each SIGNED usage therefore expresses exactly ONE value beyond the GR12 minimum — `-2**(w-1)` — and the four UNSIGNED usages are exactly the minimum. A value outside the band raises the size-error condition (§14.7.5), never a silent widening; GR12's nesting sentence still holds at the wider end, LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC of each narrower signed usage being expressible in the next. The band is written down ONCE — the byte width per usage (1/2/4/8) in `PicInfo.StorageWidth` under the `BinaryCapacity` discipline (`PicInfo.Truncation`) — and read by the runtime store bound (`CobolNum.InBinaryRange` / `CobolNum.TryStoreU`) and by the compile-time HIGHEST/LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC fold (`IntrinsicBinder.BindAlgebraicFold`), which `AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests` holds in agreement. Owner decision Q30, 2026-09-09 (kb/Work PB592): this cell previously read “Not provided. Each usage holds exactly the GR12 minimum range”, which was false for all four SIGNED usages and, written as `±128`, was not readable as a range at all. | `conformance:2002/pb592_binary_usage_range_bounds` |
| DOC-A.1-207 | **USAGE BINARY-SHORT/-LONG/-DOUBLE and FLOAT-SHORT/-LONG/-EXTENDED — representation and length**, §13.18.60.4 GR13 + GR21, required + documented | BINARY-CHAR **1** byte, BINARY-SHORT **2**, BINARY-LONG **4**, BINARY-DOUBLE **8** — two's complement, big-endian, SIGNED and UNSIGNED the same width (GR21). FLOAT-SHORT is **IEEE 754 binary32 (4 bytes)**; FLOAT-LONG and FLOAT-EXTENDED are both **IEEE 754 binary64 (8 bytes)** — the standard's subset nesting (GR13) is satisfied since every binary64 value is expressible in binary64. **A floating-point item participates in group images, records and CALL/MOVE byte transfers as exactly those big-endian IEEE interchange bytes** (kb/Work PB164 wave 2 — bit reinterpretation, never a numeric conversion; the byte order matches USAGE BINARY so records interchange). **A REDEFINES view over one reads and writes those same bytes** — the item's window IS its storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1), so a windowed float DISPLAYs, CONVERTs and INITIALIZEs exactly as its natively-carried twin does: one representation, whichever description names it. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_fixed_width_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-208 | **USAGE COMPUTATIONAL clause — alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR6, required + documented | COMPUTATIONAL (and its COMP / COMP-4 synonyms) is **identical to USAGE BINARY** — radix 2, the item 205 width ladder and byte order, the same PICTURE-digit-count truncation discipline. **COMP-5 shares the representation but OWNS ITS FULL CONTAINER RANGE** (owner decision 2026-08-07, kb/Work R10): the value is bounded by the native two's-complement range of the byte width, never the picture's digit count. **It participates in group images, records, CALL/MOVE byte transfers and a REDEFINES view on exactly that representation** (kb/Work PB164 — a full-container value rides the image bit-identically; the earlier exclusion was predicate drift, not a documented determination). A REDEFINES view over a COMP/COMP-5 item reads and writes those same bytes: the storage association is the item's own storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1) and either data-name may reference it (GR2), so a windowed item behaves exactly as its natively-carried twin does — pinned byte-level by `stepd_mixed_usage_redefines` (an unsigned `9(4) COMP-5` window reads `FF FF`, and bytes written through the alphanumeric view read back as the value they encode). The in-memory CARRIER follows the container so the whole range is representable: a signed item rides `long` (≤ 8-byte container) or `Int128` (16-byte); an **unsigned 8-byte container rides `ulong`** ([0, 2^64) exceeds `long`) and an **unsigned 16-byte container rides `UInt128`** ([0, 2^128) exceeds `Int128`). `FUNCTION HIGHEST-ALGEBRAIC` / `LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC` of such an item fold exactly the container's ends (§15.43.4 r2 / §15.58.4 r2 — the §15.43.4 NOTE's `BINARY-CHAR UNSIGNED → +255` row is the standard's own container-range illustration), and the folded maximum stores back into the item losslessly. Pinned by the golden `highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned` and `AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests`. | `conformance:2023/stepd_mixed_usage_redefines`; `conformance:2023/highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned`; `unit:AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests.SixteenByteUnsigned_StoresFullRange_AsContainerBits` |
| DOC-A.1-211 | **USAGE INDEX clause — alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR10, required + documented | An index item is a **64-bit managed integer occurrence number** (8 bytes as reported by `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`), never a byte offset. **Its character image is that occurrence number as an 8-byte BIG-ENDIAN two's-complement binary** (owner decision R40, 2026-08-30) — the same byte order as every other pinned representation (items 205/207/208), so records interchange — and an INDEX-leaf group therefore **participates in group images, records, CALL/MOVE byte transfers and DISPLAY** on exactly those bytes. **Alignment: none** (the item 205 rule). §13.18.60.3 SR10's reference restriction is untouched: an index item may be referenced explicitly only in a SEARCH or SET statement, a relation condition, an intrinsic function argument, an inline method invocation argument, or the USING phrase of a procedure division header / CALL / INVOKE. **A REDEFINES view over one reads and writes those same 8 bytes** — the item's window IS its storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1), so a windowed INDEX item behaves exactly as its natively-carried twin does. Naming one as a SORT key remains outside SR10's reference set. | `conformance:2023/pb164_index_group_image` |
| DOC-A.1-216 | **USAGE POINTER clause — alignment, size, representation, and range of values**, §13.18.60.4 GR23, required + documented | A data-pointer item is **one managed `ManagedPointer` reference — 8 bytes as reported by `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`, naturally aligned on the 8-byte boundary the .NET runtime gives a reference, with no PICTURE; its VALUE is the reference, and its 8 STORAGE positions hold the image `PointerImage` defines**. ⛔ **STORAGE IMAGE (kb/Work PB970 arm 2; CLAUDE.md rule 1's ISO → GnuCOBOL → IBM/Micro Focus precedence):** GnuCOBOL stores a pointer as the machine address in the byte order of the platform's native binary integers and NULL as the zero address (IBM's and Micro Focus's NULL too), so the image read as an 8-byte binary integer IS the address. This implementation has no machine address, so its image is an ADDRESS TOKEN of the same shape, in this implementation's binary byte order — **big-endian** (A.1 items 205 and 207): NULL of every category = eight X"00"; a data-pointer = its storage area's base + its displacement, the k-th area imaged receiving base k × 2^32 (so `SET P UP BY n` moves the image by n and two areas never meet while a displacement stays within ±2^31); a program-pointer / function-pointer = a base from the same allocator per (category, case-folded externalized name). The token is stable for the run unit and one-way (never decoded back into a reference — no conforming pairing needs it). ONE codec (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Control/PointerImage.cs`) defines the image; its consumer is the CALL boundary's BY CONTENT record into a same-length non-pointer formal (§14.8.2.3.3 rule 1; §14.2.3 GR9 "That argument is moved to this allocated record without conversion" — `CobolArgAdapt.Text`/`Num` via `WithPointerContent`); a formal of any other length stays EC-PROGRAM-ARG-MISMATCH. ⚠ A pointer MEMBER of a shared storage area (a BASED, EXTERNAL or ADDRESS-OF cell) keeps its value in the area's managed slot, and a byte view of its positions still reads kb/Work PB231's reserved fill, not this image (pinned by `conformance:2002/pb231_based_pointer_leaf`) — an open residue reported for the registrar, whose repair routes the ALLOCATE fill, the group-image composer and the slot store through this same codec. BY REFERENCE (§14.8.2.3.2) and a rule-2 activation (§14.8.2.3.3 rule 2d → §14.9.25.3 SR1) still refuse the pairing, at run time and at compile time respectively. Its VALUE is either the predefined address NULL or a (storage area, character-position displacement) pair over the one `StorageCell` shape (`CobolPtr`; COBOLNET_DATA_MODEL_DESIGN). **The RANGE of values is the signed 64-bit interval of displacements, −9 223 372 036 854 775 808 through +9 223 372 036 854 775 807** — the carrier's own range, `CobolPtr.MinAddress` / `CobolPtr.MaxAddress`, and the range §14.9.39.4 GR20 means by "the range of values allowed by the implementor for a data-pointer data item". A SET pointer UP/DOWN BY whose new address leaves that interval sets **EC-RANGE-PTR** and leaves identifier-9 unchanged (GR20), and the displacement is formed in `Int128` so the boundary is a value the guard can see rather than a wrap it cannot (kb/Work PB465 — `SET P UP BY 18446744073709551618` used to move the pointer by 2). ⛔ **An address inside the range but outside the addressed STORAGE is not out of range**: it is a legal pointer VALUE, and §13.18.5.4 GR4 raises EC-BOUND-PTR when it is DEREFERENCED, not when it is formed — a storage-bounds test at SET time would reject conforming programs. That deferral is why this range is the carrier's and not the cell's. ⛔ **GR20's range is not GR19's rule**: GR19 asks only whether arithmetic-expression-3 "evaluates to an integer", so an integral amount of 1.0E19 does NOT satisfy it and must not set EC-SIZE-ADDRESS. | `conformance:2002/pb465_set_pointer_range_ec`; `conformance:negative/pb465-set-pointer-up-by-at-85`; `unit:CobolPtrTests.UpByAmountReal_AnIntegralAmountPastTheCarrier_IsGr20_NotGr19`; `conformance:2002/pb970_pointer_by_content_storage_image`; `conformance:negative/pb970-pointer-by-content-prototype-alnum-formal`; `unit:PointerImageTests.DataPointer_IsAreaBasePlusDisplacement_BigEndian`; `unit:PointerImageTests.Null_OfEveryCategory_IsTheZeroAddress` |
| DOC-A.1-214 | **USAGE OBJECT REFERENCE clause — amount of storage allocated**, §13.18.60.4 GR22 a), required + documented | **One .NET managed reference — 8 bytes on the 64-bit runtime this compiler targets, and the SAME amount for every one of the §13.18.60.2 general format's eight writable shapes.** GR22 a) expressly permits an implementation to differ per shape (“not necessarily the same for every object reference”) and COBOL.NET does not take that latitude: the descriptor (kind · name · FACTORY · ONLY) is a COMPILE-TIME fact that selects the emitted C# reference type — `CobolObject?`, the class's type, its `__FACTORY` sibling, or the interface — and every one of those is a single managed reference at run time, so no shape carries a wider or narrower carrier than another. **Alignment: none** (the item 205 rule), and the item contributes **no character positions to any group image**: §13.18.60.3 SR14 admits USAGE OBJECT REFERENCE only “for an elementary data item at level 1 or an elementary data item subordinate to a type declaration that includes the STRONG phrase”, and SR15 forbids it in the file section, so an object reference never sits inside a record whose bytes are written or read. The amount is OBSERVABLE, which is what makes this a determination rather than a claim: §15.14.3 argument rule 1 admits “a data item of any class or category”, and §15.14.4 rule 1 returns “the length of argument-1 in number of bytes” — the golden below measures all five declarable shapes and gets one number. | `conformance:2002/pb496_object_reference_storage` |
| DOC-A.1-135 | **MODULE-NAME — the form of a module name, and the composition of the STACK list**, §15.65.4 r4 + r9, required + documented | **Names.** A program element is reported by its PROGRAM-ID / FUNCTION-ID — the DECLARED program-name-1 / user-function-name-1, never the `AS literal-1` externalized name, even when the paragraph writes one (r4 offers the AS form as one of four and this determination does not take it; kb/Work PB303, `2023/pb303_as_module_name_and_invoke`); a METHOD by its METHOD-ID; a method's CURRENT (r7) is its class, the outermost element of its compilation unit. r4 permits any of these forms and the method-id form is chosen because it is the more informative. **STACK (r9) lists RUNTIME ELEMENTS, not compilation units** — entry 1 is CURRENT, every entry after it is what ACTIVATING would return within the previous one, the penultimate is TOP-LEVEL and the last is a single space, exactly as r9 composes it. ⚠ **Consequence, deliberate:** a CONTAINED (nested) program yields its outermost name TWICE (`MAIN;MAIN; `), because r9's first entry is outermost-granularity (r7) while the chain is element-granularity (r5), so for a nested program they coincide. The alternative — collapsing consecutive frames of one compilation unit, which §15.65.1's looser "a list of all the module names" wording would allow — was REJECTED (owner decision 2026-08-05) because it cannot represent RECURSION: three nested activations of one RECURSIVE program collapsed to a single entry while ACTIVATING in the same frame named the program, so STACK and ACTIVATING contradicted each other. A visible repeat can be read; missing frames cannot. | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-134 | **MODULE-NAME — what is returned if the indicated module is not COBOL**, §15.65.4 r3, required + documented | **A single space.** The only non-COBOL runtime elements COBOL.NET can designate are the operating environment itself and a .NET host that drives a COBOL element without entering through `ProgramTable.RunMain`: with no COBOL element running every keyword returns one space; TOP-LEVEL (r10) returns one space when frame 0 was not pushed as the main (the host, not the environment, activated it — `ModuleStack.Name` case 4, kb/Work PB63 / RV-15.65.4-10); ACTIVATING and STACK's final entry are one space for the environment (r5 / r9). | `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.TopLevel_WithoutAMainFrame_IsTheDocumentedNonCobolValue` |
| DOC-A.1-136 | **MODULE-NAME — how it is determined whether the program is a main program**, §15.65.4 r5, required + documented | **The main program is the element the run unit was started with** — the program `ProgramTable.RunMain` activates (the CLI's `--run` entry, a host's `RunMain` call): it is pushed as the run unit's `IsMain` frame; nothing else ever is. A program CALLed, INVOKEd or referenced as a function is never main, whatever its PROGRAM-ID; a RECURSIVE re-activation of the main is a CALLed frame. So ACTIVATING in the main is a single space (r5) and TOP-LEVEL is its name (r10). | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths`; `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.ActivatingInTheMain_IsASingleSpace_TheOneR1Exception` |
| DOC-A.1-137 | **MODULE-NAME — the ACTIVATING value when the activation was in a nested program**, §15.65.4 r6, required + documented | **The nested (contained) program's OWN name**, i.e. the runtime element that executed the CALL / INVOKE / function reference — never its outermost containing program. r6 grants either; the element form is chosen because r9 defines every STACK entry after the first as "what ACTIVATING would return within the previous module", so ACTIVATING and the STACK chain agree by construction (`ModuleStack.Name` builds STACK from the same frame field): a separately-compiled program CALLed from contained `INR` inside `TOP` reports `ACTIVATING = INR` and `STACK = EXT;INR;TOP; ` (pinned by `2023/pb63_module_name_lengths`). | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-140 | **POP directive — the warning mechanism for an unsuccessful POP directive**, §7.3.20.4 GR2, required + documented | **A compile-time WARNING, COBOLNET2297, on the POP line; the compilation continues and nothing is restored.** A `>>POP directive-name` is unsuccessful when no state of that directive was saved by a PUSH in the compilation group, or every saved state was already restored by an earlier POP (GR2's two antecedents). A `>>PUSH ALL` saves every directive §7.3.22.4 GR2 names — including those whose state this compiler does not hold (LISTING, DISPLAY, CALL-CONVENTION, PROPAGATE) — so a later `>>POP LISTING` after a `>>PUSH ALL` is SUCCESSFUL. ⚠ GR2 speaks of directive-name only, so a `>>POP ALL` with nothing stored restores nothing and draws no warning. The warning is issued once, by the post-COPY stage that sees every PUSH/POP of the final text (`DirectiveSiteProcessor`); a PUSH/POP in an omitted `>>IF` branch is not processed and cannot make a later POP successful (kb/Work PB941). | `unit:DirectiveStateStackTests.ANamedPopWithNothingStored_Warns_AndPopAllDoesNot` |
| DOC-A.1-133 | **MODULE-NAME — the length of the returned value item and whether it may have trailing spaces**, §15.65.4 r1/r2, conditionally required (documented voluntarily) | **A dynamic-length alphanumeric item with no trailing spaces**, r1's stated exception included (a single space for ACTIVATING in a main program, and STACK's final single-space entry — content, not padding). The name is delivered as its exact string; there is no fixed width between the module stack and the expression, so §15.65.4 r2's "does not fit" antecedent (EC-BOUND-FUNC-RET-VALUE) is structurally unreachable — pinned by `ModuleStackInvariantTests` (kb/Work PB63 / RV-15.65.4-2), which also fixes the r10 host-boundary arm above. | `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.NoTrailingSpaces_AndNoTruncation_AtEveryDepth` |
| DOC-A.1-209 | **USAGE DISPLAY — size and representation of characters**, §13.18.60.4 GR7, required + documented | **One byte per character position, uniform at every length.** GR7 states three obligations and this determination answers the third: the item “is aligned on a character boundary” (every byte is one, so a DISPLAY item never causes alignment filler — `X(1) X(3) X(1)` occupies 5); characters are “of uniform size” (`X(1)`/`X(5)`/`X(9)` occupy 1/5/9 — the same one byte per position at every extent, so no variable-width external form is used); and that size is “equal to or less than the size of characters in the computer’s national character set” — the national determination is **2** bytes per position (row 213, UTF-16 code units), and 1 ≤ 2, which is what makes the DISPLAY size a CONSTRAINED choice rather than a free one. `FUNCTION LENGTH` (character positions, §15.50.4 r3) and `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` (bytes, §15.14.1) therefore agree on every alphanumeric item. A **numeric** item with no USAGE clause is USAGE DISPLAY by implication (§13.18.60.3 SR13 b) at one byte per DIGIT position: `9(5)` and `S9(5)` are both 5 — §13.18.52.4 GR5 a associates an unseparate operational sign with an EXISTING digit position — and `S9(5) SIGN IS TRAILING SEPARATE` is 6, GR6 a making the sign its own character position that “is not a digit position”. ⚠ A.1 item 209 permits an implementor option to vary the size or representation per compilation unit; **COBOL.NET provides none** — the width law is `PicInfo.ByteWidth` alone, so one program cannot see two answers. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_display_character_size` |
| DOC-A.1-215 | **USAGE PACKED-DECIMAL clause — computer storage allocation, alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR11, required + documented | A PACKED-DECIMAL item is **binary-coded decimal, two digits per byte, most significant first**, with a **trailing sign nibble** in the low half of the last byte: `C` positive, `D` negative, `F` for an item with no operational sign (the convention IBM, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL all write, so a data file interchanges). The digit run is padded on the left with a zero nibble when needed, giving **`digits / 2 + 1` bytes**; the implied decimal point occupies no nibble. Under the COBOL-2023 **WITH NO SIGN** phrase the sign nibble is not reserved at all — every nibble is a digit and the width is **`ceil(digits / 2)`**. ⚠ The two forms can occupy the SAME number of bytes: 3 digits is 2 bytes either way, laid out `12 3C` signed and `01 23` unsigned-no-sign. **Alignment: none** (no SYNCHRONIZED padding). Worked example: `PIC 9(4) COMP-3 VALUE 1234` occupies 3 bytes `01 23 4F`; `PIC S9(4) COMP-3 VALUE -1234` occupies `01 23 4D`. Decoding accepts the universal readings — `B` and `D` negative, everything else positive — so a file written by another COBOL system reads correctly. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_packed_decimal_storage` |
| DOC-A.1-82 | **Floating-point numeric literals — the maximum and minimum permitted value of the exponent**, §8.3.3.3.3 r3, required + documented | The FORM is the standard's (SR2/SR3: a 1–36-digit significand with a decimal point, an exponent of at most four digits — COBOLNET1661 otherwise, kb/Work PB99). **The permitted VALUE is the decimal128 range — about 1E-6176 to 9.99E+6144 — in EVERY position and EVERY arithmetic mode** (owner decision D-B, 2026-08-30; kb/Work PB156 + PB195). The literal IS its exact §8.3.3.3.3 rule-5 value wherever it appears (significand × 10^exponent), carried on the SDIDI lane, so the implementor-defined range r3 asks for is that carrier's; `1.0E+400` evaluates, `1.0E+9999` and `1.0E-9999` are COBOLNET1661. ⛔ This REPLACED a per-position split in which an arithmetic expression, a relation or a MOVE source screened the literal against IEEE binary64 (about 4.9E-324 ≤ \|v\| ≤ 1.8E+308): §14.9.2.4 GR4 and its §14.9.44.4 GR4 twin exclude only operands "described with usage" binary-*/float-*, never a literal, and §14.7.7 rule 2 names a floating-point literal as its OWN bullet where it means to include one — so `ADD 1.0E+0` to a PIC 9(20) holding 12345678901234567890 owed …891 and a binary64 operand delivered …168, and `IF 1.0E+400 > X` was refused under native arithmetic while the identical program compiled under ARITHMETIC IS STANDARD-DECIMAL. A literal's notation now never changes its VALUE. ⚠ It does still select the intermediate LANE under native arithmetic, and that is a published §8.8.1.3 determination, not an invariant: a float literal operand routes a native statement onto the decimal128 lane (34 significant digits) where a fixed-point literal keeps the Int128 one (~38), so `A * B + 0.0E+0` and `A * B + 0.0` differ for 18-digit A and B, and INTERMEDIATE ROUNDING (§11.9.11) reaches the first and not the second — pinned by `pb156_float_literal_exact_native`'s LANE/LANEF legs (kb/Work PB274). Separately, the exact-integer `**` arm is keyed on the exponent's VALUE and not its spelling, so `10 ** 30`, `10 ** 30.0` and `10 ** 3.0E+1` are one expression (kb/Work PB272). **The VALUE-clause range is a different rule and keeps the carrier's bound:** §13.18.63.3 SR2 wants "permissible values within the range indicated by the PICTURE clause or the USAGE clause" (verbatim, cite.py-verified) and a FLOAT-* subject has no PICTURE, so a VALUE on FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-BINARY-32 shall lie in binary32's range and on FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-BINARY-64 / FLOAT-EXTENDED in binary64's, while a VALUE on a fixed-point numeric item or a floating-point numeric-edited item keeps the literal's EXACT value and its range is the receiver's PICTURE (`PIC 9E+9999 VALUE 1.0E+9999` seeds `1E+9999`; `PIC 9(5)V99 VALUE 1.5E+3` seeds 1500.00). **Residual, stated honestly:** SR2 admits a 36-digit significand and the SDIDI carrier holds 34, so the 35th and 36th significand digits of such a literal round at `CobolDec.FromParsed` under the INTERMEDIATE ROUNDING mode; the form is accepted (SR2 is satisfied) and the two trailing digits are not preserved. Pinned by `2023/pb99_floating_literal_extremes`, `pb99_floating_literal_operand_exact`, `pb99_floating_literal_standard_decimal`, `pb156_float_literal_exact_native`; negatives `pb99-floating-literal-form` / `-range`. | `conformance:2023/pb99_floating_literal_extremes`; `conformance:2023/pb156_float_literal_exact_native`; `conformance:negative/pb99-floating-literal-range` |
| DOC-A.1-87 | **FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE function — accuracy of the returned time portion**, §15.38.4 r2 (Annex A.1 lists it under "Returned value rule 1"; the obligation's text is r2 — do not "correct" it the other way), required + documented | **The run unit's clock TICK — 100 ns, i.e. 7 significant fraction digits**, exactly SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT's precision (item 171) and the same injectable `RunUnit.Clock` seam (`COBOLNET_CLOCK`-deterministic; the code once read `DateTimeOffset.Now` directly and no longer does). The value is carried at 9 fraction digits as `ticks × 100` — integer arithmetic, no binary64 conversion on the way (kb/Work PB65, 2026-08-18) — so a format's `s` fraction field renders the first 7 digits from the clock and ZEROS beyond them, up to the §15.3.3.2 maximum of 18; nothing is truncated or diagnosed. The date portion is the clock's local calendar date; the UTC-offset portion (`+hh:mm`) is the clock's offset. ⚠ **This determination is CONFORMS-but-UNTESTED, and says so** (2026-09-02): the only method in the tests tree that reaches `FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE` through the clock seam, `NowFunctions_OnePinnedClock_OneInstant`, renders at `ss.ss` — **two** fraction digits (`.81` of a pinned `.8124791`) — while this row claims the clock tick, seven significant digits and then zeros. **A test that does not verify the determination cannot close its row**, so `Pinned by` is deliberately EMPTY, the inventory row carries no `test-ref`, and item 87 remains a GAP until a golden renders a long fraction field from a pinned clock. (Until 2026-09-02 this row's inventory evidence was `SecondsPastMidnight_PinnedClock_ExactTicks`, which is **item 171's** test and never calls this function — the A11 misfiling one layer down, in the evidence rather than in the determination; re-referencing it onto the function's own test fixed the misfiling but did not make the row tested.) | `conformance:2023/l1_formatted_current_date_accuracy`; `unit:CobolDateAccuracyTests.FormattedCurrentDate_PinnedClock_ClockTickThenZeros` |
| DOC-A.1-144 | **RANDOM function — seed value when no argument on first reference**, §15.75.3 r4, required (the seed VALUE need not be documented — this row is VOLUNTARY, the item-92 precedent, kept because the determination is load-bearing for users) | **Per-process OS entropy — sequences are deliberately NOT reproducible across runs without an explicit seed argument.** The unseeded generator is .NET's parameterless `Random` (xoshiro256** seeded from OS entropy); there is no fixed seed value, which is itself the determination §15.75.3 r4 requires. ⚖ Decided 2026-08-09 under the owner's standing latitude rule (follow GnuCOBOL where the standard leaves latitude): **surveyed, not assumed** — GnuCOBOL 3.x `cob_intr_random` seeds its first unseeded reference from `get_seconds_past_midnight() * (module-pointer bits)` (libcob/intrinsic.c, read 2026-08-09), i.e. per-process entropy, the SAME choice — and IBM documents the unseeded seed as unpredictable, so the vendors do not even split. A program needing a reproducible sequence writes `FUNCTION RANDOM(seed)` on first reference, which fully determines the sequence per r3/r5. | `conformance:2023/l1_random_seed_determination` |
| DOC-A.1-145 | **RANDOM function — the subset of the domain of argument-1 that yields distinct sequences**, §15.75.4 r3 ("shall include the values from 0 through at least 32767"), required + documented | **Every seed 0 through 2,147,483,647 selects its own generator state; the seeds 0 through 65,535 are MEASURED pairwise distinct** — `RandomSeedSubsetTests` compares the first three draws of every seed in that range and finds no two alike, which discharges the required floor 0..32,767 with a margin (only what is measured is claimed: distinctness above 65,535 is the generator's design, `System.Random(int)` seeding injectively on the value, not a measurement). **The DOMAIN is every non-negative integer — the seed is a TOTAL argument** (kb/Work PB636): §15.75.3 r2 constrains the SIGN alone and r3 makes this subset a FLOOR, not a domain, so a seed of any magnitude is legal and §15.3 has no incorrect value to raise on. A seed at or above 2³¹ ALIASES to `seed AND 0x7FFFFFFF` (2,147,483,648 → 0, 2,147,483,649 → 1 — pinned), and that same low-31-bit reduction is what a 19-digit or 31-digit seed takes; a negative seed is EC-ARGUMENT-FUNCTION (§15.75.3 r2, item 144's sibling row). The generator is `System.Random(int)` (the .NET Knuth subtractive sequence, `CobolIntrinsics.Random(Int128 seed)` — the Int128 carrier IS the totality claim the renderer's intake pairs against); the same seed reproduces the same sequence within and across processes on a given .NET runtime (§15.75.4 r2). | `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.Seeds_0_Through_65535_YieldPairwiseDistinctSequences`; `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.SeedsAtOrAbove2Pow31_AliasToTheirMaskedValue`; `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.SeedsPastLong_AnswerInRange_AndRaiseNothingUnderChecking`; `conformance:2002/pb636_random_total_seed` |
| DOC-A.1-151 | **RECORD DELIMITER clause — the method for determining the length of a variable-length record when the clause is NOT specified**, §12.4.5.11.4 GR5, required + documented | **Two methods, chosen by the file’s ORGANIZATION — and this determination governs EVERY variable-length file COBOL.NET writes, including one that DOES write a RECORD DELIMITER clause, because that clause is declined whole (§2 row 26, COBOLNET1778).** **(a) Record sequential, relative and indexed:** each record is framed on the medium by a **4-byte little-endian binary length prefix** giving the record’s byte length, immediately followed by that many payload bytes; the prefix value `0xFFFFFFFF` is the relative store’s empty-slot tag and carries no payload. ONE implementation serves every organization — `RecordFraming` (store-level `WriteStore`/`ReadStore`, stream-level `WritePrefix`/`PrefixLength`), streamed by `SequentialConnector` — so a file written by one organization’s connector is readable by another’s. **(b) LINE SEQUENTIAL:** no prefix is written; the length is the number of characters between line delimiters, which is §9.1.7.2’s own rule (“In line sequential files the length of each record is determined by the number of characters between the preceding line delimiter and the following line delimiter or the end of file if no line delimiter is present”), so a line-sequential file stays plain text. **The licence to add a header at all is §9.1.7.2**, not §9.1.13.6 — “In record sequential files the length of each record is determined by any information the implementor may add to the record on the physical storage medium (such as record length headers)”; §9.1.13.6’s “externally-defined boundaries” is the I-O status 34 rule about writing past a FILE’s end and answers a different question (the miscitation it replaced lived in `RecordFraming`’s own header until 2026-09-05, kb/Work PB292). **§12.4.5.11.4 GR1 is honoured in both arms** — “Any method used shall not be reflected in the record area or the record size used within the function, method, or program”: the prefix is stripped before the record area is filled and never enters a record size a program reads, and the line delimiter likewise. Witness `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness`. | `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness` |
