| DOC-A.1-115 | **Line sequential character set**, §14.9.30.4 GR16 + §9.1.13.2 item 7 (the '09' side) and §14.9.51.4 GR23 + §14.9.35.4 GR17 d) + §9.1.13.10 item 1 (the '71' side), required + documented | **Every character whose code point is U+0020 (SPACE) or above is a member of the line sequential character set; the characters BELOW U+0020 — the C0 controls U+0000–U+001F, which include the two line delimiters CR U+000D and LF U+000A, and the tab U+0009 — are outside it.** DEL (U+007F) and everything above it, the whole Latin-1 supplement included, are members, so an ordinary 8-bit text file is read without '09' (pinned: the golden's `B2ORD4=128` / `B2ORD5=256`, FUNCTION ORD's 1-based ordinals for 0x7F and 0xFF). **ONE set, three rules** (kb/Work PB329): a SUCCESSFUL READ whose record area holds a non-member sets '09' (§14.9.30.4 GR16 / §9.1.13.2 item 7 — the record is still delivered, and '0' as a first digit raises only the continuable EC-I-O-WARNING); a WRITE (§14.9.51.4 GR23) or a REWRITE (§14.9.35.4 GR17 d) of such a record area is UNSUCCESSFUL with '71', and §9.1.13.10 item 1's "the record area remains unchanged" means nothing reaches the medium — the golden re-reads the file and finds only the records the successful writes released. `LineSequentialCharacterSet` is the set's one executable copy and `SequentialConnector.RecordAreaOutsideLineCharacterSet` its one call shape, so the read arm and the three write entry points cannot disagree about what a line sequential character is. **THE DERIVATION, IN FOUR STEPS.** (1) The delimiters are FORCED out, not chosen: §9.1.7.2 fixes a line sequential record's extent as "the number of characters between the preceding line delimiter and the following line delimiter", so a record area holding CR or LF cannot round-trip as one record. (2) The set cannot be ONLY "everything but the delimiters": the reader consumes CR/LF as the framing, so a record area could never hold one and GR16's '09' would be unreachable by construction — a REQUIRED A.1 determination that makes its own rule vacuous is not a determination. (3) The remaining C0 controls are the stream/device repertoire rather than text — NUL terminates a host string, SUB (0x1A) marks end-of-file on DOS-descended hosts, VT/FF are page controls, ESC introduces an escape sequence — and a line sequential file exists to BE plain text and to interchange with non-COBOL tools, the same property that keeps the §9.1.6 fixed file attributes of a SEQUENTIAL file out of item 129's validated set entirely — a line sequential file records no attribute because recording one would stop it being plain text. (4) ⚖ **Surveyed, not assumed** (the standing latitude rule): GnuCOBOL's `COB_LS_VALIDATE` defaults to TRUE "per COBOL 2022" and "defines for LINE SEQUENTIAL files that the data should be validated as it is read (status 09) / written (status 71)", treating data below SPACE as invalid — the same boundary and the same two statuses. **CHARACTERS, NOT BYTES.** §14.9.30.4 GR15 is explicit that a record area is specified implicitly or explicitly as alphanumeric OR as national, and this connector channel carries one char per BYTE, so a NATIONAL record area is tested two bytes at a time as UTF-16BE (§13.18.60.4 GR8 / determination D-N1): `N"CD"` occupies the bytes `00 43 00 44`, and a byte-level test would read the 0x00 halves as control characters and refuse every national line sequential record (it would have broken the standing golden `2023/pb327_national_line_sequential_fill`). A trailing ODD byte is half a national position, whose content §14.9.30.4 GR14/GR15 leave undefined; it forms no character and is not tested. **'09' WINS OVER '06'.** GR16 is stated after GR15 and its only condition is "the execution of the READ statement is successful", so it lands on a truncated over-length read too. **ONE EDITION.** ORGANIZATION LINE SEQUENTIAL is a COBOL-2023 introduction (§12.4.5.10.3 GR2; the Foreword lists “Line Sequential file organization” among the main changes over ISO/IEC 1989:2014), so this determination and every rule that consumes it exist at 2023 alone — the 2002 and 2014 copies of the golden were deleted as programs no edition below 2023 can express (kb/Work PB688). | `conformance:2023/pb329_line_sequential_charset_2023` |
| DOC-A.1-150 | **RECORD DELIMITER clause — feature-name-1 and the method associated with it**, §12.4.5.11.3 SR2 + §12.4.5.11.4 GR4, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Not provided.** No name is available for use as feature-name-1. §12.4.5.11.3 SR2 — “The implementor shall specify the names available for use as feature-name-1” — leaves the set to this implementation, and the set specified here is **empty**, which Annex A.1’s preamble licenses in terms: “Optional: The element may be provided at the implementor’s option.” A `RECORD DELIMITER IS feature-name-1` written anyway is ACCEPTED and diagnosed by name (COBOLNET1778, §2 row 26): §12.4.5.11.4 GR4 has no method to associate with the spelling, so GR5’s implementor method (item 151) frames the record instead and §12.4.5.11.4 GR1 keeps that invisible to the program. ⛔ **This is a §4.2.7-shaped decline on an A.1-OPTIONAL item, not a §4.2.6 one** — A.3 item 26 reaches only the STANDARD-1 phrase, so the clause’s two arms are declined on two different licences and the row that records THIS one is here rather than in §2. Closes as DOCUMENTED-NON-SUPPORT under the `a1-optional-not-provided` derived selector (kb/Work PB280 Q1, owner decision 2026-09-02). Witness `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness` + `conformance-test:CobolNet.Tests.Conformance.DocumentedNonSupportWitnessTests.RecordDelimiterFeatureName_NamedNonSupportWarning_PinnedToSpec`. | `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness`; `conformance-test:DocumentedNonSupportWitnessTests.RecordDelimiterFeatureName_NamedNonSupportWarning_PinnedToSpec` |
| DOC-A.1-112 | **LEAP-SECOND directive — whether standard numeric time form values greater than or equal to 86,400 may be reported**, §7.3.17 GR4 + §15.80.3 r4 + §15.3.3.3, required + documented | **NO — never.** The determination §15.80.3 r4 requires ("The implementor defines whether a value greater than or equal to 86,400 may be returned from the SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT intrinsic function") is answered in the negative: .NET's `DateTime` cannot represent a leap second, so the day's tick count is always < 86,400 seconds and the returned value satisfies §7.3.17.4 GR4's ON-mode bound ("greater than or equal to zero and less than 86,401") on BOTH directive branches. `>>LEAP-SECOND ON` is recognized (`LeapSecondDirectiveProcessor`, kb/Work PB65) and governs the ARGUMENT side of the directive as §15.3.3.3 and §7.3.17.4 GR4 require: a formatted-time argument may carry 60 in its seconds subfield (`SECONDS-FROM-FORMATTED-TIME("hhmmss", "235960")` = 86,400; TEST-FORMATTED-DATETIME accepts it), a standard numeric time form value is bounded at 86,401 (FORMATTED-TIME / FORMATTED-DATETIME present 86,400 as 23:59:60; COMBINED-DATETIME accepts it). The REPORTED side stays this negative determination on both directive branches. Golden pair `pb65_leap_second_on` / `pb65_leap_second_off`. Range endpoints pinned by `CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm`; see item 171 for the precision half of the same function. | `conformance:2023/pb65_leap_second_on`; `conformance:2023/pb65_leap_second_off`; `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm` |
| DOC-A.1-171 | **SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT function returned value — precision**, §15.80.3 r3, required + documented | **100 nanoseconds — 7 fraction digits**, the .NET `DateTime` tick resolution: the function returns the day's TICK count as the unscaled value at scale 7 (the renderer's documented contract). Range **[0, 86 400)** — LEAP-SECOND's §7.3.17 default OFF is the only supported mode (§15.5.5), so a value ≥ 86 400 is unreachable. Pinned by `CobolDateWindowingTests` (05:14:27.8124791 → 188 678 124 791 ticks, plus both range endpoints) and read through the ONE injectable `RunUnit.Clock` seam, so the precision and the clock's determinism are the same mechanism. | `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_PinnedClock_ExactTicks`; `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm` |
| DOC-A.1-176 | **SET statement — the value in a NaN PAYLOAD**, §14.9.39.4 GR34 and GR35, required + documented | **Quiet NaN: payload ZERO. Signaling NaN: payload ONE.** GR34/GR35 set the content of identifier-14 to "a canonical representation of a quiet NaN / of a signaling NaN, with the payload set to an implementor-defined value, as described in ISO/IEC 60559:2020, Clause 3, for the basic interchange format corresponding to the usage of identifier-14", and A.1 item 176 makes the choice documentable. §14.9.39.3 SR32 confines the receiver to a STANDARD floating-point usage (§3.166 / §3.167), and of those COBOL.NET provides FLOAT-BINARY-32 = IEEE binary32 and FLOAT-BINARY-64 = IEEE binary64 EXACTLY — the other three are Annex A.3 items 17/19 documented non-support (§2 rows 17 and 19), refused at declaration — so the interchange format the rules name IS the .NET carrier and nothing is approximated. **The full encodings, sign bit from GR34/GR35's own last sentence (the SIGN phrase, positive when absent):** binary32 quiet `0x7FC00000` (`0xFFC00000` negative), binary32 signaling `0x7F800001` (`0xFF800001`); binary64 quiet `0x7FF8000000000000` (`0xFFF8000000000000`), binary64 signaling `0x7FF0000000000001` (`0xFFF0000000000001`). The QUIET payload of zero is the canonical NaN every IEEE implementation produces — leading significand bit set, every other significand bit clear; the SIGNALING payload of one is the SMALLEST value that keeps the significand nonzero once ISO/IEC 60559:2020 §6.2.1's quiet bit is cleared, which a signaling NaN requires. **⛔ Written as BIT PATTERNS, never as a CLR constant:** `double.NaN` is `0xFFF8000000000000` — its sign bit is SET — so GR34's "otherwise the sign is positive" would have to be spelled `-double.NaN` and would depend on how a constant negation folds; and the CLR has no signaling-NaN constant at all, because every managed arithmetic path quiets one. **⛔ And delivered to the item with no binary64 anywhere on the way (kb/Work PB961):** a binary32 → binary64 widening QUIETS a signaling NaN, so the value is materialized by an opaque runtime call over the bits (`CobolFloat.FromBinary32Bits` / `FromBinary64Bits` — an inline `BitConverter` reinterpretation of a constant is a JIT floating constant, which the JIT carries as a double, and it stored the binary32 signaling NaN as the QUIET `0x7FC00001`), and a windowed or record-image FLOAT-BINARY-32 encodes and decodes through the binary32 carrier's own lanes (`CobolNum.FormatImageSingle` / `ParseImageSingle`). A same-usage MOVE of the item copies it on its own carrier, as §14.9.25.4 GR6 c) requires ("transferred to the receiving data item without change"), so a copy keeps the payload too. ONE table, `IeeeSpecials.Bits` (`src/Cobol.Net.Compiler/Binding/IeeeSpecials.cs`), is both the emitted encoding and the documented one; `RuntimeApi.FloatFromBits` spells it. The quiet-versus-signaling distinction is COBOL-observable through §8.8.4.4.4 GR3 j)/k)'s FLOAT-NOT-A-NUMBER-QUIET / -SIGNALING class conditions (kb/Work PB225), decided on the carrier's raw bits by `CobolFloatClass`; `CobolFloatClassTests` pins that every value this table writes classifies as what it was written as. | `unit:IeeeSpecialsTests.EveryCanonicalValue_DecodesToWhatItsGeneralRuleRequires`; `unit:IeeeSpecialsTests.TheStoredImage_CarriesTheDocumentedBits`; `conformance:2014/pb452_set_content_float`; `conformance:2014/pb961_signaling_nan_binary32`; `unit:CobolFloatClassTests.EverySetFormat15Value_ClassifiesAsWhatItWasWrittenAs` |
| DOC-A.1-174 | **SET statement — the effect of SET on the FUNCTION whose address is being stored in a function-pointer**, §14.9.39.4 GR15, required + documented | **NONE. Storing a function's address does nothing whatever to the function.** GR15 says only that the effect "is implementor-defined", so the whole obligation is to say what this implementation does, and what it does is defined by what a function-pointer IS here: the externalized function-name (§8.4.3.12.4 GR2 — "For a COBOL function, the address is that of the function identified by the externalized function-name in its FUNCTION-ID paragraph"), carried in the managed `FunctionPointer` identity struct. **Taking that address therefore performs no activation, no loading, no linking, no allocation and no initial-state establishment**: §14.6.2.3.2's initial state is established "the first time the function … is activated in a run unit", and a SET is not an activation (§8.4.3.2.4 GR6 is the only thing that activates a function). Concretely: a function's WORKING-STORAGE is untouched, a previously activated function keeps its §13.5.4 GR1 static data exactly as it stood, and a never-activated function stays never-activated — `ProgramTable.FunctionAddressOf` performs a registry LOOKUP only, and its one side effect (the separately-compiled sibling-module probe) is the same module load a CALL would perform and changes no COBOL-visible state. Consequently a function's RESULT cannot depend on whether its address was taken, which is what the witness measures. ⚠ The empty answer is the substantive one: an implementation that (say) pinned or eagerly initialized the function would have to say so here, and a program relying on either would be relying on an implementor definition. **The witness is an OBSERVATION, not a restatement:** `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer` activates `FUNCTION PBF8DBL(21)` before the first SET and again after the last, and both activations print `000000042` — so the function's result cannot depend on whether its address was taken. | `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer`; `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime` |
| DOC-A.1-175 | **SET statement — the effect of SET on the PROGRAM whose address is being stored in a program-pointer**, §14.9.39.4 GR17, required + documented | **NONE. Storing a program's address does nothing whatever to the program.** GR17 says only that the effect "is implementor-defined", so the obligation is to say what this implementation does, and it is fixed by what a program-pointer IS here: the externalized program-name of an OUTERMOST program (§8.4.3.13.4 GR2), carried in the managed `ProgramPointer` identity struct. `SET … TO ENTRY` performs a registry LOOKUP only (`ProgramTable.EntryOf` — its one side effect, the separately-compiled sibling-module probe, is the same module load a CALL would perform and changes no COBOL-visible state), and `SET pp TO pp2` / `TO NULL` is a carrier copy (`SetEmitter.EmitSetProgramPointer`). **So the SET performs no activation, no CANCEL, and no initial-state establishment**: a program already activated keeps its WORKING-STORAGE in its last-used state (§8.6.4), a never-activated program stays never-activated, and a later CALL through the pointer is an ordinary CALL of that program (§14.9.4). ⚠ The empty answer is the substantive one, exactly as for item 174: an implementation that (say) loaded, initialized or cancelled the program would have to say so here. **The witness is an OBSERVATION:** `conformance:2002/pb460_set_program_pointer_effect` calls a counting subprogram once (`COUNT=1`), stores its address three times, and calls it through the pointer — `COUNT=2`, where a SET that re-initialized it would print `COUNT=1`; a third program whose address is stored but which is never called prints nothing. | `conformance:2002/pb460_set_program_pointer_effect`; `conformance:2002/program_pointer` |
| DOC-A.1-210 | **USAGE FUNCTION-POINTER clause — alignment, size and representation of data, and allowable languages**, §13.18.60.4 GR26, required + documented | GR26 leaves four things to the implementor and this determination answers all four. **ALIGNMENT: none.** A function-pointer occupies NO character positions and is never part of a group's §13.18.60.4 GR4 character image — it rides a MANAGED SLOT (`StorageForm.FunctionPointerRef`, image width 0), the identical posture `POINTER` and `PROGRAM-POINTER` already have — so there is no boundary for it to be aligned on and §13.18.60.3 SR14 confines it to a level-1/77 elementary item or a STRONG type declaration, where no sibling can be displaced by it. **SIZE: 8 character positions** wherever a size is asked for (`FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`, the `ElementaryByteWidth` pin) — the 64-bit managed reference width this implementation reserves for every class-pointer leaf, shared with POINTER / PROGRAM-POINTER / OBJECT REFERENCE / INDEX so the four cannot drift. **REPRESENTATION: the function's EXTERNALIZED FUNCTION-NAME**, held in the readonly record struct `FunctionPointer(string? Name)` (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Control/FunctionPointer.cs`) and resolved through the ONE run-unit `ProgramTable`; the predefined address NULL is the null name, which §8.4.3.10.4 GR2's "guaranteed not to represent the address of any function" satisfies because no FUNCTION-ID paragraph can name a function null. It is deliberately a SEPARATE type from `ProgramPointer`, whose representation is the same shape: §8.4.3.10.4 keeps the predefined NULLs apart by category and §14.9.39.3 SR20/SR21 forbid mixing the categories in one SET, so one shared struct would have made the two assignment-compatible in the GENERATED C#, the one place a binder screen cannot reach. **ALLOWABLE LANGUAGES: COBOL only.** GR26's second paragraph admits "another language for which the implementor has declared support for access by function pointers" and this implementation declares none: a non-COBOL function has no run-unit registration to resolve, so §8.4.3.12.4 GR4 — EC-FUNCTION-NOT-FOUND and the predefined address NULL — is the defined answer for one, never an undefined outcome — which the `NF-RAISED` / `B-NULL` pair of `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime` measures. | `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer`; `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime`; `conformance:negative/pb452-function-pointer-without-to` |
| DOC-A.1-177 | **SIGN clause — representation when the picture character-string contains the symbol 'S' and no optional SIGN clause applies**, §13.18.52.4 GR4 + §8.5.1.5, required + documented | **The operational sign is FUSED onto the TRAILING digit position, and WHICH characters it uses is the compile option `--sign-encoding`, default `ibm`** — owner decision kb/Work PB803, 2026-09-09: *"Keep both behind an option with the default being IBM and Micro Focus compatibility."* GR4 grants both halves at once (“Neither the representation nor the position of that operational sign is specified by the symbol 'S'. The implementor shall specify the position and representation of the operational sign”) and §8.5.1.5 repeats the obligation (“if it is not used, operational signs will be represented as defined by the implementor”), so this determination answers both. **POSITION — TRAILING**: an item with no SIGN clause is byte-identical to one that writes `SIGN IS TRAILING`, and different from one that writes `SIGN IS LEADING`. **REPRESENTATION — two conventions, one table pair each, chosen at COMPILE time:** `ibm` (**the default**) fuses positive 0–9 to `{ABCDEFGHI` and negative 0–9 to `}JKLMNOPQR`, so `PIC S9(3)` +123 is `12C`, −123 is `12L` and `SIGN IS LEADING` −123 is `J23`; `ascii` leaves a POSITIVE digit alone and makes a negative digit *digit + 0x40* (`pqrstuvwxy`), so `PIC S9(4)` −1234 is `123t` and `SIGN IS LEADING` −1234 is `q234`. Surveyed rather than invented: `ibm` is what IBM Enterprise COBOL, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL's `-fsign=ebcdic` write, `ascii` is GnuCOBOL's `-fsign=ascii` (its default on a PC host) — read from that compiler's published test expectations, never its sources. The value is spelled **`ibm`, not “ebcdic”**, because nothing about those characters is EBCDIC-encoded on an ASCII host: they are what an EBCDIC-descended CONVENTION produces after translation, and the misnomer would make the option unreadable. **The fused sign occupies an EXISTING digit position and adds none** (§13.18.52.4 GR5 a, which is normative and not part of the latitude), so `PIC S9(3)` is 3 character positions under either convention. **⚠ THE INTERCHANGE CONSEQUENCE, stated rather than left implicit:** the convention is a property of the **COMPILED PROGRAM** — baked into every emitted `NumProfile` and into every emitted signed class condition, never read from the process environment — so two programs of one run unit compiled under different options each read storage by their OWN convention, exactly as two vendors' programs do. Nothing records the convention of a DATA FILE: it is whatever wrote the file, and a mismatch is undetectable (a record holding `123t` read through a program compiled `ibm` yields +1230, silently). A signed DISPLAY item that must cross a convention boundary should be declared `SIGN IS SEPARATE`, whose characters §13.18.52.4 GR6 b) pins to `+`/`-` with no implementor latitude at all. ONE table pair per convention lives in `ZonedSign` (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Values/Numeric/ZonedSign.cs`), consulted by the encoder (`CobolNum.FormatDisplaySigned`), the decoder (`CobolNum.ParseDisplay`) and the class condition alike; the option reaches them through `PicInfo.ProfileInitializer(SignEncoding)`, the one place a `NumProfile` is written. | `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_default_representation`; `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_encoding_ascii`; `conformance:85/pb803_sign_default_representation_85` |
| DOC-A.1-178 | **SIGN clause — what constitutes a valid sign when the SEPARATE CHARACTER phrase is not present**, §13.18.52.4 GR5 b), required + documented | **At the SIGN POSITION: a plain digit `0`–`9`, plus both tables of the program's `--sign-encoding` convention. Every other character position must be a plain digit.** Under the default `ibm` the sign position admits `0`–`9`, `{`, `A`–`I`, `}`, `J`–`R`; under `ascii` it admits `0`–`9` and `p`–`y`. Any other character there makes the item **NOT NUMERIC**. GR5 b) — “The implementor defines what constitutes valid signs for data items” — is the whole of the latitude, and §8.8.4.4.4 GR3 n)1.a delegates the NUMERIC class condition to it by name (“Valid operational signs are defined in 13.18.52, SIGN clause”). **A PLAIN DIGIT IS VALID, and means POSITIVE**: an unseparated sign shares a digit position, so an item whose sign was never punched — what a group MOVE of raw characters deposits, and what the `ascii` convention writes for every positive value — is well formed. **A punched character carries a DIGIT as well as a sign**, so the valid set also fixes the VALUE: under `ibm`, `12C` is +123, `12L` is −123, `12{` is +120 and `12}` is −120, and at the LEADING position `C23` is +**3**23 (the punch contributes digit 3, exactly as it does at the trailing position of `12C`) — a sign-only reading would answer +123 and be wrong. The refused near-misses are worth naming: `+` and `-` are §13.18.52.4 GR6 b)'s SEPARATE characters and are NOT valid here, and neither are a space, a lower-case `l`, or a letter past the end of the run such as `Z`. **The set is DERIVED from the same `ZonedSign` tables the encoder punches with** — `ZonedSign.IsSignCharacter`, reached from `CobolClass.IsNumericZoned` (the class condition, emitted by `ConditionRenderer.RenderClass`) and from `CobolNum.IsNumericImage` (§14.6.13.2 rule 2's checked sending read, which the standard defines BY REFERENCE to the class condition) — never written a second time; it previously stood as a literal character-range pattern beside the tables and could only ever have answered for one convention. **GR5 a) is documented with it because it is NOT latitude:** the sign is *presumed to be associated with* a DIGIT position, so `PIC S9(3) SIGN IS LEADING` and `PIC S9(3) SIGN IS TRAILING` are each **3** character positions, never 4. What a numeric operation makes of a character OUTSIDE the valid set is a different obligation (§14.6.13.2 / EC-DATA-INCOMPATIBLE) and is not filed here. | `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_valid_set`; `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_encoding_ascii`; `conformance:85/pb803_sign_default_representation_85` |
| DOC-A.1-56 | **DISPLAY statement — data conversion**, §14.9.11.4 GR1, required + documented | A numeric item converts to its **PICTURE-digit image**: the digit positions the description declares, leading zeros intact, the decimal point and sign rendered per the item's description. ⛔ **A BinaryCapacity item (COMP-5 / the BINARY-CHAR family) holding a beyond-PICTURE value displays the PICTURE-digit image — the value modulo 10^digits — NOT the full container value** (owner decision 2026-08-08, kb/Work R13: the vendors are split — IBM renders the full container value, GnuCOBOL truncates to the PICTURE — and COBOL.NET **follows GnuCOBOL**). The full container value remains reachable through the spec-fixed MOVE path (§14.9.25.4 GR6a) into a wider receiver. Pinned by `comp5_display_beyond_picture` (446744073709551615 displayed for a stored 2^64−1; 18446744073709551615 after MOVE to PIC 9(20)). ⛔ **A strongly-typed group with a class pointer/object leaf** (the only conforming spelling of such a group, §13.18.60.3 SR14) is a legal identifier-1 — §14.9.11.3 SR1 bars only an item OF class message-tag, object or pointer, and a strongly-typed group's class is its type-name (§8.5.2.1) — and **displays its STORAGE image: each pointer/object leaf contributes its 8 reserved placeholder positions as SPACES, never the managed reference** (kb/Work PB244). This is the byte extent D-SLOT reserves for the leaf in a byte-addressed storage area, so the group shows the same characters from a record struct as from a BASED/ADDRESS-OF cell, and `FUNCTION LENGTH` equals the displayed width. GnuCOBOL (the precedence tiebreaker) displays a group's raw storage, including a pointer's address bytes; COBOL.NET's pointer has no address bytes (a managed reference), so its storage positions are the placeholders. The same one-way image is a MOVE's sending group (§14.9.25.4 GR4). Comparison of two such groups and every read-back still refuse it, because the placeholder image is neither injective nor invertible. | `conformance:2002/pb148_display_forms`; `conformance:2023/comp5_display_beyond_picture`; `conformance:2002/pb244_pointer_group_transfer` |
| DOC-A.1-22 | **CHAR-NATIONAL function — which one of the multiple characters is returned**, §15.16.4 r2 (second sentence), required + documented | §15.16.4 r2 has TWO branches and only the second is an implementor obligation. **Branch 1 — the order is DEFINED** (an `ALPHABET … FOR NATIONAL` literal phrase with `ALSO`): the standard itself answers — “the character returned is the first character defined for that character position”, and §12.3.7.4 GR7 k)6 fixes which one that is (“Literal-1 is the first character in the sequence of multiple characters defined at that ordinal position”). COBOL.NET obeys it (`NationalCollation.CharAt` reads the emitted `__COLLATE_NAT` table in the alphabet’s own written order), but that is CONFORMANCE, not a determination. **Branch 2 — the order is UNDEFINED, and this is item 22:** an `ALPHABET … FOR NATIONAL IS LOCALE` sequence is an ALGORITHM, not a written order, so every set of characters the locale collates EQUALLY shares one ordinal position with no order among its members. **COBOL.NET returns the LOWEST-CODED member of that rank** — `LocaleCollation.OrderVector.FirstOfRank`, a vector materialized once per collator, so the answer is the same for every invocation at a given implementation, collating sequence and ordinal position, exactly as r2’s third sentence requires. (Position 1 of a locale national sequence is the completely-ignorable rank; its lowest-coded member is U+0000.) Determination **L7** (§4 locale facility) states the position model this refines; `HIGH-VALUE`/`LOW-VALUE` read `LastOfRank`/`FirstOfRank` of the same vector. | `conformance:2002/l1_char_national_locale_tie`; `conformance:2002/l1_char_national_also_positions` |
| DOC-A.1-180 | **SMALLEST-ALGEBRAIC function — usage of the argument when native arithmetic is in effect**, §15.83.3 r4, required + documented | **No restriction beyond the standard's own rules 2 and 3** (kb/Work PB122, 2026-08-29 — supersedes the earlier refusal of floating-point usages, whose rationale — that a smallest increment for an IEEE float is exponent-dependent — confused the ULP of a stored VALUE with the format minimum §15.83.1 actually asks for; r4's latitude also could not license the refusal under the STANDARD modes, where it was an over-rejection). Every numeric usage is accepted. A fixed-point argument returns **10^(−scale)** (§15.83.4 RVR2 — `S999` → +1, `99V9(3)` → 0.001, `S9PP` → +100, `BINARY-CHAR` → +1). A floating-point argument returns **the smallest positive (subnormal) value of the item's CARRIER** — the smallest algebraic difference between two representable values (§15.83.1), a property of the format exactly as 10^(−scale) is of a PICTURE: binary32 carriers (COMP-1 / FLOAT / FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-BINARY-32) → 2^−149 = 1.401298464324817070923729583289916E−45, binary64 carriers (COMP-2 / FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-EXTENDED / FLOAT-BINARY-64) → 2^−1074 = 4.940656458412465441765687928682214E−324 (34 correctly-rounded digits — full decimal128 precision; the HIGHEST/LOWEST max folds keep R10's round-trip grain), the decimal carrier → 1E−28. The §15.83.4 r1 IN-ARITHMETIC-RANGE screen on the entry is enforced beside the r2/r3 usage bars (every carrier declarable today passes under both reachable modes; the guard fires automatically if a wider carrier ever lands). Pinned by `2023/pb122_smallest_algebraic_float` and `Algebraic_SmallestFloat_FoldsToCarrierSubnormal`. | `conformance:2023/pb122_smallest_algebraic_float` |
| DOC-A.1-183 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-1 - the alphanumeric coded character set and collating sequence, the ordinal number of characters, and the correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set**, ISO 12.3.7.4 GR7 i, **conditionally required** ("conditioned upon the existence of a code-name") + documented | **Two code-names exist, so this item is LIVE** (it was vacuous until owner decision kb/Work PB793 filled the SR15 set; see A.1 item 184). GR7 i asks for three things per name, and Table 6 gives code-name-1 a Y in both the coded-character-set and the collating-sequence column, so each name answers all three.<br>**`ASCII` - the ISO/IEC 646 International Reference Version.** *Coded character set:* the 128 characters of ISO/IEC 646 IRV. *Ordinal number of characters:* 128; ordinal n is the character at ordinal position n of that set. *Correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set:* the IDENTITY on U+0000-U+007F - ordinal n corresponds to native character U+(n-1) - because the first 128 positions of the native alphanumeric character set ARE ISO/IEC 646 IRV (item 188 / determination D-N1). *Collating position of each character:* its native collating position, i.e. the sequence IS the native order, which is what 12.3.7.4 GR7 c already says of the STANDARD-1 keyword ("The collating sequence referenced by both the STANDARD-1 and STANDARD-2 phrases shall be as defined by the order in which characters appear in ISO/IEC 646"). `ASCII` is therefore a supported SPELLING of a set the standard also names; it is provided because other implementations spell it that way and a program that does is not otherwise portable. No runtime carrier is emitted for it.<br>**`EBCDIC` - IBM CCSID 37 (code page 037, "EBCDIC US/Canada").** *Coded character set:* the 256 characters of CCSID 37. *Ordinal number of characters:* 256; ordinal n is the character CCSID 37 assigns to code unit n-1. *Correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set:* the CCSID 37 code page itself - the whole table, derived at compile time from .NET's `Encoding.GetEncoding(37)` and never transcribed (`ImplementorCodeNames.SingleByteCodePage`). It is a bijection of the 256 code units onto exactly the 256 native characters U+0000-U+00FF. *Collating position of each character:* a character the page spells takes the position equal to its CCSID 37 code unit (so X'81' `a` &lt; X'C1' `A` &lt; X'F0' `0`, the reverse of the native order on both pairs). **Every OTHER native character** - the 65,280 UTF-16 code units above U+00FF that CCSID 37 does not spell - **takes a position greater than every specified one, in unchanged native relative order**: this implementation adopts 12.3.7.4 GR7 k3's own rule ("Any characters of the native collating sequence that are not specified in the literal phrase shall assume a position in the collating sequence that is greater than that of the highest character specified in this literal phrase. The relative order within the set of these unspecified characters is unchanged from the native collating sequence") as its GR7 i specification, so every native character has a position and none shares one. Those characters are NOT members of the coded character set: they have no ordinal and no medium representation, so no SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS or CLASS ordinal reaches them and a CODE-SET record containing one cannot be written (13.18.13.4 GR6 b). **HIGH-VALUE / LOW-VALUE under this sequence** (12.3.7.4 GR8/GR9) are the native characters CCSID 37 puts at code units X'FF' and X'00'. **Which EBCDIC**, surveyed rather than assumed: CCSID 37 is the canonical registered complete single-byte EBCDIC page and the z/OS default, i.e. the one the compiler whose users write `ALPHABET ... IS EBCDIC` means; GnuCOBOL's own default translation table corresponds to no registered CCSID and its named alternates are CCSID 500 variants, so following its LATITUDE (which names are accepted) does not mean adopting its table - none of its tables or sources are used here. CCSID 500 differs from 37 in five punctuation positions and is the obvious second row for whoever needs it. | `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets`; `conformance:2023/pb793_code_set_ebcdic`; `conformance:85/pb793_code_name_alphabets_85` |
| DOC-A.1-184 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-1 and code-name-2 - the names supported for code-name-1 and code-name-2**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR15, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **code-name-1: `ASCII` and `EBCDIC`. code-name-2: NONE.** SR15 - "The implementor shall specify the names supported for code-name-1 and code-name-2 in the ALPHABET clause, if any" - leaves the set to this implementation, and "if any" licenses an empty one in either class. The ALPHANUMERIC class is not empty (owner decision kb/Work PB793, following the surveyed model implementations where the standard leaves a point open and the vendors split); what each name MEANS is item **183**. The NATIONAL class IS empty and item **185** is therefore vacuous: the native national character set is the UTF-16 repertoire (determination D-N1) and the standard's own `UCS-4` / `UTF-8` / `UTF-16` keywords already name the ISO/IEC 10646 forms, so no national code-name would name a set that is not already reachable. The OTHER words that may stand alone in an alphabet definition are the general format's own keywords - `NATIVE`, `STANDARD-1`, `STANDARD-2` in the ALPHANUMERIC branch; `NATIVE`, `UCS-4`, `UTF-8`, `UTF-16` in the NATIONAL one; `LOCALE` in either - and the 12.3.7.4 GR10 figurative constants of a one-operand literal phrase. Any OTHER bare word is a source error, **COBOLNET1907** `alphabet-code-name`, whose message names the supported set from the table itself. &#x26D4; That refusal closes an under-rejection that was also a WRONG ANSWER: an unrecognized code-name used to be reinterpreted as a LITERAL PHRASE SPELLING OUT ITS OWN LETTERS, so `ALPHABET A-ASC IS ASCII` built an alphabet whose first four positions were A, S, C, I and every downstream reference (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE, SORT, SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS ... IN, CLASS ... IN, CODE-SET) read that (kb/Work PB770 leg e). The supported set is ONE table (`ImplementorCodeNames`), read by the binder, by the coded-character-set reference sites and by the diagnostic alike, so the names, their meanings and the message can never disagree. Witnesses, ONE PER CLASS plus the acceptance: `conformance:negative/pb770-alphabet-unsupported-code-name` refuses a code-name-1 outside the set, `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15` refuses code-name-2 on the empty national class, and `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets` accepts both supported spellings. | `conformance:negative/pb770-alphabet-unsupported-code-name`; `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15`; `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets`; `conformance:85/pb793_code_name_alphabets_85` |
| DOC-A.1-185 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-2 - the national coded character set and collating sequence, the ordinal number of characters, and the correspondence with the native national character set**, ISO 12.3.7.4 GR7 j, **conditionally required** ("conditioned upon the existence of a code-name") + documented | **VACUOUS - there is no code-name-2.** A.1 conditions this item "upon the existence of a code-name", and A.1 item 184 records the national half of the SR15 set as empty, so there is no national coded character set, no ordinal numbering and no native correspondence for this item to describe. It is stated rather than omitted because the condition is a DECISION (kb/Work PB793) that could change: the code-name table is keyed by class and the binder asks it for both, so a national row would wake this item without a code change. The refusal that proves the class is empty is the witness. | `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15` |
| DOC-A.1-188 | **SPECIAL-NAMES ALPHABET clause, UCS-4, UTF-8 and UTF-16 phrases — correspondence with the native character set**, §12.3.7.4 GR7, required + documented | The native alphanumeric and national repertoires are **UTF-16, one code unit per character position** (the D-N1 substrate). The UTF-16 phrase's correspondence with the native set is therefore the **BMP identity** — code point = code unit, and §8.5.1.4's denial of surrogate-pair recognition makes the supplementary-plane codepoint/code-unit divergence unreachable. **UTF-8 and UTF-16 name coded character sets ONLY** — Table 6 leaves their collating-sequence column empty, and GR7 g/h give each only a correspondence — so naming one where a collating sequence is required (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE, SORT/MERGE, a file COLLATING SEQUENCE) is a syntax-rule violation. **UCS-4 names BOTH** (Table 6 row UCS-4, and GR7 f: “The collating sequence referenced by the UCS-4 phrase shall be defined by the order in which characters appear in ISO/IEC 10646”); on the one-code-unit-per-position D-N1 substrate that ISO/IEC 10646 appearance order over the 65,536 single-position characters **is code-unit order**, i.e. the NATIVE national sequence, and §8.5.1.4’s denial of surrogate-pair recognition puts the only place the two could diverge out of reach. UTF-8’s external form is converted to/from the native UTF-16 form at a codec boundary. `CollatingModel` carries this split (`HasCollatingSequence` false for exactly UTF-8 and UTF-16). | `conformance:2023/l1_alphabet_ucs4_utf8_utf16_correspondence` |
| DOC-A.1-189 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, device-name (names available, restrictions on use)**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR7 and SR8, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The device-names are `CONSOLE`, `SYSIN`, `SYSOUT` and `SYSERR`**: `CONSOLE` = the process standard input (ACCEPT) and standard output (DISPLAY); `SYSIN` = standard input only; `SYSOUT` = standard output only; `SYSERR` = standard error, output only. SR8 - "The implementor shall specify the names that are available for switch-name-1, feature-name-1, and device-name-1" - is answered by ONE table, `ImplementorNames` (`src/Cobol.Net.Compiler/Binding/ImplementorNames.cs`), which is also the answer to items **190** and **191**; a SPECIAL-NAMES entry naming anything else is **COBOLNET2241** (kb/Work PB862), whose message lists the available names FROM the table. ⛔ Before that table `SPECIAL-NAMES. WIBBLE WOBBLE.` compiled and registered a mnemonic naming nothing, and a misspelled `CONSOEL IS PRT` surfaced only at the DISPLAY that used it, as a device 'CONSOEL' that "is not capable of receiving data". **Restrictions on use** (SR7's "additional restrictions"): a device's mnemonic-name-3 may be the FROM operand of ACCEPT only when the device has input capability and the UPON operand of DISPLAY only when it has output capability (`COBOLNET0817`, §14.9.1.3 SR2 / §14.9.11.3 SR2); by SR7 itself it may appear in no other statement - `SET` refuses it (`COBOLNET1757`, §14.9.39.3 SR5) and `WRITE ... ADVANCING` refuses it (`COBOLNET2243`, §14.9.51.3 SR16). Item **2** and item **59** state the default devices. | `conformance:85/pb862_implementor_names`; `conformance:negative/pb862-unavailable-device-name`; `unit:ImplementorNamesDriftTests.EveryTableName_IsDocumented_AndNamedByTheDiagnostic` |
| DOC-A.1-190 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, feature-name (names available, any positioning rules, any restrictions on use)**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR6 and SR8, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The feature-names are `C01` and `CSP`**, and their only use is as the ADVANCING operand of a WRITE statement (SR6; §14.9.51.3 SR16). **Positioning rules** (§14.9.51.4 GR25 d - item **222**): `C01` - skip to channel 1, the top of the next page, which is exactly the `ADVANCING PAGE` advance of the same statement; `CSP` - suppress spacing, a ZERO-line advance (the record is presented on the current line). ⚖ Surveyed (owner-decision protocol, `follow_gnucobol_on_split_latitude`): IBM Enterprise COBOL, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL all spell both names with these meanings. Channels 2-12 (`C02`-`C12`) are **not** provided: each names a stop on a physical carriage-control tape, and a line-sequential print file has no forms-control model to position against. **Restrictions on use**: none beyond SR6 - a feature's mnemonic in ACCEPT or DISPLAY is `COBOLNET0817`, in SET `COBOLNET1757`; a WRITE whose file has a LINAGE clause may not name one (§14.9.51.3 SR13). A name outside the table is **COBOLNET2241** (item **189** carries the one-table rule). The NIST SQ207M placeholder `XXXXX073` is supplied as `CSP` by the NIST preprocessor, preserving the zero-line advance its golden encodes. | `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingC01_IsThePageAdvance`; `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingMnemonic_ZeroLineAdvance_RecordAlwaysReleased`; `conformance:negative/pb862-write-advancing-device-mnemonic` |
| DOC-A.1-191 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, switch-name (names available, which switches may be referenced by the SET statement, scope of, and external facility for modification)**, ISO 8.3.2.3.11 and 12.3.7.3 SR8, 12.3.7.4 GR2-GR4, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The switch-names are `SWITCH-0` through `SWITCH-36` (GnuCOBOL's set) and `UPSI-0` through `UPSI-7` (IBM's User Program Status Indicators).** Every one may be referenced by the SET statement (GR3). The **scope** of every switch is the RUN UNIT - one switch shared by every runtime element (GR4 and its NOTE 1). The **external facility** that supplies a switch's initial status is the process environment variable `COBOL_<SWITCH-NAME>` with hyphens as underscores (`SWITCH-1` -> `COBOL_SWITCH_1`): `ON`, `1` or `TRUE` (any case) means on, anything else or absent means off; the value is read at the first interrogation and a later SET governs for the rest of the run unit (`SwitchStore`). Only a switch-name may carry ON STATUS / OFF STATUS condition-names, in either order, each at most once (§12.3.7.2 choice indicators, §5.2.6.4); a device-name or feature-name written with one is **COBOLNET2241**, because a system-name belongs to exactly one type (§8.3.2.3.1). A name outside the table is COBOLNET2241 too (item **189** carries the one-table rule). ⚖ Before kb/Work PB862 ANY word was accepted as a switch-name - a determination that could not stand beside a device-name set, since §8.3.2.3.1 forbids a name belonging to two types. | `conformance:85/pb862_implementor_names`; `conformance-test:AlterSwitchesDifferentialTests.Switch_EnvironmentVariableSuppliesInitialState`; `unit:ImplementorNamesDriftTests.EveryTableName_IsAccepted_InTheArmItsKindAllows` |

| DOC-A.1-222 | **WRITE statement (page advance when mnemonic-name-1 specified)**, ISO 14.9.51.4 GR25 d, **conditionally required** + documented | Item **221** provides mnemonic-name-1, so this item is LIVE. The page is advanced per the feature-name's row in `ImplementorNames`: `C01` advances exactly as `ADVANCING PAGE` does in the same position (BEFORE or AFTER); `CSP` advances zero lines. | `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingC01_IsThePageAdvance`; `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingMnemonic_ZeroLineAdvance_RecordAlwaysReleased` |
| DOC-A.1-123 | **Native arithmetic — techniques used, intermediate data item**, §8.8.1.3 / §11.9.5 / §14.7.7, required (the internal procedure need not be documented) | **The native intermediate data item is a scaled `Int128`** — a 128-bit two's-complement integer holding the unscaled value with a compile-time decimal scale (magnitude bound ≈ 1.7 × 10^38; every ≤ 31-digit operand and every aligned sum of them is exact). Any expression with a floating-point operand evaluates entirely in IEEE binary64 instead; a nested quotient carries up to 14 guard fraction digits and rounds once, at the final transfer (§14.7 NOTE 1). One value — the maximum of an unsigned 16-byte COMP-5 item, 2^128−1 — exceeds the intermediate range while being storable in an item; see item 179 for what an arithmetic reference to it does. | `conformance:2023/l1_native_arithmetic_intermediate`; `conformance:2023/l1_native_arith_lane_and_rounding`; `conformance:2002/pb125_factorial_native_size` |
| DOC-A.1-70 | **Fatal exception condition — whether execution continues, and how a numeric receiving operand is affected, when checking for the condition is NOT enabled: the arithmetic size error condition without a SIZE ERROR phrase (EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION, §14.7.5 no-phrase rule 4) and a non-finite value in a fixed-point landing (EC-DATA-NOT-FINITE)**, §14.6.13.1.3 item 8, required + documented (this row discharges the numeric-store family; other fatal conditions' dispositions are documented with their statements) | **Execution continues with the next statement, and the resultant identifier receives the LOW-ORDER digits of the result aligned at its scale** — the classic high-order truncation (`CobolNum.Store`), for EVERY value carrier: the standard-decimal intermediate (`CobolDec.ToUnscaled`, kb/Work PB74), the native scaled-`Int128` lane (`CobolNum.RescaleStoreCap` — the widening `CobolNum.Store` and `CobolEdit.Format` take, and the unchecked arm of the exact family's `CobolIntrinsics.Rescaled`, and the §14.2.3 GR9/GR10 CALL-argument landing `CobolArgAdapt.Land`; kb/Work PB288, whose crossing IS "a COMPUTE statement without the ROUNDED phrase" into a record of the formal's description and so takes this same no-phrase disposition, where it used to wrap the `Int128` intermediate in binary), and a binary64 (`CobolFloat.ToScaledUnchecked` / `CobolIntrinsics.FromDouble`'s unchecked form — the exact decimal expansion past the Int128 carrier; kb/Work PB77, 2026-08-18). Before PB77 a value past the carrier landed the saturation SENTINEL and the store kept its low digits: `COMPUTE X5 = D40` (a COMP-2 holding 1.0E+40, PIC 9(5)) stored 03715 — the low digits of `Int128.MaxValue` — where the result's are 90752. A non-finite binary64 (NaN, ±Infinity) landing in a fixed-point receiver with checking off stores ZERO. "Low-order digits" is a DECIMAL statement for a DISPLAY / COMP / PACKED receiver and the CONTAINER's two's-complement residue for a BinaryCapacity one (COMP-5 / the BINARY-CHAR family, §13.18.60.4 GR12); it is never the Int128 carrier's own binary wrap taken modulo the picture. Until kb/Work PB639 (2026-09-05) the inline arithmetic store and the MOVE composed exactly those two — the alignment multiplied inside the carrier and only THEN met the decimal capacity — so `COMPUTE WC = 1000000000000000000000000000000` into `PIC S9(9)V9(9)` stored −123822295.304634368 where 10^39's low-order 18 digits are all zero; the determination and the code had disagreed on this row's own primary path since it was written. The other resultant identifiers of a multiple-receiver statement are stored normally. **An INTERMEDIATE that cannot be formed at all** — a standard-decimal intermediate past decimal128 (§14.7.5 case 7 / no-phrase rule 3, `10 ** 100000`), a native value past the Int128 escape boundary in a value-semantics position (case 5, item 179 (3)), an exponentiation-rule violation (case 1), a zero divisor (case 2) — **has no result to store: with checking not enabled execution does NOT continue; the run unit terminates abnormally** ("abnormal run-unit termination: EC-SIZE-… (fatal): …", exit 1 — kb/Work PB75, 2026-08-18; it used to be an unhandled .NET stack trace, exit 127), whether the raise came from an arithmetic statement without a phrase or from a condition / argument / subscript. With checking enabled the §14.6.13.1.3 #4/#5/#7 path applies to those statements exactly as to arithmetic ones (golden `pb75_sdidi_overflow_outside_arithmetic`, `SizeErrorDispositionTests`). With the phrase or with EC-SIZE checking enabled the disposition is §14.7.5's own — the receiver is left unchanged and the size error condition is processed — because the CHECKED landing saturates so the capacity check sees it (item 179, kb/Work PB13). ⛔ **The §14.2.3 GR9/GR10 ARGUMENT CROSSING takes that checked disposition IN THE ACTIVATING ELEMENT** (kb/Work PB640): GR9's second branch and GR10 allocate the linkage record "by the activating runtime element during the process of initiating the activation", so the crossing's COMPUTE runs under the ACTIVATING element's `>>TURN EC-SIZE` state — with checking on, a value further from zero than the formal's description permits raises EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION there (`CobolArgAdapt.LandForFormal` → `CobolNum.StoreOrRaise`), the activating element's USE declaratives run, and §14.9.4.4 GR3 g) does NOT transfer control, so the callee never executes. With checking OFF the low-order digits above stand. The same rule and the same two dispositions cover the INVOKE lane (§14.2.3 GR9's "a method"; §14.8.2.3.3 rule 2 a)), whose landing was already caller-side but unconditionally the unchecked one. ⛔ **ROUNDED MODE IS PROHIBITED on a float→fixed arithmetic transfer is raised by `CobolIntrinsics.FromDouble` itself** (§14.7.4.3 rule 7 — EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION, the receiver left unchanged), because since kb/Work PB647 a final transfer lands AT the resultant identifier's scale and the store's rescale is the identity, so the store's own PROHIBITED test can no longer see the discarded fraction. Before the move the condition depended on the receiver's SCALE rather than on the transfer's exactness: the same statement raised into `PIC 9V9` and stored silently into `PIC 9V9(9)`. Pinned by `2023/pb77_move_past_the_carrier` (the `CMP-*` rows against the `SIZE-*` rows), `2023/pb288_call_argument_scale_landing` (the CALL crossing against its inline MOVE/COMPUTE equivalents) and `CarrierLandingFormTests`. | `conformance:2023/pb77_move_past_the_carrier`; `conformance:2023/pb75_sdidi_overflow_outside_arithmetic`; `conformance:2023/pb288_call_argument_scale_landing`; `conformance:2023/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2014/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2002/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2023/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2014/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2002/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2023/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2014/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2002/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.Store_PastTheCarrier_KeepsTheLowOrderDigits`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.EditedReceiver_FollowsTheSameTwoLandings`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.LowOrderDigits_PastTheCarrier_AreTheExactExpansions` |
| DOC-A.1-127 | **OBJECT-COMPUTER paragraph — computer-name and implied equipment configuration**, §12.3.6 GR2, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** `computer-name-1` (ISO §12.3.6.2 — optional since 2002; kb/Work PB78) is accepted with any spelling and identifies no equipment configuration: there is ONE object computer, the .NET runtime the program is compiled for, whether the name is present, absent (§12.3.6.4 GR3), or the paragraph is omitted (GR4). The clauses that follow it (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE; CHARACTER CLASSIFICATION — A.4.9 item 7, implemented, kb/Work PB64 T5) behave identically with or without a name. | `conformance:2023/pb373_object_computer_name_inert` |
| DOC-A.1-128 | **OCCURS clause — range of values allowed in the index**, §13.18.38.4 GR2, required + documented | **The full signed 64-bit interval, −9 223 372 036 854 775 808 through +9 223 372 036 854 775 807.** In this data model an index IS its 1-based occurrence number, held in a C# `long` (each INDEXED BY name is an `IndexDeclaration` owning its own `long` cell, minted by `DataBinder.RegisterIndexes`; COBOLNET_DATA_MODEL_DESIGN D3), so the allowed range is that carrier's range — `CobolIndex.MinIndex` / `CobolIndex.MaxIndex`. §13.18.38.4 GR2 requires the range to include at least occurrence numbers (1 − integer-2) through (2 × integer-2); integer-2 cannot exceed this implementation's maximum occurrence count (`CobolDynTable.MaxOccurrences`, 2^30−1), so the required interval is contained with more than thirty bits to spare. ⛔ **A value inside the range but outside the TABLE is not out of range**: §14.9.39.4 GR2 a) 1. c and GR4 b) each end "even if that occurrence is not a valid occurrence within this table", so an index pointing past its own table is legal and must not raise — a table-bounds test here would reject conforming programs. GR2's own three statements enforce the range (kb/Work PB459): a SET amount lands through `CobolIndex.TryAmount` and a SET / PERFORM VARYING augment through `CobolIndex.Augment`, each of which sets EC-RANGE-INDEX and leaves the index unchanged on a value or result outside it (GR2 b's "undefined unless the value of the index is specified by the rules of that statement" — §14.9.39.4 GR2 a) 1. b and GR4 a) specify it, so it is not undefined). A SEARCH cannot reach the boundary at all: §14.9.37.4 GR4's initial-index guard leaves the scan only when the index is within the table's occurrence count, so its `+= 1` is bounded by that count. | `conformance:2023/pb459_set_index_amount_guards`; `conformance:2023/pb459_set_index_amount_ec`; `conformance:2002/pb459_set_index_range_ec`; `conformance:85/pb459_set_index_overflow_unchanged` |
| DOC-A.1-179 | **Size error condition — whether the intermediate data item's value range is checked**, §14.7.5 item 5, required + documented | **Checked, in four places.** (1) Whenever size-error checking is ENABLED for the statement — an ON SIZE ERROR phrase, or EC-SIZE checking turned on by `>>TURN` / an exception-checking PERFORM (kb/Work PB91, 2026-08-18: this now includes every arithmetic expression the statement evaluates, in a condition, a function argument, a subscript, a SET amount, not only the arithmetic statement's own operands) — multiplication, addition and subtraction are overflow-checked at the `Int128` intermediate boundary (`MulChecked`/`AddChecked`/`SubChecked` → `CobolSizeError`, the size error condition, §14.7.5 case 5; a zero divisor in a divide operation → EC-SIZE-ZERO-DIVIDE, case 2), disposed per the phrase or per §14.6.13.1.3 (golden `pb91_native_overflow_outside_arithmetic`); with checking NOT enabled they wrap unchecked, exactly as §14.7.5's "if … the implementor defines that the range … is to be checked" conditions the case on the implementor's determination. Reachable: `HIGHEST-ALGEBRAIC` of `PIC S9(19) COMP-5` is exactly `Int128.MaxValue`, where `ADD 1` overflows the intermediate itself. (2) An arithmetic OPERAND whose value exceeds the intermediate range — possible only for an unsigned 16-byte COMP-5 item holding a value above 2^127−1 — raises the size error condition on reference (`CobolNum.Widen`), never a silent wrap: with ON SIZE ERROR the phrase takes it, without it the run fails loudly naming the operand. The value-preserving paths (MOVE, DISPLAY, relation conditions) are NOT arithmetic and carry the full container range. Pinned by `highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned` (the `S19-ADD1=SIZE` / `W19-ADD1=SIZE` legs). **(3) An SDIDI-carried value entering the Int128 carrier as an INTERMEDIATE** — an intrinsic argument, an aligned arithmetic operand, a subscript, an integer argument (kb/Work PB69, 2026-08-18) — whose magnitude the carrier cannot hold at the required scale raises **EC-SIZE-OVERFLOW** (`CobolDec.ToUnscaledIntermediate`), never the modular low-order digits; under native arithmetic such a value arises only from an integer power past the carrier (the documented double approximation — an intrinsic with such an argument computes on its SDIDI body instead, a relation compares it exactly, an arithmetic operation with it evaluates on the SDIDI; a store of it is the checked §14.7 transfer, SIZE ERROR). Pinned by `2023/pb69_native_power_past_the_carrier` and `CobolDecDivisionTests`. **(4) The receiver-bound radix-point ALIGNMENT of a result, under checking** (kb/Work PB639, 2026-09-05). This is §14.7.5 case 3, not case 5 — a RECEIVER overflow "after radix point alignment" — but it shares the carrier, and the alignment multiply wraps: a widening the `Int128` carrier cannot form was aligned first and capacity-checked afterwards, so a wrap that landed back inside the picture read as fitting. `CobolNum.WideningFits` is now consulted BEFORE the multiply by all three siblings (`TryStore`, `RescaleChecked`, `RescaleEscape`) and by `CobolEdit.TryFormat`: a value the carrier cannot align is further from zero than ANY fixed-point receiver permits, so it is the size error condition and storing rule 2 leaves the receiver unchanged. Before this, `COMPUTE WC = 340282366920938463463374607432 ON SIZE ERROR` into `PIC S9(9)V9(9)` — a legal 30-digit literal that is ceil(2^128 / 10^9) — ran NOT ON SIZE ERROR and stored +0.231788544, and the same statement under `>>TURN EC-SIZE CHECKING ON` raised nothing at all. | `conformance:2023/highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned`; `conformance:2023/pb69_native_power_past_the_carrier`; `conformance:2023/pb91_native_overflow_outside_arithmetic`; `conformance:2023/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.TryStore_AWideningThatWrapsBackIntoCapacity_IsStillASizeError` |
| DOC-A.1-202 | **Time formats and corresponding function values — maximum number of digit positions in the decimal fraction of the seconds subfield**, §15.3.3.2, required + documented | **18.** §15.3.3.2 requires the implementor to define this maximum and requires it to be **≥ 9**; 18 is chosen because it is exactly where the value stops being exactly representable — a fraction of 19 or more digits overflows the emitter's `(long)Pow10.AsWide(width)` and prints garbage, so the documented bound and the representable range are the SAME number rather than two constants that can drift apart. Enforced by `DateTimeFormatGrammar.MaxFractionDigits`, which the §15.3 format recogniser consults, so a format with a longer fraction is rejected at COMPILE time (`COBOLNET1631`) instead of fabricating a value. | `conformance:2023/l1_time_fraction_max_digits` |
| DOC-A.1-92 | **Characteristics and representation of a numeric intrinsic function's returned value, and its text image**, §15.4.1 + §14.9.11.4 GR1, required — documentation NOT required by A.1 (voluntary); partially addresses A.1-56 | §15.4 places a function's returned value in a **temporary elementary data item**, and under NATIVE arithmetic §15.4.1 makes "the characteristics and representation of the returned value … defined by the implementor"; §14.9.11.4 GR1 independently makes any conversion between a DISPLAY operand and the device implementor-defined. **COBOL.NET's determination: a numeric function result used as TEXT renders in the LITERAL form of its value** — the significant digits with **no leading-zero padding**, a leading `-` when negative (never a zoned over-punch, because this is not a stored item), and a decimal point followed by exactly the result's scale in fraction digits when the value is scaled. A FLOAT-valued function (SQRT, trig, financial) renders through the same shortest-round-trip `CobolFloat.Display` a COMP-2 item does, so the function and an item holding its value agree. **The §15.4 temporary a statement materializes the value into** (the MOVE multi-receiver hoist of §14.9.25.4 GR1, an EVALUATE subject, an INVOKE argument) **has the same text image**: it is flagged as the function's returned value and described at scale 0 for an INTEGER function (§15.2 item 5), so `MOVE FUNCTION INTEGER(N) TO A B` stores in each character receiver what `MOVE FUNCTION INTEGER(N) TO A` does (kb/Work PB1007; a NUMERIC function's temporary keeps the implementor scale 9, which only `--permissive` can move to a character receiver). **AND ITS LANDING IN A FIXED-POINT ARITHMETIC RECEIVER TAKES THE STATEMENT'S ROUNDING MODE, ONCE, AT THAT RECEIVER'S SCALE (kb/Work PB647).** The quantizer used to round NEAREST-AWAY-FROM-ZERO at a working scale whatever the statement said, while the MOVE channel took the receiver's mode at the receiver's scale, so one returned value landed two ways in one receiver: with `S PIC 9V9(9)`, `MOVE FUNCTION SQRT(3) TO S` gave 1.732050807 and `COMPUTE S = FUNCTION SQRT(3)` gave 1.732050808, and `FUNCTION SQRT(0.9999999999)` into `PIC 9V9` split across a whole tenth. §15.4.1's identity sentence forbids that outright, and §8.8.1.3's native latitude reaches the INTERMEDIATE, not the transfer into a resultant identifier, which §14.7.4.1 puts "relative to the size provided for the resultant identifier" and §14.7.4.3 rule 2 makes TRUNCATION when no ROUNDED phrase is written. **The determination is therefore: the float family's returned value is a binary64, and its transfer into a fixed-point resultant identifier is the SAME transfer a MOVE of that value performs** — the receiver's scale, the statement's mode, one rounding (`ReceiverContext.FloatLanding`; the same rule governs native `**`). A nested float operand is not that transfer and lands TRUNCATED at the working scale, the receiver store performing the one rounding. The retired rationale — that rounding at the working scale is the better approximation of the returned VALUE — was true of the value and irrelevant to the transfer; the visible gain is that ROUNDED now does something on this family at all, where `COMPUTE S ROUNDED` and no-phrase `COMPUTE S` used to answer alike. ⚠ Residual, stated honestly: a nested float operand's working scale is `max(receiverScale, 9)` with no guard digits, so at a receiver scale ≥ 9 an expression over a float FUNCTION is a digit adrift of the same expression over a COMP-2 holding the identical value (`FUNCTION SQRT(3) * 2` into `PIC 9V9(9)` gives 3.464101614 where `C2 * 2` gives 3.464101615). Pinned by `pb647_float_landing_mode` at 2002/2014/2023 and `FloatLandingModeDriftTests`. Examples: `DISPLAY FUNCTION ORD("A")` → `66` (§15.70.1 — ordinal position, lowest is 1); `DISPLAY FUNCTION MIN(3 -14 0 8 -3)` → `-14`; `MOVE FUNCTION ORD("A") TO PIC X(10)` → `66` left-justified, space-filled (§14.9.25.4 GR6). **⛔ The rule exists to make the compile-time FOLD unobservable.** An intrinsic over constant arguments folds to a numeric literal and a literal renders as its own text (`DISPLAY 42` → `42`); had a computed result been given a padded fixed width instead, `DISPLAY FUNCTION LENGTH(X)` and `DISPLAY FUNCTION ORD(C)` would print in visibly different formats for no reason a COBOL programmer could see. One rule, both paths — pinned by the golden `da2_function_as_text`, whose first four lines are two fold/compute pairs of the same value. **The Table-16 consequence (kb/Work PB73, adjudicated 2026-08-18):** the function's §15.2 TYPE is its §14.9.25.3 Table-16 row as a MOVE sender — an INTEGER function (ISO §15.2 item 5, "no digits to the right of the decimal point": LENGTH, ORD, INTEGER-OF-DATE, ABS/MAX/MIN over integers, …) is the Integer row and moves to an alphanumeric or national receiver in this text form (`MOVE FUNCTION ORD("A") TO PIC X(10)` → `66`); a NUMERIC function (item 4 — SQRT, NUMVAL, RANDOM, MAX over a non-integer, …) is the NONINTEGER row and moves only to a numeric or numeric-edited receiver, whatever a particular reference's value (the principle §8.4.3.2.3 SR11 states for the integer-operand positions) — COBOLNET0819. `--permissive` keeps the pre-adjudication admission with a warning, rendering the same literal text. Pinned by `2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`, the negatives `pb73-move-numeric-function-to-alphanumeric` / `pb73-move-numval-function-to-national`, and `MoveTable16FunctionTypeTests`. Note this deliberately differs from GnuCOBOL, which zero-pads a computed `ORD` to its field width while printing a folded `MAX` minimally; the standard permits either, and internal consistency was preferred over matching a compiler that is not self-consistent here. **NUMVAL and NUMVAL-C HAVE NO NATIVE WORKING SCALE, BECAUSE §15.4.1'S LATITUDE DOES NOT REACH THEM (kb/Work PB251, rows RV-15.4.1-2 / RV-15.4.1-L2.2).** §15.4.1's closing sentence is a CONDITIONAL — "When a numeric or integer function does not have an equivalent arithmetic expression, its returned value is implementor-defined **unless otherwise specified in the function definition**" — and §15.67.4 r1 / §15.68.4 r1 are exactly such a specification, written once with no arithmetic-mode qualification: "The returned value is the numeric value represented by argument-1". Neither function has an equivalent arithmetic expression, so the latitude is withdrawn and **the native returned value is the parsed value EXACTLY**; only the *characteristics and representation* stay the implementor's. **COBOL.NET's determination: that representation is the SDIDI, in every arithmetic mode** — the one representation that holds every conforming argument (§15.67.3 r3's 31 digits native, r4's 34 standard) with no working scale at all, since its exponent is carried at run time. No compile-time scale can do the job: a scaled `Int128` temporary needs *i* + *ws* digits for an argument with *i* integer digits and r3 permits *i* = 31, so the only *ws* safe for every conforming argument is 7 — while `NUMVAL("0.123456789")` needs 9. The former `max(context scale, 6)` rule made a function's value depend on the SHAPE of its receiver and truncated wherever no receiver scale was inherited: `DISPLAY FUNCTION NUMVAL("0.1234567")` printed 0.123456, and a receiver was no protection either — it bounds the STATEMENT's result, not an OPERAND's precision, so `COMPUTE R = FUNCTION NUMVAL("0.1234567") * 10000000` stored 1234560 into `PIC 9(9)`. Pinned by `pb251_numval_native_value_fixed`, whose native lines are the SAME lines `pb60_numval_standard_decimal` prints for the same references. **NUMVAL-F under native arithmetic is the FLOAT family's case (PB60 / RV-15.69.4-2):** §15.69.4 r2 makes its native returned value an approximation, and the approximation IS a binary64 wherever no fixed-point scale governs the render — a receiver-less context (a DISPLAY/STRING operand, a relation operand, an argument, a subscript) or a float receiver — rendered through the same shortest-round-trip `CobolFloat.Display` a COMP-2 item uses and compared natively (§8.8.4.2.4); a fixed-point ARITHMETIC receiver and a MOVE sender (the receiver's scale is known — `ReceiverContext.MoveSender`) keep the EXACT Int128 parse at the receiver-capped `max(context scale, 9)` working scale, so `MOVE FUNCTION NUMVAL-F("1.5E-8") TO PIC V9(9)` stores 0.000000015 digit-for-digit. Pinned by `pb60_numvalf_native_channels`. **NUMVAL-F's determination is the NATIVE-only one; NUMVAL's and NUMVAL-C's is not.** Under `ARITHMETIC IS STANDARD-DECIMAL` (and 2002's STANDARD) §15.69.4 r3 fixes NUMVAL-F's value too ("the numeric value represented by argument-1"), so its SDIDI temporary carries the parsed value exactly at its own scale in every channel (`NUMVAL-F("1.23456789012345E-3")` renders 0.00123456789012345; a 34-digit significand is exact) — pinned by `pb60_numval_standard_decimal`. NUMVAL and NUMVAL-C behave that way in EVERY mode, which is the whole content of the r2/r3 split: the standard states the standard-decimal value separately only for the function whose native value it left an approximation. | `conformance:2023/da2_function_as_text`; `conformance:2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`; `conformance:2023/pb60_numval_one_scan`; `conformance:2023/pb251_numval_native_value_fixed`; `conformance:2023/pb2_float_argument_exact_family`; `conformance:85/pb1007_function_temporary_character_receivers` |
| DOC-A.1-92 | **Evaluation of an exact-family intrinsic whose ARGUMENT is floating-point**, §15.4.1, required — documentation NOT required by A.1 (voluntary) | §15.4.1 makes the characteristics and representation of a returned value "defined by the implementor" under native arithmetic, and each of these functions is defined by an equivalent arithmetic expression over its own operands. **COBOL.NET's determination: when ANY argument renders as floating-point (USAGE COMP-1 / COMP-2 / FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-EXTENDED), the function's equivalent arithmetic expression is evaluated in IEEE binary64** and the result is then delivered to the receiver by the ordinary rules — unquantized into a floating receiver, quantized through `FromDouble` into a fixed-point one — at the resultant identifier's own scale with the statement's ROUNDED mode when the value IS the transfer, at the working scale with truncation when it is a nested operand (kb/Work PB647). With an all-fixed-point argument list the function stays base-10 EXACT over scaled `Int128` and nothing changes. The determination is forced rather than chosen: a floating-point argument is a legal class-numeric argument (§8.5.2.12 item 2 + §8.5.2.1 Table 2), and once an operand arrives as binary64 there is no exact value left for an exact evaluation to preserve. Sign discipline is the spec's, not the carrier's — MOD floors and REM truncates (§15.64.4 / §15.77.4), INTEGER floors and INTEGER-PART truncates (§15.44 / §15.49) — so `MOD(-7,3)` is 2 and `REM(-7,3)` is -1 in binary64 exactly as in `Int128`. Pinned by the golden `pb2_float_argument_exact_family`. Before this was implemented, such a program did not merely compute differently: it failed to compile, surfacing a raw Roslyn `CS1503` from the generated C#. | `conformance:2023/da2_function_as_text`; `conformance:2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`; `conformance:2023/pb60_numval_one_scan`; `conformance:2023/pb2_float_argument_exact_family` |
| DOC-A.1-33 | **Computer's coded character set — correspondence between alphanumeric and national characters**, §14.9.25 MOVE GR6 · §8.8.4.2.11 locale-based comparison · §15.26.4 r1/r3 (DISPLAY-OF) · §15.66.4 r1/r3 (NATIONAL-OF), required + documented | Both native repertoires are **UTF-16, one code unit per character position** (item 188's substrate — note 188 itself answers a DIFFERENT question, §12.3.7.4's ALPHABET-phrase correspondence; THIS row is the character correspondence item 33 demands), so the correspondence is the **TOTAL IDENTITY, both directions**: every alphanumeric character's national correspondent is the same code unit, and vice versa. Consequences, each deliberate: **(1)** DISPLAY-OF/NATIONAL-OF **argument-2 is accepted and vacuous** — §15.26.4 r2 / §15.66.4 r2 substitute only for a character "that has no corresponding" representation, and under a total correspondence no such character exists (the §15.26.3 r2 / §15.66.3 r2 class and one-position screens still bind the argument); **(2)** the r3 **EC-DATA-CONVERSION is unreachable by declaration** from any character pathway — a conforming determination (r1 grants the correspondence to the implementor), not an accidental dead branch; **(3)** §14.9.25.4 GR6's alphanumeric→national MOVE conversion is the same identity (the reverse direction is not a MOVE at all — §14.9.25.3 SR10 / Table 16 forbid a national sender into an alphanumeric receiver, COBOLNET0819, and DISPLAY-OF is the sanctioned conversion); **(4)** CONVERT's NAT→ANUM and ANUM→NAT character legs (§15.19.4 r1/r3, whose NOTES name DISPLAY-OF/NATIONAL-OF as "the same facility") likewise never substitute. ⚠ The 8-bit BYTE serialization of usage DISPLAY (CONVERT's ANUM→HEX/BYTE legs) is a **separate** §8.1.2 NOTE 2 determination (item 209's one byte per DISPLAY position), and it is PARTIAL: a code unit above 0xFF has no one-byte image. **Decided disposition (2026-08-09, RV-15.19.4-2): the serialization substitutes `'?'` (0x3F) and sets EC-DATA-CONVERSION** — §15.19.4 r2 carries no substitution sentence because the standard's model assumes a total serialization; where ours is partial, the r1/r3-analogous substitution+EC is the smallest extension of the standard's own machinery, visible under checking and never silent (`CobolIntrinsics.ByteSub`; pinned by `Convert_ByteSerializationSetsDataConversion_WhenChecked_2023`). CONVERT's ANY source reads an item's RAW STORAGE bytes per the documented representations themselves (items 205/208/209/215, D-N1 UTF-16BE for national — `OperandText.AsStorageImage`, the one storage channel). Implemented in `CobolIntrinsics.Repertoire` (the identity, both directions); pinned by the golden `pb59_repertoire_identity` and the re-derived `national_intrinsics`. | `conformance:2002/pb59_repertoire_identity`; `conformance:2002/national_intrinsics` |
| DOC-A.1-93 | **Function returned values — returned value length exceeds implementor-defined limits**, §15.4 Returned values, required + documented (the value returned on excess shall be documented) | The maximum length of a returned value is **8,191 character positions** — the §8.3.3.4.3 SR1 / §8.8.3.2 SR2 literal-and-concatenation maximum, reused so the bound and the language’s own largest string constant are the SAME number (the BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER row documented this per-function first; it is now the project-wide determination). When a function’s returned value would exceed it, **EC-ARGUMENT-FUNCTION is set to exist** exactly as §15.4’s own sentence prescribes, and **the value returned is the §15.3 checking-off default — a zero-length value** (with checking enabled the raise is fatal and no value is returned). Enforced where a result can actually outgrow it, each as its OWN arm carrying its own citation: `CobolIntrinsics.BaseConvert` (the unbounded base-2 re-expression — a PIC X(2000) ALL "F" base 16→2 input owes 8,000 digits and still converts; 8,192+ raises), `CobolIntrinsics.BooleanOfInteger` (argument-2 > 8,191 — a §15.4 rule, NOT §15.13.3 r2's argument rule, which 8,192 satisfies; conflating the two into one guard was kb/Work PB383). Both return through the one `ExceptionState.ArgumentErrorZeroLength` mechanism, so the two sites cannot answer the same determination differently. §15.12.3 r3’s input-side maximum has no separate A.1 number — it is bounded by this determination plus the 8,191-position item bound, and the row says so rather than inventing one. | `conformance:2023/l1_returned_value_length_limit`; `conformance:2023/l1_returned_value_limit_boolean_repro` |
| DOC-A.1-50 | **DELETE FILE statement — validation of fixed file attributes**, §14.9.10.4 GR19, required + documented | **The validated set is EMPTY.** GR19 requires the implementor to *define* which of the §9.1.6 fixed file attributes DELETE FILE validates; it nowhere requires the set to be non-empty, and an empty definition is as complete a definition as any other. COBOL.NET validates **none** of them — not the organization, prime or alternate record keys, SUPPRESS WHEN attribute, code set, logical or physical record sizes, record type, key collating sequence, or record delimiter — **uniformly across every organization and storage medium** (GR19's second sentence permits the set to vary by organization or medium; ours does not, which is the simpler determination to state and to rely on). **The consequence, stated so nobody re-derives it as a defect:** GR18 sets '39' when the connector's attributes and the physical file's do not match, so with nothing validated there is nothing to mismatch and **'39' is unreachable from DELETE FILE BY DEFINITION, not by omission** — a DELETE FILE through a connector that contradicts the physical file succeeds with GR20 a)'s '00'. This is the determination the standard asks for, and it replaces the deferral ("documented non-support until a physical-attribute store exists") that stood in §3 until 2026-08-31; a deferral is not a definition. Implemented at `FileRegistry.DeleteFile`, pinned by `2023/delete_file_attribute_set_empty` (organization AND record size contradicted; still deletes). ⚠ **This says nothing about OPEN.** A.1 item **129** (§14.9.27.4 GR10) is a SEPARATE required obligation with a DIFFERENT answer — its validated set is not empty — discharged by the row below (kb/Work PB193). | `conformance:2023/delete_file_attribute_set_empty` |
| DOC-A.1-71 | **FILE-CONTROL entry, ASSIGN clause — TO phrase meaning and rules**, §12.4.5 SR5 and SR6, required + documented | §12.4.5.1 prints the TO phrase as a LIST — `ASSIGN [TO] {device-name-1 \| literal-1} …`, the ellipsis on the inner brace pair in every format — and §12.4.5.2 SR5 makes "the meaning and rules for the allowable specification of device-name-1 and the value of literal-1" the implementor's. **The determination (CLAUDE.md rule 1: ISO leaves it open → GnuCOBOL, whose documented form is a device word followed by one file name): the TO phrase is EITHER one operand, which names the physical file, OR a DEVICE CLASS — `DISK` or `PRINTER` — followed by one operand, which names the physical file.** Both device classes denote a file of the host file system, so the class word selects nothing further: `ASSIGN TO DISK "a.dat"` and `ASSIGN TO "a.dat"` name the same file. Every other list — two file names, three or more operands, a literal before another operand, or a leading word that is not a provided class (a console device such as `KEYBOARD` is not: a file connector here is always a host file) — is refused BY NAME, **COBOLNET2256**, citing SR5 and this row. **What an operand names:** literal-1's value, or device-name-1's spelling, is mapped to a host path by `CobolFile.ResolveHostPath` — a target already carrying a `.`, `/` or `\` is used verbatim; any other is lowercased and given a `.txt` extension in the current directory — the same mapping item 73 applies to data-name-1's content. **SR6 (the syntactical combinations with data-name-1):** all three are allowed — the TO phrase alone, `ASSIGN USING data-name-1` alone, and both together, in which case §12.4.5.3 GR3 b) lets the content win (item 10). The list is read in ONE place, `AssignTargetRule.Resolve`; the grammar (`CobolIO.g4#assignClause`, `assignTarget+`) takes the whole general format and used to answer a second operand with the parse error COBOL0308 (kb/Work PB829). | `conformance:85/pb829_assign_device_class`; `conformance:negative/pb829-assign-target-list` |
