A REPOSITORY class specifier whose externalized name is a .NET type (`CLASS SB AS "System.Text.StringBuilder"`) does not bind to that type: `INVOKE SB "NEW" …` is the compile-time error COBOLNET0823 (`OoBinder.OoBindInvoke`), and naming it in `USAGE OBJECT REFERENCE SB` is COBOLNET0813. The members every .NET object carries (`ToString`, `GetHashCode`, `Equals`, …) are not methods of a COBOL object: on a typed receiver `INVOKE T "ToString"` is the compile-time error COBOLNET0825 (§14.9.23.3 SR4 b)); on a universal object reference, or with the method-name held in a data item, the runtime dispatch `CobolObject.__CobolInvoke` searches only the COBOL-declared methods of the object's class hierarchy, finds none, and sets **EC-OO-METHOD** (fatal, §14.9.23.4 GR7 b)), so an unhandled occurrence terminates the run unit. GR7 e)'s implementor-defined execution of a non-COBOL method therefore never occurs. COBOL-2002, 2014 and 2023 (COBOL-85 has no INVOKE). How a REPOSITORY class specifier that names no definition of the compilation group is resolved is open under kb/Work PB1086. | — |
| DOC-A.1-104 | **I-O status — the value placed when more than one value applies**, §9.1.13.1, required — A.1 says the internal procedure need not be documented, and it is documented here anyway so that a program can rely on the value it tests | **Exactly one I-O status value is placed per execution of an input-output statement, and the choice is fixed, never timing-dependent.** (1) **An unsuccessful value always wins over a successful one**: a successful-completion warning ('0x') never displaces a status that begins with another digit (`FileRegistry.NoRewindPhraseEffect` tests the first digit). (2) **Among unsuccessful values, the statement's preconditions are tested in the order its general rules state them, and the first one that fails supplies the status**: for a sequential READ, the open mode ('47') first, then a preceding unsuccessful READ or START ('46'), then the first READ of an absent OPTIONAL file ('10'), then a record locked by another file connector ('51'), and only then the physical retrieval (`FileConnector.SequentialReadGuard`). So a failing READ keeps its '47', '46' or '10' rather than reporting a '51' for a record it would never have read. (3) **Between two successful values, a value the statement's own general rules assign wins over a value only described in §9.1.13.2**: an `OPEN INPUT … WITH NO REWIND` of an OPTIONAL file that is not present reports **'07'** (§14.9.27.4 GR11), not '05' (§9.1.13.2 item 4 a)). The first READ of that file still reports the at-end '10', so the information '05' would have carried is not lost. Same in every edition. | `conformance:2023/pb317_open_no_rewind` |
| DOC-A.1-106 | **I-O status 0x — the value of x**, §9.1.13.3 item 1, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** No input-output statement ever sets an I-O status of '0' followed by a letter: the successful-completion values COBOL.NET produces are the standard's '00', '02', '04', '05', '06', '07' and '09' (`FileStatusCode`), and its only implementor-defined I-O status is the fatal '90' (item 110). Because no '0x' value exists, the COBOL-2023 latitude on whether an upper-case and a lower-case x are equivalent does not arise. | — |
| DOC-A.1-111 | **LEAP-SECOND directive — whether a value greater than 59 seconds may be reported, and the maximum**, §7.3.17.4 GR2 (with §14.9.1.4 GR11, §15.21, §15.99), required + documented | **NO — the seconds position never holds a value greater than 59, with `>>LEAP-SECOND ON` or OFF, so the maximum is 59.** Every reported time comes from the one run-unit clock (`RunUnit.Current.Clock`, a .NET `DateTimeOffset`, which cannot represent a leap second): `ACCEPT … FROM TIME` (`AcceptSource.Time`, seconds in positions 5–6 of HHMMSScc), `FUNCTION CURRENT-DATE` (`CobolDate.CurrentDate`) and, from COBOL-2014, `FUNCTION FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE` (`CobolDate.FormattedCurrentDate`), and `FUNCTION WHEN-COMPILED`, whose stamp is the compile-time clock reading baked into the program (`ProgramEmitter`). `>>LEAP-SECOND ON` is recognized from COBOL-2002 on (`LeapSecondDirectiveProcessor`) and changes only the ARGUMENT side of the date and time functions, where a seconds value of 60 is accepted — see item 112, which also records that SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT never returns 86,400 or more. | — |
| DOC-A.1-117 | **Listings — whether and when produced by the compiler, and the effect of logical conversion**, §7.3.18.3 GR1 + §6.5 (Clause 7), required + documented | **The compiler NEVER produces a source listing**, in any edition and under any option: the `cobol` command has no listing option, and a compilation writes only the assembly, its runtime configuration and the generated C# source (`<name>.g.cs`), which is not a listing of the COBOL source. Diagnostics go to standard error and identify the source line and column. Because there is no listing, **`>>LISTING` (ON, OFF, or with no operand) and `>>PAGE` are accepted and have no effect** (§7.3.18.3 GR1; §7.3.19): they are syntax-checked and hold no state (`DirectiveStateRegistry`, NONE HELD), and they compile from COBOL-2002 on. Like every `>>` directive they are rejected at COBOL-85 with COBOLNET0900. **Logical conversion (§6.5) has no effect on a listing**, because no listing exists. | `conformance:2002/pb725_directive_facility` |
| DOC-A.1-118 | **Locale specification — how the user default and system default locales are defined**, §8.2.1, required — A.1 says this need not be documented, and it is documented here anyway because the environment variables are the user's only control over the defaults | Both defaults are decided **once per run unit, when the run unit starts** (`LocaleState`, created with the `RunUnit`), and they are always two separate settings. **User default locale:** the environment variable **`COBOL_USER_LOCALE`** if it is set; otherwise the process's current .NET culture (`CultureInfo.CurrentCulture`, which on Unix follows `LANG`/`LC_ALL`); otherwise the root locale. **System default locale:** the environment variable **`COBOL_SYSTEM_LOCALE`** if it is set; otherwise the host's installed UI culture (`CultureInfo.InstalledUICulture`); otherwise the root locale. The variables' values are locale names in the item-119 notation, trimmed; `C`, `POSIX`, `root` or `INVARIANT` names the root locale. **At least one user default and one system default always exist**: in an environment with no locale data at all (.NET invariant globalization), both fall back to the root locale and the run unit starts normally. The current locale starts as the user default for every category (§14.6.6). A program can change the user default for the rest of the run unit with `SET LOCALE USER-DEFAULT TO …`, but it cannot change the system default (§8.2.1: the capability is not provided), so `LocaleState.SystemDefault` has no setter. Example: with `COBOL_USER_LOCALE=de-DE` and `COBOL_SYSTEM_LOCALE=fr-FR`, `FUNCTION LOCALE-DATE("20260924")` gives `24.09.2026` after `SET LOCALE LC_ALL TO USER-DEFAULT` and `24/09/2026` after `SET LOCALE LC_ALL TO SYSTEM-DEFAULT`. COBOL-2002 onward. | — |
| DOC-A.1-119 | **Locale specification — manner of implementation**, §8.2.1, required — A.1 says this need not be documented, and it is documented here anyway because it decides which locale names a program may use | **A locale is named by a BCP 47 / CLDR language tag**, and POSIX-style spellings are accepted and normalized: `es_ES.UTF-8` is `es-ES`, `de-DE@phonebook` is `de-DE` with the phonebook collation, and `C`, `POSIX`, `root`, `INVARIANT` and the empty name are the **root locale** (`LocaleIdentification.Normalize`). **A locale is available** when COBOL.NET has CLDR collation data for it, a site tailoring names it, or .NET recognizes it as a culture (`LocaleIdentification.IsAvailable`). A name that is none of these, such as `xx-NOWHERE`, is not available (EC-LOCALE-MISSING when a program selects it with checking on). The run unit keeps a current locale for **each of the six categories**, LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC and LC_TIME, each set independently by SET LOCALE (`LocaleState`). **LC_COLLATE never comes from the host**: it is COBOL.NET's own collation table generated from Unicode CLDR release 48-2 / UCA 17.0.0 data, so a locale orders text the same way on every host. **LC_CTYPE, LC_MONETARY and LC_TIME** content comes from the nearest predefined .NET culture: the tag, then its parents (`sr-Latn-RS`, then `sr-Latn`, then `sr`) (`LocaleFacts`). When no ancestor is a .NET culture, or the process runs in .NET invariant-globalization mode, that content is incomplete: the invariant culture's values stand in, and an operation that needs the category sets EC-LOCALE-INVALID when checking is on. The implementor default when the environment supplies none is the root locale (item 118). COBOL-2002 onward. | `unit:LocaleStateTests.ExternalIdentification_IsNormalized_PerDeterminationL1`; `unit:LocaleStateTests.Availability_IsTheOneKnownLocaleRule`; `unit:LocaleStateTests.Set_SwitchesOnlyTheNamedCategories_AndTheyPersist` |
| DOC-A.1-120 | **Locale switch — whether a switch of the current locale by a non-COBOL runtime module is recognized by COBOL**, §8.2.1, conditionally required (documented if provided) | **No, for every category.** COBOL.NET never activates a non-COBOL module (a CALL cannot reach one, item 19). The only non-COBOL code that can share a run unit's process is a .NET host that activates a COBOL program, together with anything that host runs. The run unit reads the process culture **once, when it starts**, to determine its user and system default locales (item 118). After that, every locale-dependent operation uses only the run unit's own per-category state (`LocaleState`), and only SET LOCALE changes it. A later change of `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture`, `CultureInfo.DefaultThreadCurrentCulture` or the process's `LANG` by non-COBOL code is not utilized for LC_COLLATE, LC_CTYPE, LC_MESSAGES, LC_MONETARY, LC_NUMERIC or LC_TIME. Operations that are not locale-dependent never consult the process culture: DISPLAY of a floating-point or numeric item, NUMVAL and UPPER-CASE give the same result under `LANG=de_DE.UTF-8` or `tr_TR.UTF-8` as under `LANG=C`. A host that wants a different locale sets `COBOL_USER_LOCALE` (or its culture) before the run unit starts. COBOL-2002 onward. | — |
| DOC-A.1-122 | **National literals — number of hexadecimal digits that map to a national character**, §8.3.3.5.3 SR5, required + documented | **FOUR.** Each group of four hexadecimal digits in an `NX"…"` (or `NX'…'`) literal is one national character: the UTF-16 code unit with that value, which is also one national character position. `NX"004100420043"` is the three characters `ABC`, `FUNCTION LENGTH(NX"00410042")` is 2, and `FUNCTION ORD(NX"00E9")` is 234 (é is U+00E9). A character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane is written as its two surrogate code units and occupies two positions: `LENGTH(NX"D83DDE00")` is 2. A literal whose digit count is not a multiple of four, such as `NX"414"` or `NX"004100"`, is rejected with **COBOLNET1635** (`CobolLiteral.HexGroupViolation`). NX literals exist from COBOL-2002 on; at COBOL-85 they are COBOLNET0900. The check is not yet made for an NX argument of an intrinsic function written without the FUNCTION keyword (kb/Work PB1393). | `conformance:2023/r03_hexadecimal_national_and_boolean_literals` |
| DOC-A.1-125 | **NULL — the value of NULL**, §8.4.3.7.4 GR1 (object references) + §8.4.3.10.4 GR1–GR3 (data, program and function addresses), required — A.1 says this need not be documented, and it is documented here anyway because a pointer's NULL is visible in its storage image | **Object reference:** NULL is the .NET null reference, which never refers to an object, so `O = NULL` is true exactly when O refers to no object. **Data-pointer, program-pointer and function-pointer:** NULL is the default value of each pointer type (`ManagedPointer.Null`, `ProgramPointer.Null`, a `FunctionPointer` with no name), and it is distinct from every address `SET … TO ADDRESS OF` can produce: no data item, program or function has that address. **Storage image:** a NULL pointer of every category is eight X"00" bytes, the zero address, as in GnuCOBOL, IBM and Micro Focus. Any real address has a nonzero image (item 216). Every pointer and object-reference item starts as NULL, and SET … TO NULL restores it. The message-tag NULL (GR4) does not arise, because USAGE MESSAGE-TAG belongs to the message control system, which is not provided (COBOLNET1943). COBOL-2002 onward. | `conformance:2002/pb557_pointer_class_initial_value_is_null`; `unit:PointerImageTests.Null_OfEveryCategory_IsTheZeroAddress`; `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime` |
| DOC-A.1-126 | **OBJECT-COMPUTER paragraph — the default object computer**, §12.3.6.4 GR3 and GR4, required + documented | **The default object computer is the .NET runtime the program is compiled for**: the `Microsoft.NETCore.App` 10.0 shared framework (`net10.0`, named in the `.runtimeconfig.json` written beside every compiled assembly), on whatever operating system and processor it runs. There is only ONE object computer, so the default is the same whether the paragraph is written without computer-name-1 (GR3), omitted altogether with no containing source unit supplying one (GR4), or written with any computer-name (item 127: the name is accepted and identifies nothing). A program therefore behaves identically in all three cases: the same native collating sequence and character classification, and the PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE and CHARACTER CLASSIFICATION clauses have the same effect with or without a name. Same in every edition. At COBOL-85 an OBJECT-COMPUTER paragraph that carries a clause must also carry computer-name-1; a paragraph with no clauses may omit the name in every edition. | `conformance:2023/pb373_object_computer_name_inert` |
| DOC-A.1-130 | **OPEN statement with the OUTPUT phrase — positioning of the output file with regard to physical page boundaries**, §14.9.27.4 GR18, required + documented | Physical pages have meaning for a **sequential-organization** file (record sequential or LINE SEQUENTIAL) — the files WRITE ... ADVANCING and the LINAGE clause apply to; a relative or indexed file has no pages. **OPEN OUTPUT creates the host file empty and places the output medium at its first byte, which is the TOP OF THE FIRST PHYSICAL PAGE.** The OPEN itself writes nothing — no form feed, no blank lines, no margin: a file opened OUTPUT and closed with no WRITE is **0 bytes** (`SequentialConnector.OpenWriter`). **No LINAGE clause:** because the medium is already at a page top, the first `WRITE ... AFTER ADVANCING PAGE` (or BEFORE) still repositions to the NEXT physical page as §14.9.51.4 GR25 h) says, by writing a form feed (U+000C) — so a file whose first WRITE advances PAGE begins with a form feed (`SequentialConnector.Advance`). **LINAGE clause:** at the completion of the OPEN the device is on line 1 of the first logical page, and that logical page begins at the file's first line; LINAGE-COUNTER is 1 (§13.18.34.4 GR7). The logical page's TOP margin lines are written onto the medium immediately before the first record is presented — never by the OPEN, so a LINAGE file closed with no WRITE is also 0 bytes (`SequentialConnector.BeginLinagePage`, `EmitTopMarginIfPending`). Example: `FD F LINAGE 5 FOOTING 4 TOP 2 BOTTOM 1`, OPEN OUTPUT, one plain `WRITE` of `AAA` gives two empty lines, then `AAA` (line terminator per item 114). Same in every edition. | `conformance:2023/pb523_linage_margins_on_the_medium`; `conformance:85/pb523_linage_margins_on_the_medium_85` |
| DOC-A.1-132 | **MODULE-NAME function — which type of dynamic-length elementary item is the returned value**, §15.65.4 r1, conditionally required + documented | COBOL.NET supports more than one type of dynamic-length elementary item — alphanumeric and national, each either with the implementor-defined internal structure (no dynamic-length-structure-name, §8.5.1.10.2) or with a PREFIXED / DELIMITED structure named by a DYNAMIC LENGTH STRUCTURE clause (determination D-DL3 in §3) — so r1 requires the choice to be stated. **MODULE-NAME returns an ALPHANUMERIC dynamic-length elementary item with the implementor-defined internal structure** — the same type as a `PIC X DYNAMIC LENGTH` item that names no dynamic-length-structure-name: a native .NET `System.String`, one character per character position, whose maximum size is the implementor maximum of item 62 (1,073,741,791 characters). It is never national and never a PREFIXED or DELIMITED structure, so no PREFIXED length-field bound (65535 / 32767) applies to it. The value is exactly the name, with no trailing spaces (item 133): `FUNCTION LENGTH(FUNCTION MODULE-NAME(CURRENT))` in program `MNPROG` is 6, ACTIVATING in a main program is one space (length 1). Implemented as the `MODULE-NAME` entry of `IntrinsicCatalog` (result type alphanumeric), evaluated by `CobolModule.Name`. COBOL 2023 only — the function does not exist in earlier editions (COBOLNET1502). | — |
| DOC-A.1-138 | **Parameterized classes and interfaces — manner and time of expansion**, §7.1 (with §12.3.8.4 GR5 / GR8), required + need not be documented (documented voluntarily) | **Time:** a parameterized class or interface is expanded at compile time, once per compilation group, AFTER all text manipulation — COPY, REPLACE and the compiler directives have finished, the source has been parsed, and expansion runs at the start of binding (`OoExpansion.Expand`, called from `BinderDriver`). So a REPLACE that produces a formal parameter's name inside the definition takes part in the substitution. **Manner:** each distinct expansion (`CLASS new-name EXPANDS definition USING actual …` or the interface form in a REPOSITORY paragraph) becomes an ORDINARY class or interface: the definition's own tokens re-parsed with every occurrence of each formal parameter replaced by its actual parameter (§12.3.8.4 GR5 / GR8) and the header renamed to the expansion's name. It is never a .NET generic type, and from binding onward an expansion cannot be told from a written class — its FACTORY and OBJECT data are its own (two expansions of one definition keep separate factory counters). The parameterized definition itself generates nothing. **Identity (§9.3.12):** specifiers that repeat one (expansion name, definition, actual parameters) triple, in one or several source elements, create ONE class; reusing an expansion name with a different definition or different actuals is **COBOLNET2240**. COBOL 2002 and later. | `conformance:2002/pb759_parameterized_class` |
| DOC-A.1-139 | **PERFORM statement — whether location information is available when the LOCATION phrase is not specified**, §14.9.28.4 GR14 (through §7.3.25.4 GR7), required + documented | **Not available.** An exception-checking PERFORM (Format 3, `PERFORM … WHEN exception-name …  END-PERFORM`) enables the exception conditions its WHEN phrases name over imperative-statement-1 through an implicit TURN directive (§14.9.28.4 GR14), and that implicit directive carries LOCATION exactly when the PERFORM writes `WITH LOCATION`. Without it COBOL.NET saves no location information for a condition raised there — the same determination as the explicit `>>TURN` directive (item 204), so neither route yields location data the other withholds. Consequence inside the WHEN handler and after the PERFORM: `FUNCTION EXCEPTION-LOCATION` returns one alphanumeric space, `FUNCTION EXCEPTION-LOCATION-N` one national space and `FUNCTION EXCEPTION-STATEMENT` 63 spaces, while `FUNCTION EXCEPTION-STATUS` still names the condition. With `PERFORM WITH LOCATION` the full location is produced — e.g. `PL; MAIN-P; 12` and statement `RAISE`. ⛔ **An explicit TURN that already enables the name wins, in both directions:** §14.9.28.4 GR14 assumes the implicit TURN only "if checking … is not enabled for imperative-statement-1 by a TURN directive", so when a `>>TURN … CHECKING ON` in force before the PERFORM already enables a name its WHEN phrase lists, no implicit directive is assumed for that name and THAT directive's LOCATION setting governs — under `>>TURN EC-USER-X1 CHECKING ON WITH LOCATION` a PERFORM without LOCATION still yields `PL2; MAIN-P; 7`, and under `>>TURN EC-USER-X1 CHECKING ON` (no LOCATION) a `PERFORM WITH LOCATION` yields one space. The LOCATION flag travels on `BoundExceptionPerform.WithLocation` into `TurnState.WithImplicitEnable`. COBOL 2023 only (Format 3 PERFORM is COBOLNET0900 in earlier editions). | `conformance:2023/l1_exception_no_location_arms`; `conformance:2023/exception_statement_ambient` |
| DOC-A.1-141 | **Procedure division header rules when the activating or the activated runtime element is not COBOL — restrictions and mechanisms (parameter matching, data type representation, returning of a value, omission of parameters)**, §14.2.3 GR13, conditionally required + documented | **Supported non-COBOL language products: any .NET language, and only as the ACTIVATING element.** No non-COBOL element can be ACTIVATED: there is no CALL-CONVENTION directive (item 19), and a CALL, a program-pointer or `ADDRESS OF PROGRAM` resolves only COBOL.NET programs (otherwise EC-PROGRAM-NOT-FOUND). **Mechanism:** every generated program class implements `ICobolProgram`, and a .NET host activates the program as a called program through `ICobolProgram.Call(CobolArg[] args, CobolArg? returning)`. **Parameter matching:** positional, `args[0]` to the first USING formal and so on (§14.2.3 GR2) — never by name. A trailing formal with no argument is OMITTED; an argument beyond the last formal is ignored; this entry performs no argument-count check (EC-PROGRAM-ARG-MISMATCH for a count is raised only for a COBOL CALL, in `ProgramTable.CallProgram`). **Data type representation:** each argument is a `CobolArg(Mode, Carrier, Num, Layout)`. `Carrier` is a `ManagedPointer<T>` over the host's own storage: a `ManagedPointer<string>` holding the item's character image (one UTF-16 character per character position) for an alphanumeric, national, edited or group formal, or a native numeric cell (`long`, `Int128`, `double`, …) whose representation `Num` (a `NumProfile`: digits, scale, sign and usage) describes; `Layout` describes a group that contains a table. A formal of another shape sees the carrier through a converting view of the SAME storage (§14.2.3 GR8), so the program's stores reach the host's storage. `Mode` makes no copy: a COBOL CALL builds the BY CONTENT copy on the activating side, so a host that wants BY CONTENT passes a carrier over a copy it owns; a BY VALUE formal always adopts a detached copy (§14.2.3 GR10). A carrier the formal cannot read fails the activation with the .NET exception `CobolCallException` whose `EcName` is EC-PROGRAM-ARG-MISMATCH, before any statement of the program runs (`CobolArgAdapt`). **Omission of parameters:** pass `ManagedPointer.Null` as the carrier, or pass fewer arguments; the omitted-argument condition is then true (§14.9.4.4 GR11), a reference raises EC-PROGRAM-ARG-OMITTED when checking for it is enabled in the program (GR12), and otherwise reads an empty value (zero for a numeric formal). **Returning of a value:** the RETURNING item's content is delivered into the `returning` argument's carrier, described by that `CobolArg` exactly as a COBOL receiver is; GOBACK / EXIT PROGRAM is an ordinary method return to the host (§3, "Return to a NON-COBOL activating runtime element"). Same in every edition. | — |
| DOC-A.1-143 | **Program-name — formation rules for a non-COBOL program**, §8.3.2.2.21, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** COBOL.NET gives no program-name a non-COBOL meaning: there is no CALL-CONVENTION directive and no way to declare a non-COBOL program (item 19), so every program-name — in a CALL, a CANCEL, a program-pointer SET or `ADDRESS OF PROGRAM` — is formed and matched by the COBOL rules of §8.3.2.2 and resolves only to a COBOL.NET program. A name that happens to spell a non-COBOL module (e.g. `CALL "System.Console"`) is simply a program that is not found: EC-PROGRAM-NOT-FOUND, the CALL's ON EXCEPTION phrase, and a NULL program-pointer from `ADDRESS OF PROGRAM` (`ProgramTable.ProbeSiblingModule` accepts only a module that registers COBOL.NET programs). | — |
| DOC-A.1-148 | **RECORD clause — whether format 3 (`RECORD CONTAINS integer-4 TO integer-5`) produces fixed-length or variable-length records**, §13.18.43.4 GR17, required + documented | **Variable-length.** A file whose RECORD clause is format 3 has variable-length records in the range integer-4 through integer-5 bytes: each WRITE or REWRITE writes the record named in the statement at that record's own size, and a READ delivers the size that was written — records are NEVER padded to integer-5. On the medium each record carries the framing item 151 describes: record sequential, relative and indexed files write a 4-byte little-endian length prefix before each record, LINE SEQUENTIAL files end each record with a line delimiter. Example: `FD F3 RECORD CONTAINS 5 TO 10 CHARACTERS` with `01 R3A PIC X(5)` and `01 R3B PIC X(10)`; writing R3A = `ABCDE` then R3B = `ABCDEFGHIJ` to a record sequential file gives the 23 bytes `05 00 00 00 'ABCDE' 0A 00 00 00 'ABCDEFGHIJ'`. Format 3 differs from format 2 (`RECORD IS VARYING …`) only where the standard ties a rule to the word VARYING — the READ INTO implicit move of §14.9.30.4 GR4 b) (`VaryingRecordInfo.VaryingClause`, set by `DataBinder.BindRecordClause`). Same in every edition. | — |
| DOC-A.1-153 | **Record locking — the default mode when the user specifies none**, §12.4.5.9.4 GR1 b) 2., required + documented | **No record locking** — the third option GR1 b) 2. names. A file connector whose file control entry has neither a LOCK MODE clause nor a SHARING clause, opened by an OPEN with no SHARING phrase, SETS NO RECORD LOCKS: a READ does not lock, and an explicit `WITH LOCK` phrase on its I-O statements obtains nothing (`FileRegistry.ImplementorDefaultShare`, lock mode `FileLockMode.None`). It still RESPECTS other connectors' locks, because §9.1.16 makes a locked record inaccessible to every other file connector: its READ, REWRITE and DELETE of a record another connector has locked are refused with I-O status `51`, and READ … IGNORING LOCK reads it. Example (that two clause-less connectors can have the file open at the same time is the DEFAULT SHARING MODE, item 131, which is not yet determined — kb/Work PB322; the example shows the lock behaviour only): two such connectors on one relative file — A `READ … WITH LOCK` record 1 (`00`), B READs and REWRITEs record 1 (`00`, `00`); a third connector with `LOCK MODE IS AUTOMATIC` READs record 1 and locks it, and B's next READ of record 1 is `51`. COBOL 2002 and later. | `conformance:2002/pb669_lock_visibility_plain_connector` |
| DOC-A.1-154 | **Record locking — the maximum number of record locks a run unit may hold**, §12.4.5.9.4 GR7, required + documented | **255** — the standard's minimum (`PhysicalFileTable.RunUnitLockMax`). The count is every record lock held by every file connector of the run unit, over all physical files. An I-O statement that would obtain a 256th lock is unsuccessful with I-O status **`53`** (§9.1.13.8 item 3) and obtains no lock; a WRITE or REWRITE refused this way changes no record (the ceiling is checked before the record is touched, `PhysicalFileTable.PreflightNewLock`). Re-locking a record the same connector already holds does not count again. When the connector's own ceiling (item 155) is also reached, `54` is reported. COBOL 2002 and later. | `unit:CobolFileLockTests.RunUnitLockLimit_256thRunUnitLock_Is53` |
| DOC-A.1-155 | **Record locks — the maximum number of record locks one file connector may hold**, §12.4.5.9.4 GR7, required + documented | **15** — the standard's minimum (`PhysicalFileTable.ConnectorLockMax`). Only a connector with multiple record locking (`LOCK ON MULTIPLE RECORDS`) can hold more than one lock (GR6), and the MULTIPLE phrase is not allowed for sequential organization or access (§12.4.5.9.3 SR2), so the ceiling is reachable on relative and indexed files with random or dynamic access. An I-O statement that would obtain a 16th lock for the connector is unsuccessful with I-O status **`54`** (§9.1.13.8 item 4), obtains no lock and — for a READ — is an unsuccessful READ like any other (§14.9.30.4 GR18): no record position is established, so the next sequential READ is `46`, never the record after the refused one; a WRITE or REWRITE refused this way changes no record (`PhysicalFileTable.PreflightNewLock`). Re-locking a record the connector already holds does not count again; UNLOCK and CLOSE release its locks. COBOL 2002 and later. | `conformance:2023/pb338_read_lock_limit_position`; `unit:CobolFileLockTests.ConnectorLockLimit_16thRecord_Is54` |
| DOC-A.1-160 | **Report writer printable item — the fixed correspondence between columns and national characters**, §13.18.14.4 GR2, required + documented | **One column per national character position** — the same one-to-one correspondence as for an alphanumeric character. A national printable item described `PIC N(n)` (explicit or implied USAGE NATIONAL, including one inherited from a group entry) occupies exactly n columns, starting at the column its COLUMN clause names, and the horizontal counter advances by n (§13.18.14.4 GR9; `DataItem.DisplayTextWidth`, used by `ReportWriterEmitter.EmitFieldPlacements`). Example: `COLUMN 1 PIC N(4) SOURCE WN1` with WN1 = N"WXYZ" and `COLUMN 6 PIC X(3) VALUE "ABC"` on one line prints `WXYZ ABC` — the national item fills columns 1–4 and column 5 stays blank. COBOL 2002 and later (national data does not exist at `--std 85`: COBOLNET0900). ⚠ Which bytes a national character becomes in the report FILE is the record-structure question (item 159), open under kb/Work PB690. | `conformance:2002/pb541_report_national_item` |
| DOC-A.1-169 | **SAME SORT AREA / SAME SORT-MERGE AREA clause — extent of allocation**, §12.4.6.4 GR4 b) (§12.4.6.4.4), required + need not be documented (VOLUNTARY) | **The extent is NONE.** A non-sort file named in a sort-merge-area format SAME clause never lends its storage to a SORT or MERGE: `DataBinder.BindIoControl` links only the SAME RECORD AREA format and gives the SORT AREA / SORT-MERGE AREA formats no storage effect, and SORT/MERGE work storage is the run time's own managed memory, separate from every file's record area. GR4 b)'s "may be allocated" makes zero a permitted extent, and GR4 c) (the non-sort files do not share storage with each other) holds because the clause creates no sharing at all. The clause is accepted in every edition and a program that names a plain file in it sorts, reads and writes that file exactly as it would without the clause. | — |
| DOC-A.1-170 | **SEARCH ALL statement — varying of the search index during the search operation**, §14.9.37 GR9 (§14.9.37.4), required + need not be documented (VOLUNTARY) | **A forward scan in occurrence order.** The initial setting of the search index is ignored: SEARCH ALL sets it to occurrence **1**, evaluates the WHEN condition, and on a false result advances it by one, until a WHEN is satisfied or the last element has been tested (`ControlFlowEmitter.EmitAllScan`). The bound is the table's maximum occurrence count, the CURRENT count of an OCCURS DEPENDING ON table, or the current capacity of an OCCURS DYNAMIC table. The last-element test happens BEFORE the advance, so the index never holds a value outside 1 through the last element — also on an unsuccessful search, where the AT END phrase sees it at the last element tested; an empty table (current count 0) is unsuccessful with no element tested and the index at 1. On success the index holds the occurrence number of the element that satisfied the WHEN. No binary search is performed, so a table whose ASCENDING/DESCENDING KEY data is not actually in sequence still finds an element that satisfies the condition when one exists. GR9 makes the final setting after an unsuccessful search undefined, so a program shall not rely on which in-range value is left. | `conformance:85/pb447_search_all_index_stays_in_table` |
| DOC-A.1-181 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph — allowable locale-names and literal values of the LOCALE clause**, §12.3.7 GR5 (§12.3.7.4), optional (documented if provided) | **Provided (2002 and later).** `LOCALE locale-name-1 IS {external-locale-name-1 or literal-4}` accepts the SAME set of identifications in both forms: a **locale tag** — any BCP-47 tag of a CLDR locale or of a culture .NET recognizes (`fr-FR`, `de-CH`, `sr-Latn-RS`, `zh-Hant`), a `-u-` extension allowed — or the root locale spelled `INVARIANT`, `ROOT`, `C`, `POSIX` or `und`. A **POSIX spelling** `ll_CC[.codeset][@modifier]` is normalized before use (`LocaleIdentification.Normalize`): underscores become hyphens, a `.codeset` suffix is ignored (the repertoire is always UTF-16), an `@modifier` that names a CLDR collation type becomes the `-u-co-<type>` extension (`de_DE@phonebook` is `de-DE-u-co-phonebook`), and any other modifier leaves a tag that names no locale. So `fr_FR`, `fr-FR`, `"fr_FR.UTF-8"` and `"fr-FR"` identify the same locale; the comparison is case-insensitive. The word form is limited to what a COBOL word can spell (no `.` or `@`), so `.codeset` and `@modifier` spellings need the literal form. literal-4 shall be an alphanumeric or national literal (or a concatenation expression of that class), neither zero-length nor a symbolic-character figurative constant (§12.3.7.3 SR10/SR11, `COBOLNET0898`; ⚠ today every figurative constant, SPACE included, is also refused with `COBOLNET0898`, which §8.3.3.6.3 SR1 does not permit — an open defect, kb/Work PB1537); a locale-name declared twice is `COBOLNET1665`. **Availability is decided at RUN time, never at compile time**: any spelling compiles, and a locale that is not available (`LocaleIdentification.IsAvailable` — the root, a locale with CLDR collation data, a site `.tailor` file, or a culture .NET recognizes) raises `EC-LOCALE-MISSING` at the statement that uses it when that check is enabled; with the check not enabled the statement proceeds with the root locale's conventions (a `LOCALE-DATE` through an unavailable `"zz_QQ"` or `"fr_FR@euro"` formats as `09/24/2026`, exactly as `INVARIANT` does). Before 2002 the clause is rejected (`COBOLNET0900`). | `unit:LocaleStateTests.ExternalIdentification_IsNormalized_PerDeterminationL1`; `unit:LocaleStateTests.Availability_IsTheOneKnownLocaleRule`; `conformance:2002/pb64t1_locale_declare` |
| DOC-A.1-182 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause — coded character set referenced by the STANDARD-2 phrase**, §12.3.7 GR7 c) (§12.3.7.4), required + documented | **STANDARD-2 always references the ISO/IEC 646 International Reference Version — never a national version, under any circumstance** (no compiler option, locale or environment setting selects one). STANDARD-2 is therefore the same coded character set as STANDARD-1: its 128 characters are the first 128 positions of the native alphanumeric character set, U+0000 through U+007F, with the correspondence and ordinals item 187 states (`CodedCharacterSet.OrdinalCount` = 128; class membership `CobolClass.CodedSetKind.Ascii`). A class condition `IS alphabet-name` on a STANDARD-2 alphabet is true exactly for characters in U+0000–U+007F (`"AB~"` is, `é` is not), and a STANDARD-2 alphabet used as the PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE collates in native order. The phrase is available for alphanumeric alphabets in every edition. | — |
| DOC-A.1-186 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, literal phrase — ordinal number of the characters not specified**, §12.3.7 GR7 k) 4. (§12.3.7.4), required + documented (the scheme, not each character) | **The ordinal number of every character is its position in the alphabet's collating sequence, counting the first position as 1, and the unspecified characters are numbered in the order GR7 k) 3. collates them.** A literal-phrase alphabet's coded character set contains every native character — 65,536 UTF-16 code units, for an alphanumeric and a national alphabet alike (`CodedCharacterSet.OrdinalCount`). The characters the literal phrase specifies take ordinals 1 through *n*, one per collating position in the order written (a THROUGH range fills successive positions; an ALSO group shares one position, and that ordinal names its first character, per GR7 k) 6.). The characters NOT specified then take ordinals *n*+1, *n*+2, … in ascending native code order, skipping the specified ones (`CodedCharacterSet.CharAt`). Worked example: `ALPHABET AL IS "Z" "Y" ALSO "W" "X"` has *n* = 3, so ordinal 1 is `Z`, 2 is `Y`, 3 is `X`, 4 is U+0000, 5 is U+0001, and ordinal 70 is `B` (U+0042, the 67th unspecified character). These are the ordinals `SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS … IN alphabet-name` and a numeric `CLASS … IN alphabet-name` literal use. Because an ALSO character has no ordinal of its own, the highest ordinal is 65,536 minus the number of ALSO characters (in the example, ordinal 65,535 is U+FFFF and 65,536 is `COBOLNET1670`). The same in every edition. | — |
| DOC-A.1-187 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause — correspondence of the STANDARD-1 and STANDARD-2 phrases with the native character set**, §12.3.7 GR7 c) (§12.3.7.4), required + documented | **The identity on U+0000–U+007F.** Both phrases reference ISO/IEC 646 IRV (item 182), and each of its 128 characters corresponds to the native alphanumeric character with the same code: ordinal *n* of the standard set is native character U+(*n*−1), so `SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS ... IN` a STANDARD-1/-2 alphabet accepts ordinals 1 through 128 (129 or more is `COBOLNET1670`), and ordinal 66 is `A`. The collating sequence is the native order of those characters. Native characters above U+007F have no counterpart in the standard set: they are not members for a class condition `IS alphabet-name` (`é` is not), and no ordinal reaches them. For `CODE-SET` the correspondence converts as the identity — a record of IRV characters is written to and read from the medium unchanged. ⚠ A record character above U+007F under a STANDARD-1, STANDARD-2 or `ASCII` CODE-SET is at present NOT screened: it reaches the medium as its native code unit (`é` is written as X'E9', status 00), where the `EBCDIC` code-set refuses an unrepresentable character (today by an unhandled run-time exception rather than an I-O status); both are one open defect, kb/Work PB1542, so a program shall not rely on writing such a character. Implemented by `CodedCharacterSet` (128 ordinals, identity medium) and `CobolClass.CodedSetKind.Ascii`; the same in every edition. | `conformance:2023/pb109_class_condition_alphabet`; `conformance:2023/pb110_code_set_identity` |
| DOC-A.1-193 | **STOP and GOBACK statements — mechanism for error termination**, §14.9.42 GR4 (§14.9.42.4) · §14.9.18 GR9 (§14.9.18.4), optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** No implementor-defined mechanism can mark a run unit as terminating in error, so a `STOP RUN` or a main program's `GOBACK` written without an ERROR, NORMAL or STATUS phrase always reports a normal termination — process exit code **0** — whatever the program did before it. There is no `RETURN-CODE` special register (a reference to it is `COBOLNET1639`, not defined), no compiler option and no run-time setting that changes this; only the ERROR / STATUS phrases of the terminating statement (item 192) and an abnormal run-unit termination on a fatal exception condition (item 44), which is not a STOP or GOBACK, produce a nonzero exit code. | `conformance-test:StopGobackExitCodeTests.TerminationStatus_SetsProcessExitCode` |
| DOC-A.1-194 | **Subscripts — mapping of an index value to an occurrence number**, §8.4.2.3 GR1 c) (§8.4.2.3.4), required + need not be documented (VOLUNTARY) | **The identity.** An index holds its 1-based occurrence number directly — a 64-bit integer, never a byte offset or displacement (the storage of an index is item 211) — so subscripting by `index-name-1` selects the occurrence equal to the index value (`BoundIndexRef`; `NumericRenderer.Visit`). `index-name-1 + integer-1` and `index-name-1 − integer-1` select the occurrence the index value plus or minus the integer names, computed without changing the index. A `SET` of a numeric item to an index, or of an index to another index of the same table, carries that occurrence number unchanged. Example: with `05 E PIC X OCCURS 5 TIMES INDEXED BY I` holding "ABCDE", after `SET I TO 3` the references `E(I)`, `E(I + 1)` and `E(I - 2)` are `C`, `D` and `A`, and `SET N TO I` stores 3. | `conformance:85/pb215_index_windows` |
| DOC-A.1-196 | **SYNCHRONIZED clause — how the records of a file are handled**, §13.18.55 GR11 (§13.18.55.4), optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** COBOL.NET has no rule for synchronizing the records of a file: a record area is never aligned or padded as a whole, and a SYNCHRONIZED elementary item in a record sits at exactly the offset it would have without the clause (item 195), so the record written to and read from the file is byte-for-byte the record's own image. Example: `01 R. 05 A PIC X. 05 B PIC S9(4) COMP SYNC. 05 C PIC X.` with "Q", 258 and "Z" writes the 4 bytes `51 01 02 5A` to a sequential file. The same in every edition. | — |
| DOC-A.1-197 | **SYNCHRONIZED clause — positioning when neither RIGHT nor LEFT is specified**, §13.18.55 GR3 (§13.18.55.4), required + documented | **The item is positioned exactly where it would be without the clause: it begins at the byte immediately after the preceding item.** COBOL.NET treats every byte position as a natural boundary — a record is a typed .NET structure, not an addressable byte buffer, so an unaligned binary item costs no extra machine operations — and therefore performs no positioning, adds no slack bytes, and does not change the size of the item or of any containing group or REDEFINES (item 195). The same is true of `SYNCHRONIZED LEFT` and `SYNCHRONIZED RIGHT`: with a one-byte natural boundary an item both begins at the left byte and ends on the right byte of the boundaries that delimit it, so GR4 and GR5 are met with no adjustment, and the three forms lay out identically. Example: `01 G. 05 A PIC X. 05 B PIC S9(4) COMP SYNCHRONIZED. 05 C PIC X.` is 4 bytes, and `B` occupies bytes 2–3, with or without LEFT/RIGHT and with no clause at all. The same in every edition. | `conformance:2002/l1_byte_length_implicit_filler` |
| DOC-A.1-198 | **System-names — rules for the formation of a system-name**, §8.3.2.3 (§8.3.2.3.1), optional (documented if provided) | **Provided — one restriction.** A system-name is formed as any COBOL word of the target edition — letters, digits, hyphen, and (2002 and later) underscore, not beginning or ending with a hyphen or underscore (`ABC-` is a syntax error), at most 30 characters (COBOL-85), 31 (2002 and 2014) or 63 (2023), the length limit being `COBOLNET1567` — with ONE added restriction: a system-name shall not consist of digits only, because such a character-string is always read as an integer literal (`OBJECT-COMPUTER. 12345.` is a syntax error, `COBOL0001`). Unlike a user-defined word, a system-name need not contain a letter otherwise: `123ABC`, `MY_BOX-9` and `1-2` are all accepted as computer-names. Which system-names are RECOGNIZED is a separate matter: device-names, switch-names and feature-names are a fixed list (items 189–191; an unknown one is `COBOLNET2241`), code-names are items 183–184, and external-locale-names are item 181. | — |
| DOC-A.1-201 | **Text manipulation — stage of processing at which parameterized classes and interfaces are expanded**, §7.2 (the sentence is in §7.1), required + need not be documented (VOLUNTARY) | **At compile time, after all text manipulation.** COBOL.NET expands a parameterized class or interface when it BINDS the compilation group (`BinderDriver` → `OoExpansion`): for each `EXPANDS` specifier in a REPOSITORY paragraph it takes the parameterized definition's source text AS IT STANDS AFTER COPY, REPLACE and conditional compilation have been applied, substitutes each actual parameter for the corresponding formal parameter, and compiles the result as an ordinary, non-parameterized class or interface. So text manipulation can build or rename a formal parameter (a `REPLACE` that turns `ZZELEM` into `ELEM` before `CLASS-ID. ... USING ZZELEM` is seen gives the same class as writing `USING ELEM`), but a REPLACE never sees, and cannot alter, the text an expansion produces. Nothing is expanded at run time and no .NET generic type is used. Parameterized classes and interfaces exist from 2002 on; a class definition is rejected below 2002 (`COBOLNET0900`). | `conformance:2002/pb759_parameterized_class` |
| DOC-A.1-203 | **THROUGH phrase in the VALUE clause and the EVALUATE statement — collating sequence for a range when no alphabet-name is specified**, §14.7.8 rule 2, required + documented | **The PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE of the range's class — the same sequence a relation condition between the same operands uses.** A range of alphanumeric (or alphabetic) values, in a level-88 `VALUE … THROUGH …` or an `EVALUATE … WHEN … THROUGH …`, written without `IN alphabet-name`, is evaluated in the alphanumeric program collating sequence declared by `OBJECT-COMPUTER. … PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE` (§12.3.6), or in the native order (UTF-16 code-unit order) when none is declared; a range of national values uses the national program collating sequence (`… FOR NATIONAL`, 2002 and later), else the native national order (`CollatingSelection.ThroughRangeClass` → `CollatingSelection.ForComparison`). A national or alphanumeric GROUP conditional variable takes the sequence of its own class. A range whose first value collates after its second in that sequence is empty (with EC-RANGE-INVALID when that check is enabled). Example: with `PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE IS EB` where `ALPHABET EB IS EBCDIC`, the value `"_"` is NOT in `"Z" THRU "a"` (in EBCDIC `a` collates before `Z`), while with no PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE it is. Numeric ranges are algebraic (rule 1) and use no sequence. | `conformance:2002/pb398_range_in_alphabet`; `conformance:2002/pb728_level88_group_usage_collating` |
| DOC-A.1-213 | **USAGE NATIONAL — size and representation of characters**, §13.18.60 GR8 (§13.18.60.4), required + documented | **Two bytes per national character position, uniform at every length: one UTF-16 code unit, stored MOST SIGNIFICANT BYTE FIRST (UTF-16BE).** `PIC N(2)` occupies 4 bytes (`FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` 4, `FUNCTION LENGTH` 2), and a national-form numeric or numeric-edited item takes 2 bytes per symbol (`PIC 9(3) USAGE NATIONAL` is 6 bytes). The value of each position is the character's UTF-16 code unit — `A` is `00 41`, `é` is `00 E9`, `€` is `20 AC` — and this is the image seen through a REDEFINES, in an alphanumeric group that contains the item, and in a record written to a file. A character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane is a surrogate pair and occupies TWO character positions, each half treated as a separate character exactly as §8.5.1.4 rule 2 requires, so a MOVE that truncates between the halves keeps only the first. There is no option to vary the size or representation per compilation unit. USAGE NATIONAL exists from 2002 on (`COBOLNET0900` below). | `conformance:2023/pb646_national_form_numeric_bytes`; `conformance:2023/l1_usage_display_character_size` |
| DOC-A.1-220 | **Variable-length data items — actual time when the resources used are freed**, §8.5.1.11.3, required + need not be documented (VOLUNTARY) | **Whenever the .NET garbage collector reclaims them — a time the program cannot observe and COBOL.NET does not fix.** No variable-length item has storage of its own to release: a DYNAMIC LENGTH elementary item is a managed .NET string, so the old value becomes unreachable when a new value is stored (a MOVE of "XY" over "ABCDEFGHIJ" leaves an item of length 2), and the collector reclaims it later. A dynamic-capacity table (`OCCURS DYNAMIC`, `CobolDynTable`) holds its occurrences in a managed array that grows by doubling and is NEVER shrunk: lowering the capacity (`SET … TO`, `DOWN BY`) only lowers the current count, keeping the removed occurrences' storage until the table is enlarged again — when each re-created occurrence is created afresh as a new occurrence, never with the value it held before — or until the table itself becomes unreachable. Freeing never affects a program's results. DYNAMIC LENGTH items and dynamic-capacity tables exist from 2014 on (`COBOLNET0900` below). | — |

## 8. Rules with no observable obligation — owner-signed derivations (§4.4 / Annex A.2)

> **⛔ THIS SECTION IS THE ONE ALTERNATIVE TO A SPEC-DERIVED TEST, AND THE OWNER OPENED IT** — decision
> `kb/Work/PB386`, 2026-09-03, implementing `docs/rearchitecture/DESIGN-spec-conformance-review.md` **§1.1**.
> Some normative rules impose no obligation a conforming program can observe, and for those §1(c)'s covering
> test is not merely unwritten, it is **unmeetable**: any golden would pin an IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE as though it
> were the standard's answer, which is the exact thing the traceability inventory's `spec-derived` clause exists
> to prevent. A determination here stands in place of that test — **and in place of nothing else**. The verdict,
> its evidence, and (for an Annex A.1 row) the §7 determination and a greenfield implementing site are all still
> paid in full.
>
> ⚙ **The row key is `DRV-` plus the inventory `rule-id`, and the inventory row's `derivation` field carries
> `docs/CONFORMANCE.md#DRV-<rule-id>` COMPUTED from that same id** — never typed, for the reason §7's key is
> computed: a determination filed against a neighbouring rule cannot be *spelled*. Every cell below is machine
> read: `scripts/spec/audit_derivations.py` and `SpecTraceabilityInventoryDriftTests` both evaluate the arm, and
> `Schema.state_for` refuses to close a row whose determination does not stand.
>
> ⚙ **Three arms, and only one of them is an argument twice over.**
> · **`undefined-A.2`** is MECHANICAL. Annex A.2 is *"a list of the COBOL language elements within this Working
> Draft International Standard that are explicitly undefined"* (§A.2), and §4.4 2) makes the point that matters
> here: *"A COBOL run unit that allows these situations to happen is a conforming run unit, although the
> resultant execution is not defined by standard COBOL."* Each A.2 item ends in the standard's own citation of
> the rule it is about, so `scripts/spec/extract_annex_a2.py` resolves item → `rule-id` and the checker requires
> the named item to cover **this** row. Naming a real item about a different rule is refused.
> · **`unpopulatable-antecedent`** and **`indistinguishable-consequent`** are reviewed ARGUMENTS. Their shape is
> checked — the closed set must be STATED, the named rule must EXIST and not be this one — and the `Signed`
> cell is the record that the argument was read. An unchecked argument would make the GAP metric cheaper to move
> than the work it stands for, which is the failure this whole register is built against.
>
> ⚖ **This does not reverse `kb/Work/PB280` Q2.** An Annex A.1 row asks the implementor to STATE a choice, so
> "there is nothing to observe" is unfalsifiable there and still cannot close a row: the A.1 row below is
> admitted because its determination **is** stated, in §7, and it names implementing code — only its WITNESS is
> impossible. `Schema.state_for` encodes that by ORDER, charging a DOC row's anchor and observability costs
> before it ever consults this register.

| Rule | Arm | Names | The derivation | Signed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DRV-GR-14.9.5.4-11 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 4 | **§14.9.5.4 GR11** — *"If a program-pointer has been set to point to the program to be canceled, the result of referencing the program-pointer in a subsequent CALL statement is undefined."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 11). **Annex A.2 item 4 repeats it verbatim**, and §4.4 2) makes a run unit that reaches the situation a conforming one, so every behaviour conforms and there is no expected value to compute. COBOL.NET's behaviour is nevertheless determinate: `ProgramTable.CancelNode` assigns `Node.Instance = null` and nothing structural, leaving the entry in `_byPath` and `_order`, so `CallPointer` re-resolves the canonical name the pointer holds and re-enters `CallProgram`, which re-instantiates the program in its initial state. That is one admissible realization of "undefined" and is stricter than the rule requires — specifically, not a dangling reference. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.30.4-3 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 41 | **§14.9.30.4 GR3** — *"the content of any data item, where any part of that data item lies beyond the range of the current record, is undefined"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.30.4` → OK, rule 3). **Annex A.2 item 41** carries it, and unlike Annex A.1 the A.2 list creates no documentation obligation, so there is not even a documented choice to assert. COBOL.NET makes the content determinate: `FileConnector.Fit` pads or truncates the physical record to the record-area width with SPACES and `SequentialIoEmitter.EmitImageInto` stores that full-width image into the largest record description (§13.18.33.4 GR3), so every position past the current record reads as a space. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.34.4-2 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 46 | **§14.9.34.4 GR2** — *"the content of any data item, where any part of that data item lies outside the range of the current record, is undefined"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.34.4` → OK, rule 2). **Annex A.2 item 46** carries it. This is the RETURN twin of GR-14.9.30.4-3 and it is doubly unreachable: `SortBinder.BindReturn` resolves the record area through `file.AreaRecord` — the largest record's view — and `SortEmitter.EmitReturn` writes through `CobolString.Store`, which space-fills to the image width, so the bytes outside the returned record are deterministically SPACES. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.11.4-11 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the DISPLAY device set is closed at two members — System.Console and System.Console.Error, admitted by the output-capable device rows of ImplementorNames (read by AcceptDisplayBinder.MnemonicDevice) and emitted by a two-valued ternary in AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay | **§14.9.11.4 GR11** — *"If vertical positioning is not applicable on the device, the vertical positioning shall be ignored."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.11.4` → OK, rule 11). The antecedent is FALSE for every device this implementation offers. `BindDisplayUpon` admits only the output-capable device-names of `ImplementorNames` and diagnoses anything else (COBOLNET0817); the emitter's sink is a two-valued ternary over the §14.9.11.4 GR8 standard display device (*"If the UPON phrase is not specified, the implementor's standard display device is used."*) and the SYSERR routing recorded at §7 item 59. Both are line-structured streams on which vertical positioning IS applicable, so there is never any positioning to ignore. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT: it is voided the moment `ImplementorNames` grows an output-capable device row — a printer or reel mnemonic — and this row must then be re-adjudicated rather than inherit its closure. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.30.4-20 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file set is closed at one host path per connector — FileConnector.HostPath resolves exactly one, on a mass-storage medium with no reel or unit model, and FileRegistry.CloseReelUnit is the matching no-op | **§14.9.30.4 GR20** — *"shall be a single reel/unit mass storage file"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.30.4` → OK, rule 20). This is a requirement on the SOURCE PROGRAM, and in this implementation every physical file a program can name already satisfies it: a connector binds exactly one resolved host path, there is no reel/unit or volume model, and `CLOSE … REEL/UNIT` is a successful no-op reporting `07` (§9.1.13.2 item 6). No program can name a multi-reel physical file, so there is no violating state to detect and neither a positive nor a negative witness is constructible. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.30.4-23 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the same closed medium — one host path per connector, one StreamReader over it, so the end of the only unit IS the logical end of file and no distinct end-of-reel event exists to recognize | **§14.9.30.4 GR23** — *"a reel/unit swap occurs and the current volume pointer is updated"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.30.4` → OK, rule 23). Its antecedent needs an end of reel/unit recognized **while the logical end of the file has not been reached**. On a single-volume medium those are the same event: `SequentialConnector.Read` holds one stream over one host path, so nothing can recognize an end of reel/unit distinct from an end of file and no swap is ever owed. The medium disposition is owner-documented in §2 (A.3 items 26, 28–30, 33–34). | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.6.4-L2.1 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file category set is closed at the two `PhysicalFileCategory` values any connector can report — `SequentialConnector` answers (a) Non-unit and `RelativeConnector`/`IndexedConnector` answer (d), so no file is on unit media | **§14.9.6.4 GR3 symbol e, branch “Input files and input-output files (unit media)” item 1** — *“If the current unit is the last or only unit for the physical file, there is no unit swap, the current volume pointer remains unchanged, and the file position indicator is set to indicate that no next or previous logical record exists.”* (`cite.py --check 14.9.6.4` → OK, rule 3). Its antecedent needs a physical file **on unit media**, i.e. §14.9.6.4 GR2 category (b) or (c). No supported medium is in either: the placement is stated in §7's A.1 item 24 row, realized as `FileConnector.Category`, and FORCED by §9.1.13.2 item 6, which defines the `'07'` this compiler already reports for the REEL/UNIT and NO REWIND phrases as the status of a CLOSE that *“references a physical file on a non-reel/unit medium”* (`cite.py --check 9.1.13.2` → OK, rule 6). Every `CLOSE … UNIT` therefore takes symbol e's THIRD branch (non-unit media), which `GR-14.9.6.4-L2.2` covers, and no run unit can reach this one — there is neither a positive nor a negative witness to construct. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT on the placement: `CloseTable14Tests.Category_PlacesEverySupportedConnectorKind` and `ReachableCategories_NeedNoUnitStructuredSymbol` fail the moment a connector answers (b) or (c), and this row must then be re-adjudicated rather than inherit its closure. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-RV-15.50.4-9 | indistinguishable-consequent | RV-15.50.4-5 | **§15.50.4 rule 9** — *"the returned value is rounded to the next larger integer value"* (`cite.py --check 15.50.4` → OK, rule 9) — cannot be told apart from **§15.50.4 rule 5**, *"The returned length shall include the number of implicit FILLER positions, if any, in argument-1."* (`cite.py --check 15.50.4` → OK, rule 5). §8.5.1.6.3 generates implicit filler bit positions to complete the last byte of a bit-bearing item (*"Implicit filler bit positions are generated"*, `cite.py --check 8.5.1.6.3` → OK), and rule 5 requires those positions to be COUNTED — which already yields the byte-rounded integer rule 9 would round up to. Confirmed in code: `BitLayout.ExtentBits` returns `RoundUpToByte` for every group without GROUP-USAGE BIT, so removing rule 9's ceiling would change none of the returned values. No conforming program can therefore distinguish an implementation that applies rule 9 from one that does not, and a golden written for rule 9 would be measuring rule 5. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-DOC-A.1-19 | indistinguishable-consequent | GR-14.9.5.4-7 | **A.1 item 19** (result of canceling a non-COBOL program) documents **§14.9.5.4 GR10**, *"If the program to be canceled is other than a COBOL program, the effects of the CANCEL statement are implementor-defined."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 10). GR10 governs a program the runtime system LOCATED per **GR2**, *"The program-name is used by the runtime system to locate the program"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 2) — and `ProgramTable.Cancel` resolves every target with `probe: false` and returns on a null node, so no locate step runs and the named module's language is never consulted. The effect is therefore byte-identical to **§14.9.5.4 GR7**, *"No action is taken when a CANCEL statement is executed referencing a program that has not been called in this run unit"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 7), which the corpus already pins. ⛔ The witness was WRITTEN, in golden-lane round 2, and deleted: its output was invariant under the determination it claimed to measure. The determination itself is stated in §7 and the row names implementing code, so only the witness is impossible — which is why this row is admissible where `kb/Work/PB280` Q2 refused an anchor-only one. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.27.4-12 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file category set is closed at the two `PhysicalFileCategory` values any connector can report — `SequentialConnector` answers (a) Non-unit and `RelativeConnector`/`IndexedConnector` answer (d), so no medium permits rewinding | **§14.9.27.4 GR12** — *"If the storage medium for the file permits rewinding, the following rules apply: a) When neither the EXTEND, nor the NO REWIND phrase is specified, execution of the OPEN statement causes the file to be positioned at its beginning. b) When the NO REWIND phrase is specified, execution of the OPEN statement does not cause the file to be repositioned …"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.27.4` → OK, rule 12). Its antecedent is FALSE for every supported medium, and that is forced rather than chosen: GR12 and **GR11** partition the media between them — GR11 governs a medium the NO REWIND phrase *"does not apply to"*, which §9.1.13.2 item 6 fixes as the non-reel/unit medium (`cite.py --check 9.1.13.2` → OK, item 6) — so the category (a) placement that makes this compiler's `'07'` correct for `CLOSE REEL/UNIT`, `CLOSE WITH NO REWIND` and `OPEN WITH NO REWIND` selects GR11 and empties GR12. Arm (a)'s observable — an OPEN INPUT positioned at the beginning — is **§14.9.27.4 GR14**'s (*"the file position indicator for that file connector is set to 1"*), which is unconditional, so a golden written for GR12 a) would be measuring GR14. Arm (b) is not merely unimplemented: `FileRegistry.NoRewindPhraseEffect` refuses any category but (a) LOUDLY, so a future rewinding medium cannot inherit this closure silently. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT on the placement: `CloseTable14Tests.OpenNoRewind_IsTheCloseArmsTwinOnTheSameMedium` fails the moment a connector answers (b) or (c), and this row must then be re-adjudicated. (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q25, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.20.4-9 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 20 | **§14.9.20.4 GR9** — *"If identifier-1 occupies the same storage area as identifier-2, the result of the execution of this statement is undefined, even if they are defined by the same data description entry."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.20.4` → OK, rule 9). **Annex A.2 item 20 carries it** ("INITIALIZE statement. The result of the execution of the INITIALIZE statement is undefined if the receiving operand and the sending operand occupies the same storage area even if they are defined by the same data description entry. (14.9.20, INITIALIZE statement, General rule 9)"). The rule WIDENS undefinedness rather than requiring anything: it overrides §14.6.10 rule 2 ("When the data items are described by the same data description entry, the result of the statement is the same as if the data items shared no part of their respective storage areas." — `cite.py --check 14.6.10` → OK, rule 2) so that the overlap is undefined *even then*. Every behaviour conforms, so there is no expected value to compute and any golden would pin an implementation choice. COBOL.NET's behaviour is nevertheless determinate and inside the envelope: `INITIALIZE G REPLACING ALPHANUMERIC DATA BY A1`, where `A1` is subordinate to `G`, compiles and runs, and §14.9.20.4 GR4's implicit MOVEs are executed per elementary item in the GR8 definition order, so a sender that is itself overwritten mid-statement is read as it then stands (measured: `A1`/`B1` both read `AAA`). (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q26, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.28.4-2 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 37 | **§14.9.28.4 GR2** — *"The results of executing the following sequence of PERFORM statements are undefined and no exception condition is set to exist when the sequence is executed: a) a PERFORM statement is executed and has not yet terminated, then b) within the range of that PERFORM statement another PERFORM statement is executed, then c) the execution of the second PERFORM statement passes through the exit of the first PERFORM statement."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.28.4` → OK, rule 2). **Annex A.2 item 37 carries it** ("PERFORM statement. The results of the execution of overlapping PERFORM statements are undefined. (14.9.28, PERFORM statement, General rule 2)"). ⛔ The rule's second clause — *no* exception condition is set — cannot be witnessed either, and that is not for want of trying: the only place a witness could observe it is after the sequence has run, and where control goes after (c) is precisely what the first clause leaves undefined, so no expected output line is derivable. NOTE 1 makes the point the standard's own way ("On some implementations it causes stack overflows, on some it causes returns to unlikely places"). COBOL.NET's behaviour is determinate: `DispatchEmitter.EmitDispatchMethod` models no overlap and Table 13 defines no exception-name for it, so nothing can be raised; measured under `>>TURN EC-ALL CHECKING ON`, a `PERFORM PA THRU PB` whose range performs `PC`, which `GO TO PB`, runs PA/PC/PB, falls past PB and exits 0 with no EC and no diagnostic — one admissible realization, and the no-exception half is honoured. (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q27, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-RV-15.4.1-4 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the set item 4 governs is CLOSED by the standard itself and is EMPTY: the functions that have an equivalent arithmetic expression are exactly §15.7 ABS, §15.9 ANNUITY, §15.17 COMBINED-DATETIME, §15.23 DATE-TO-YYYYMMDD, §15.25 DAY-TO-YYYYDDD, §15.36 FACTORIAL, §15.60 MEAN, §15.74 PRESENT-VALUE, §15.79 SECONDS-FROM-FORMATTED-TIME, §15.81 SIGN, §15.86 STANDARD-DEVIATION, §15.88 SUM, §15.98 VARIANCE and §15.100 YEAR-TO-YYYY — the fourteen clauses containing “The equivalent arithmetic expression is as follows” — and NOT ONE of those fourteen function clauses contains the words “implementor-defined”, “implementor defines” or “defined by the implementor” anywhere in it | **§15.4.1 NOTE 1 item 4** — *“The result is explicitly implementor-defined”* (`cite.py --check 15.4.1 "The result is explicitly implementor-defined"` → OK, rule 4), the fourth disjunct of *“The result of an equivalent arithmetic expression is implementor-defined if any one or more of the following applies”*, whose normative twin under a standard mode is the same clause’s rule 2 c), *“The returned value is explicitly implementor-defined”* (`--check` → OK, rule 2). **Its antecedent is false for every equivalent arithmetic expression THE STANDARD DEFINES**, so it is unpopulatable before it is ever an implementation question. NOTE 1’s own lead-in scopes item 4 to *the result of an equivalent arithmetic expression*, so it reaches only functions that HAVE one; that set is closed and enumerable, and the enumeration in the Names cell was taken mechanically over §15 rather than recalled. Each of the fourteen was then searched for an explicit implementor-defined result and none carries one. The three functions this row’s own history named — §15.75 RANDOM, §15.80 SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT and §15.38 FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE — are not members of the set at all: they have no equivalent arithmetic expression, so their implementor-defined results are §4.2.5 Annex A.1 determinations (§7 items 144/145, 171, 112, 87) and instances of §15.4.1’s LAST paragraph (*“When a numeric or integer function does not have an equivalent arithmetic expression, its returned value is implementor-defined”*), never of item 4. §15.27 E and §15.73 PI do make a native returned value an implementor-defined approximation of a stated expression, but that is NOTE 1 item 1 (*“Native arithmetic is in effect”*) and those clauses print no equivalent arithmetic expression either. ⚠ The vacuity is the STANDARD’s, not this implementation’s, so unlike the device- and medium-set rows above it cannot be voided by a later implementation choice — only by a future edition that makes some EAE-bearing function’s returned value explicitly implementor-defined, at which point the rule-digest changes and the row is re-adjudicated by construction. The row’s companion `RV-15.4.1-1` (*“the returned value shall equal the value of the equivalent arithmetic expression”*) carries the observable half and keeps `conformance:2023/exp_standard_decimal_eae`; that test-ref was on THIS row and is cleared here, because a test about rule 1 is not evidence about rule 4. (kb/Work PB245.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
